# macOS gap audit: evidence digest (2026-09-29)

Evidence digest of the multi-agent audit of `kciceblue/fontplayground@14b6572` (PySide6 + fontTools, Windows-first) run on macOS 27.0 / Apple Silicon / Xcode 27.
Every dimension had an auditor and an adversarial verifier that re-ran the evidence. A completeness critic ran last.
**Specs cite these IDs** (e.g. `ENGINE-1`). The *verified* severity is the verifier's corrected severity. Where the verifier changed a recommendation, the **Verifier** line is the one to follow.

Machine-specific numbers (counts of fonts, timings) come from one Mac and are indicative, not normative.

Legend: severity blocker > major > minor > polish. Verdict: confirmed / partially / refuted / unverifiable (verifier). `M` IDs were found by the verifier, `CRIT` IDs by the critic.

## Font discovery, catalog & Qt font loading (`CATALOG-*`)

On macOS the scanner is fast (379 files, 795 faces, 2.7 s cold, 0.19 s warm) but gets the wrong set of fonts. It misses everything CoreText keeps outside the three standard folders. That covers all 29 fonts in /System/Library/AssetsV2, including PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO, Kaiti, Yuanti, SimSong, Lantinghei, YuGothic/YuMincho and Nanum Gothic. It also misses PingFangUI in PrivateFrameworks. At the same time it takes in 112 hidden system faces (74 '.'-families). One of them, .LastResort, covers all 1,114,112 code points with 7 box glyphs, so it becomes the #1 suggestion: the Chinese picker opens with 'Use .LastResort' preselected and lists only 10 Chinese families. A name-ranking rule written for EPSON fonts on Windows gives 18 Apple Arabic/Indic families native family and style names. Because of that, their Bold/Black faces preview as Regular. The Qt side is mostly sound: QFont::NoFontMerging does stop CoreText fallback on macOS, and TTC faces with index > 0 (PingFang TC, Hiragino W6, Songti) resolve to the exact face. The preview can still be wrong, though, because fonts are resolved by family name: an installed copy wins over the file being previewed. A prototype that combined CoreText enumeration (CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs) with a '.'-prefix filter raised the Chinese list from 10 to 37 families, with PingFang and no hidden faces. A one-rule fix to face.py's name ranking removed all family-name mismatches with CoreText (0 of 829 faces) and the full test suite still passed (339).

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| CATALOG-1 | blocker | confirmed | Directory walk misses CoreText-managed fonts (AssetsV2 downloads incl. PingFang, PrivateFrameworks) — the main Chinese UI font is absent |
| CATALOG-2 | blocker | confirmed | Hidden macOS system faces leak into the catalog; .LastResort becomes the top suggestion and is preselected |
| CATALOG-3 | major | confirmed | Name ranking prefers non-English Windows records over Mac Roman English: Apple Arabic/Indic fonts get native family/style names and preview in the wrong weight |
| CATALOG-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | The proposed CoreText enumerator pulls in PingFangUI.ttc, whose plain-named faces duplicate PingFang.ttc; previews and forges then read different files |
| CATALOG-4 | minor | partially | Preview resolves fonts by family name: an installed font with the same family/style silently draws instead of the file being previewed |
| CATALOG-5 | minor | partially | macOS downloadable fonts (Font Book 'Download') are invisible; synchronous CoreText matching of them blocks |
| CATALOG-6 | minor | confirmed | Catalog does not refresh when the font set changes (Font Book install/download, the app's own Install, Adobe/app activations) |
| CATALOG-7 | minor | confirmed | Recipes and cache are keyed by absolute path; macOS asset paths contain a content hash that changes on asset updates |
| CATALOG-M2 | minor | (verifier-found) | Native names stored only in Mac Chinese/Korean-encoded name records are ignored (STSong 华文宋体, STHeiti 华文黑体, STKaiti 华文楷体, STFangsong 华文仿宋, LiSong Pro 儷宋 Pro) |
| CATALOG-8 | polish | confirmed | Apple bitmap font NISC18030.ttf ('bhed' instead of 'head') fails to parse, giving a permanent '1 file couldn't be read' on every Mac |

| CATALOG-10 | polish | confirmed | .dfont suitcases and extensionless PostScript fonts are skipped |
| CATALOG-11 | polish | confirmed | Suggestions ignore the platform's own default font per language (native feel) |
| CATALOG-12 | polish | confirmed | Variable fonts show as one face while macOS shows their named instances |

### CATALOG-1: Directory walk misses CoreText-managed fonts (AssetsV2 downloads incl. PingFang, PrivateFrameworks) — the main Chinese UI font is absent

- **Severity:** blocker (auditor: blocker); effort M; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:22-23`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19-28`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:411`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:142-170`
- **macOS behaviour:** default_font_dirs('darwin') = /System/Library/Fonts, /Library/Fonts, ~/Library/Fonts. CoreText registers 411 font files; the scanner sees 379, and the 33 it misses are 29 in /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/<hash>.asset/AssetData/, 1 in PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework/Resources/Reserved (PingFangUI.ttc), NISC18030 (parse failure) and 2 extensionless Type1 files. Missing families: PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO, Kaiti SC/TC, STKaiti, Yuanti SC/TC, SimSong, Lantinghei SC/TC, Libian, Xingkai, Hannotate, HanziPen, Baoli, Wawati, Weibei, Yuppy, LingWai, BiauKai, LiSong Pro, STFangsong, STHeiti, YuGothic, YuMincho, YuKyokasho, Klee, Toppan Bunkyu Gothic, Nanum Gothic. The Chinese (Simplified) picker lists only 10 families, 2 of them hidden. A knock-on effect: BuildController.conflict() only blocks family names it finds in the catalog. It would therefore not stop the user naming a forged font 'PingFang SC' (and windows_fonts_dir never matches on macOS).
- **Evidence:** work/catalog/scan_run.py: 'find_font_files: 379 files … COLD: faces=795 failed=1 time=2.73s … WARM … 0.19s'. ct_enum.py: 'CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs: 2988 urls, 411 unique paths (0.04s)'; by dir: 289 Supplemental, 82 /System/Library/Fonts, 29 AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8, 10 ~/Library/Fonts, 1 PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework/Resources/Reserved. Diff: 'CT-only files: 33 … scan-only files: 0'. picker_shot.py row_texts: ['SUGGESTED FOR YOUR TEXT', '.LastResort', 'Hiragino Sans GB', '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface', 'ALL CHINESE FONTS · 10', …]. Prototype (repo copy, scanner unions CT URLs): 'rows: … ALL CHINESE FONTS · 37 … PingFang HK, PingFang MO, PingFang SC, PingFang TC …'. CT+dirs scan: 'files=409 faces=920 failed=1 cold=6.47s warm=0.50s cache=12.7MB'. The asset folder name is versioned ('com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8' is the only one on this Mac), so hard-coding it is brittle. Qt (qcoretextfontdatabase.mm) itself lists fonts with CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qt/qtbase/dev/src/gui/text/coretext/qcoretextfontdatabase.mm
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, add a CoreText enumerator: files = {Path(u.path()) for u in CoreText.CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs() if u.isFileURL()}. Union it with default_font_dirs() and extra_dirs in find_font_files (it takes 0.02 s), then keep the is_font_file filter. Add the dependency as `pyobjc-framework-CoreText; sys_platform == 'darwin'` (CoreText 248 KB, objc 10 MB). If the import fails, fall back to the directory walk plus a glob of /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font*/*/AssetData. Put the enumerator behind a function in paths.py (e.g. system_font_files()) so tests can fake it. Also make build.conflict() treat the macOS system folders (/System/Library/Fonts, /System/Library/AssetsV2, /Library/Fonts) the way it treats %WINDIR%\Fonts.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced with work/verify-catalog/scan1.py and ct1.py. Scanner: 379 files (213 ttf / 128 ttc / 38 otf), 795 faces, 1 failure (NISC18030 KeyError 'head'), 2.29 s cold, 0.16 s warm, 5.4 MB cache. CoreText: 2988 URLs, 411 unique files in 0.015 s. 32 CT-only files (29 AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8, PingFangUI.ttc in PrivateFrameworks, HelveLTMM, TimesLTMM), plus NISC18030 (scanned but failed), gives the auditor's 33. Scan-only files: 0. 65 CT families are missing from the scanner's family set, including PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO, Kaiti SC/TC, Yuanti, SimSong, Lantinghei, YuGothic, YuMincho and Nanum Gothic. The other 18 are Arabic/Indic names that belong to CATALOG-3. Chinese (Simplified) covers_well list is 10 families including '.LastResort' and '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface'. build.py:160-166 only hard-blocks faces under windows_fonts_dir, as claimed. Qt side: once the AssetsV2 file is in the catalog, all 24 PingFang.ttc faces resolve by name to the exact face (identity checked on the head table). Severity is borderline blocker/major, since 'Add folder' on /System/Library/AssetsV2 is a hidden workaround. Blocker stands because Latin + PingFang (the Mac equivalent of Segoe UI + Microsoft YaHei) can't be done out of the box. The recommendation has a flaw, see CATALOG-M1: a blind union also pulls in PingFangUI.ttc, whose 4 plain-named faces duplicate PingFang.ttc's family/style/ …
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Add a darwin-only system_font_files() in paths.py built on CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs(). Use pyobjc-framework-CoreText with a sys_platform marker, or ~25 lines of ctypes over CoreText/CoreFoundation (CFArrayGetCount / CFArrayGetValueAtIndex / CFURLGetFileSystemRepresentation) to avoid the 10 MB pyobjc dependency. Union it with the directory walk and extra_dirs, then filter with is_font_file. Then dedupe faces by PostScript name (name ID 6), keeping the file CoreText resolves for that name (CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize(ps) -> kCTFontURLAttribute). In practice that means dropping URLs under /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks, which would otherwise duplicate PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO Medium. Record each face's source (system / asset / user / third-party activation) so the UI can flag fonts that font managers such as Adobe Fonts activate from hidden folders; their licences usually forbid modification. The fallback when CoreText is unavailable is a glob of /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font*/*.asset/AssetData. conflict() should treat /System/Library/Fonts, /System/Library/AssetsV2 and /Library/Fonts the way it treats %WINDIR%\Fonts.

### CATALOG-2: Hidden macOS system faces leak into the catalog; .LastResort becomes the top suggestion and is preselected

- **Severity:** blocker (auditor: blocker); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:49-52`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:157-193`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:573-590`, `fontplayground/ui/languages.py:111-115`
- **macOS behaviour:** Of 795 scanned faces, 112 (74 families) are hidden system faces whose names start with '.': .SF*, .New York, .LastResort, .Aqua Kana, .Hiragino Kaku Gothic Interface, .Hiragino Sans GB Interface, .Apple SD Gothic NeoI, *PUA, .CJK Symbols Fallback*, .Keyboard. 102 of them count as supported. .LastResort (CFF, 7 glyphs) maps all 1,114,112 code points, so covers_well() is True for every language. suggest_materials() ranks by missing characters covered, so .LastResort is always #1. The picker opens with 'Use .LastResort' preselected and draws boxes. Forging with it would map missing characters to placeholder boxes. Every CJK list also carries hidden duplicates (e.g. '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface'). CoreText will not resolve '.' names at all: 'Client requested name ".SanaPUA", it will get TimesNewRomanPSMT'. SFNS.ttf has the ordinary family 'System Font' but the PS name '.SFNS-Regular'.
- **Evidence:** Hidden-face count from the cached Mac catalog: 'hidden faces: 112 hidden families: 74 supported hidden: 102'; 'LastResort: .LastResort CFF 1114112 True installable {latin: True, chinese_s: True, … symbols: True}'; 'LastResort glyphs: 7'. smart.suggest_materials with Main=Helvetica on DEFAULT_SAMPLE: "suggest (all): [('.LastResort','Regular'), ('Hiragino Sans GB','W3'), ('.Hiragino Sans GB Interface','W3')]". Screenshot work/catalog/picker_chinese_s.png shows the preselected '.LastResort' row drawn as boxes and the 'Use .LastResort' button. PS-name check: "faces with '.'PS name but non-dot family: [('System Font','.SFNS-Regular'), ('System Font','.SFNS-RegularItalic')]".
- **Recommendation:** Filter by face, not by file: several TTCs mix visible and hidden faces (Hiragino, AppleSDGothicNeo, AlBayan). Drop a face when its family or its PostScript name (name ID 6) starts with '.'. That rule also catches .LastResort and System Font/.SFNS. Keep dropped faces in the cache so the file isn't re-read, but not in ScanResult.faces. Leave the 127 families CoreText hides from menus but that have ordinary names (Times, Courier, Hiragino Kaku Gothic Pro/ProN, Noto Sans <rare scripts>, STIX*, Seravek, Athelas, Rockwell). Tag them visible=False using CTFontManagerCopyAvailablePostScriptNames() and optionally put them behind a 'Show hidden system fonts' toggle. Also add a guard in smart.suggest_materials against faces with glyph_count far below their cmap size (7 glyphs for 1.1M code points).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced (work/verify-catalog/hidden.py): 112 hidden faces, 74 '.'-families, 102 of them supported. .LastResort is CFF with 7 glyphs and 1,114,112 code points, and covers_well is True for all 14 languages. suggest_materials(Helvetica, DEFAULT_SAMPLE) returns ['.LastResort', 'Hiragino Sans GB', '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface']. picker._pick_row (picker.py:617-636) falls back to selectable[0], the first suggestion, so .LastResort is preselected. The auditor understated the damage (lr_rules.py). Once .LastResort is a material, smart_supplier (smart.py:61-78) gives it hebrew/arabic/indic/SE-asian/hangul/kana/han/cjk_symbols/symbols/emoji/other even when Hiragino Sans GB sits ahead of it: order [Helvetica, Hiragino Sans GB, .LastResort] -> han/kana/cjk_symbols = '.LastResort'. .LastResort maps every code point in each group, so the real CJK font's count × 2 is below it. The forged font would draw boxes for all CJK, not just the missing characters. One correction: the claim that CoreText won't resolve '.' names doesn't hold for Qt. system_font('.Al Nile PUA') returned a font and QTextLayout drew with '.Al Nile PUA', because Qt resolves private families through its own descriptor database. The 'System Font'/'.SFNS-Regular' PostScript-name case is confirmed.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Filter faces in the scanner (or in picker.add_face / model.set_catalog) with Qt's own predicate, QFontDatabase.isPrivateFamily(face.family). It matched the '.'-prefix rule exactly (74 of 74 scanner families, none extra), it also treats a bare 'LastResort' as private (qcoretextfontdatabase.mm isPrivateFontFamily), and it returns False on Windows. Add a PostScript-name '.'-prefix rule to catch SFNS 'System Font'. Keep the proposed glyph_count-vs-cmap guard (only .LastResort trips it on this Mac: ratio 159,159, next face below 2), and apply it in smart_supplier/group_counts as well as suggest_materials, so a bogus face can never take a group.

### CATALOG-3: Name ranking prefers non-English Windows records over Mac Roman English: Apple Arabic/Indic fonts get native family/style names and preview in the wrong weight

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:100-128`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:182`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:187`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:55-65`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:97-108`
- **macOS behaviour:** Apple fonts often have the English name only in a Mac Roman record (1,0,0) and localized names in Windows records such as 0x401/0x439, with no Windows English record. _name_rank puts any Windows language (rank 1) ahead of the Mac Roman record, which is not ranked. Result: 33 visible faces in 18 families disagree with CoreText. Examples: Al Bayan → 'البيان', Geeza Pro → 'گیزا پرو', Damascus → 'دمشق', Gujarati MT → 'गुजराती एचटी', InaiMathi → 'इनाईमाथी'. Style names come out native too ('عادي', 'عريض', 'أسود'). Consequences: searching for 'Al Bayan' finds nothing; system_font() never matches, so the file gets loaded; and make_font() asks Qt for style 'عريض', which Qt does not know. It then falls back to QFont(family), so the Bold and Black faces preview as Regular while the forge uses the real Bold/Black face.
- **Evidence:** name_diff.py (CoreText descriptor family vs scanner, per face): 'faces compared: 829 family mismatches vs CoreText: 33', e.g. "AlBayan.ttc[0]: scanner='البيان' CT='Al Bayan'", "Muna.ttc[4]: scanner='منى' CT='Muna' (style 'أسود' / 'Black')". Name table of AlBayan.ttc: 'id1 plat1 enc0 lang0x0 Al Bayan' and 'id1 plat3 enc1 lang0xc01 البيان', with no 0x409 family record. Qt check: "Al Nile.ttc [2] family='النيل' style='عريض' weight_class=700 -> Qt draws 'Al Nile' / 'Regular'", "Muna.ttc [4] … style='أسود' weight_class=900 -> Qt draws 'Muna' / 'Regular'". Patch tried in a private repo copy: rank Windows-English 0, then Mac Roman English (1,0,0) only when its raw bytes are printable ASCII (1), then other Windows languages (2), then Mac Japanese (3). Results: 'family mismatches vs CoreText: 0'; Al Nile → family 'Al Nile', style 'Bold', local_names ('النيل',); tests/test_face.py + test_catalog.py 23 passed; full suite '339 passed, 3 skipped, 2 deselected'. The EPSON Shift-JIS-in-Mac-Roman case still works because mojibake bytes are not ASCII.
- **Recommendation:** In _name_rank, rank a Mac Roman English record (platformID 1, encID 0, langID 0) whose raw bytes are all printable ASCII above non-English Windows records, and below Windows English only. Mojibake can't be plain ASCII, so the EPSON protection stays. Add a test fixture with records [(1,0,0,'Al Bayan'), (3,1,0x0C01,'البيان')] and assert family 'Al Bayan' and local_names ('البيان',). Bump cache SCHEMA to 4 so old names are re-read.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced (work/verify-catalog/names.py, qt1.py). AlBayan.ttc[0] has family 'البيان' but style 'Plain', because a 0x409 style record exists and no 0x409 family record does. The only English family record is (1,0,0) 'Al Bayan'. Al Nile [2] gets family 'النيل', style 'عريض', weight_class 700. make_font falls back to QFont('Al Nile') and QFontInfo reports Regular/400, so the Bold preview is Regular. system_font is False for the Arabic-named faces (Qt families contain 'Al Nile', not 'النيل'). I applied the proposed ranking independently in a private copy (Windows-English 0, printable-ASCII Mac Roman English 1, other Windows 2, Mac Japanese 3). Results: Al Nile/Muna/Al Bayan -> English family and style, Arabic local_names; tests/test_face.py and test_catalog.py pass (23 passed), including the EPSON SJIS-as-Mac-Roman fixtures at tests/test_face.py:8,60.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed. Bump cache SCHEMA to 4, and land it together with CATALOG-M2 (the Mac Chinese/Korean local names), since both change face.py's name reading.

### CATALOG-M1: The proposed CoreText enumerator pulls in PingFangUI.ttc, whose plain-named faces duplicate PingFang.ttc; previews and forges then read different files

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19-28`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:97-113`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:41-53`
- **macOS behaviour:** CATALOG-1's fix (union every CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs file) adds /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework/Resources/Reserved/PingFangUI.ttc. Of its 32 faces, 28 have '.'-families, but indexes 20-23 are named 'PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO', style 'Medium', with PostScript names PingFangSC-Medium etc. Those are identical to PingFang.ttc [4-7] in AssetsV2, so the '.'-filter keeps them. The picker's PingFang families get two 'Medium' faces from different files. Qt/CoreText resolve the name to PingFang.ttc, so the preview of the PingFangUI face draws the AssetsV2 file, while the forge would read glyphs from the private UI file. At CoreText level, all 24 visible PingFang PostScript names occur in both files.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-catalog/qt_ttc.py: 'PingFangUI.ttc faces 32 visible ok 0 wrong 4 via system_font 4 [(20,'PingFang SC','Medium'), (21,'PingFang TC','Medium'), (22,'PingFang HK','Medium'), (23,'PingFang MO','Medium')]' (the head table of the font Qt drew doesn't match the face's own). fontTools: '20 PingFang SC | Medium | PingFangSC-Medium', while PingFang.ttc has (7,'PingFangSC-Medium'). CoreText: CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize('PingFangSC-Medium') -> kCTFontURLAttribute = …/AssetsV2/…/PingFang.ttc. CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL over all URLs: 'visible PS names in >1 file: 24', all PingFang* in PingFang.ttc and PingFangUI.ttc. By contrast, PingFang.ttc alone: 24/24 faces resolve to the exact face.
- **Recommendation:** When adding the CoreText enumerator, dedupe faces by PostScript name and keep the file CoreText itself resolves for that name (CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize(ps, 0) -> CTFontCopyAttribute(kCTFontURLAttribute)). Or, simpler, drop URLs under /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks, since only PingFangUI.ttc comes from there on this Mac. Add a scanner test with two fake files sharing a PostScript name.

### CATALOG-4: Preview resolves fonts by family name: an installed font with the same family/style silently draws instead of the file being previewed

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:24-32`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:55-65`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:97-108`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:136-141`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:191-206`
- **macOS behaviour:** FontLoader loads the file with addApplicationFont, which on macOS keeps descriptors private to Qt. make_font() then asks for QFontDatabase.font(family, style). When the same family and style is installed, Qt/CoreText picks the installed copy. system_font() checks only QFontInfo.family() against face.family. The preview then claims to show the file but draws glyphs the file lacks and the build won't contain. Realistic cases on a Mac: (a) an extra folder or a ~/Library/Fonts copy with a different version of an installed family; (b) the built preview (preview.py:138 make_font(built_path)) after the user already installed an older build under the same name. The new build then previews with the old installed glyphs. QFont::NoFontMerging does work on macOS, so glyph-level fallback is not the problem.
- **Evidence:** qt_honesty.py: SongtiSC_subset.ttf is Songti SC Regular cut down to 'ABCabc' with its names kept. 'subset face: Songti SC Regular has 永: False'; 'system_font(subset face) -> OK (installed family) runs=[('Songti SC','Regular',[1878, 36])]'; 'make_font(subset file) -> … runs= [('Songti SC','Regular',[1878, 36])]'. Glyph 1878 is 永 from the installed Songti.ttc, which the file does not contain. Control test of the fallback mechanism: 'Helvetica default strategy runs: [(PingFang SC,[4062]), (Helvetica,[36]), (Apple SD Gothic Neo,[3296])]' vs 'Helvetica NoFontMerging runs: [(Helvetica,[36,0,0])]'. qt_identity.py fix check: 'subset file: Qt-resolved font is that file? False (1.6 ms)', 'installed Songti SC Regular [idx 6]: … True', and a renamed in-memory copy via addApplicationFontFromData: "['FPG_c773d231 Songti SC'] [('FPG_c773d231 Songti SC', [0, 1])]", where the file's own glyphs are drawn and 永 is correctly missing. Qt addApplicationFont keeps descriptors private (qcoretextfontdatabase.mm, fetched from https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qt/qtbase/dev/src/gui/text/coretext/qcoretextfontdatabase.mm).
- **Recommendation:** Check identity, not name. In system_font() and make_font(), compare bytes(QRawFont.fromFont(font).fontTable('head')) with the face's raw head table (TTFont(...).reader['head'], or TTCollection(...).fonts[index].reader['head']), and cache the result per face.key. It costs about 1.6 ms. When the resolved font is a different file, load that face under a unique family instead. The steps: extract the face (TTCollection.fonts[i]), rewrite name IDs 1/4/6/16 with a prefix such as 'FPG_<hash8> ', save to BytesIO, call QFontDatabase.addApplicationFontFromData(), then use the returned family. Always do this for the built font, since its name is expected to collide with installed builds.
- **Verifier (partially):** The auditor's evidence is invalid. qt_honesty.py/qt_identity.py call read_faces("SongtiSC_subset.ttf") with a relative path. I confirmed that QFontDatabase.addApplicationFont returns -1 for a relative path (qt_sig2.py: 'add -> -1'), so FontLoader.family_for returned None and make_font just used the installed family name. Their test never loaded the file. With an absolute path (qt_ident3.py, the same SongtiSC_subset.ttf, load id 0), make_font(subset) draws the subset: runs [('Songti SC','Regular',[0, 1])], with 永 missing as it should be. So the claim that the installed copy wins over the file being previewed is refuted. The real defect runs the other way: the most recently registered copy wins. After the subset is loaded, system_font(installed Songti SC Regular), the picker/face_font path, draws the subset's glyphs ([0,1] instead of [1878,36]). make_font(installed Songti.ttc) then flips it back. Same result with my own STSong subset (qt_ident2.py). Out of the box there are 0 family+style pairs in more than one file on this Mac (faces.json), so this needs user-created duplicates, such as an extra folder with another version or an installed older build. The built-font preview (preview.py:138) is effectively correct, since the newly loaded build wins. Severity drops to minor. The head-table identity check is sound. It returned True for the correctly loaded file, and none of the 144 …
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** In system_font(), don't trust a by-name match when FontLoader has loaded a different file that registered the same family. Check that first (a dict family -> set(paths) in FontLoader, no I/O), and fall back to the head-table comparison only in that case. Cache the result per face.key, and invalidate the _SYSTEM cache whenever FontLoader loads or unloads a file. Renaming the built font via addApplicationFontFromData isn't needed for correctness, because the newly loaded build already wins. Keep that renaming only for user-supplied duplicates.

### CATALOG-5: macOS downloadable fonts (Font Book 'Download') are invisible; synchronous CoreText matching of them blocks

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort L; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19-28`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:1-11`
- **macOS behaviour:** The MobileAsset font catalog lists 1,289 macOS-deliverable faces in 607 families. 955 faces in 425 visible families are not on disk, and neither the scanner nor CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs/CTFontCollectionCreateFromAvailableFonts can see them. Examples: Hei, Kai, Apple LiGothic, Apple LiSung, LiHei Pro, Osaka, BIZ UDGothic/UDMincho, Tsukushi A/B Round Gothic, Toppan Bunkyu Mincho/Midashi, Hiragino Sans TC, GungSeo, PilGi, NanumMyeongjo, Nanum Brush/Pen Script, Canela, Graphik, Proxima Nova. The descriptors CoreText returns for downloaded fonts carry kCTFontDownloadable=1 and kCTFontDownloaded=1 (93 of 624). Calling CTFontDescriptorCreateMatchingFontDescriptors on a not-yet-downloaded family name ('Tsukushi A Round Gothic', 'Osaka') did not return within 90 s and 20 s. The same call on installed families returned in about 0.0 s.
- **Evidence:** dl_diff.py over /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8.xml: 'macOS faces in catalog: 1289 families: 607 … on disk: 334 not on disk: 955'; 'not-on-disk visible families (425)'. ct_enum.py: "downloadable attr: Counter({(False, False): 531, (True, True): 93})". dl_match.py: 'Times matches: 4 0.0s', 'PingFang SC matches: 6 0.0s', 'Songti SC matches: 4 0.0s'; 'Tsukushi A Round Gothic' was killed after ~90 s and 'Osaka: still blocked after ~20s, killed'. pyobjc exposes CTFontDescriptorMatchFontDescriptorsWithProgressHandler and kCTFontDescriptorMatchingDidFinish (both True). Apple sample: https://developer.apple.com/library/content/samplecode/DownloadFont/Listings/DownloadFont_ViewController_m.html
- **Recommendation:** Add an optional 'Available to download' section to the picker, clearly marked, that is never used for coverage until downloaded. For the list of names: the MobileAsset XML is undocumented, so treat it as best-effort — wrap it in try/except, use only entries whose PlatformDelivery contains 'macOS-download', and fall back to a list shipped with the app. On explicit user action, download on a worker thread via CTFontDescriptorMatchFontDescriptorsWithProgressHandler([desc(PostScriptName)], None, handler), showing progress (kCTFontDescriptorMatchingPercentage) and rescanning at kCTFontDescriptorMatchingDidFinish. Or simply offer 'Open Font Book' (open -b com.apple.FontBook). Never call CTFontDescriptorCreateMatchingFontDescriptors or CTFontCreateWithName for catalog-only names on the UI thread. Downloading installs system-wide assets, so ask the user first.
- **Verifier (partially):** Core claim confirmed from com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8.xml (ma2.py): 1289 macOS-deliverable faces in 607 families. With CTFontManagerCopyAvailablePostScriptNames as the availability test, 800 'macOS-download' faces in 376 visible families aren't available (auditor: 955/425, different method, same conclusion). They include Hei, Kai, Apple LiGothic, Osaka, BIZ UDGothic, Tsukushi A Round Gothic and Hiragino Sans TC. The catalog also has 100 'macOS-autoactivated' faces. That delivery class likely explains the auditor's >90 s block: name matching can trigger CoreText auto-activation, which is a network download. I deliberately didn't repeat the blocking call because it could install system assets, so that sub-claim is unverified by me. Severity drops to minor: this is a new macOS capability, not a regression from the Windows build (which had no in-app download either), and the only complete source is an undocumented private catalog file. pyobjc does expose CTFontDescriptorMatchFontDescriptorsWithProgressHandler (checked).
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** First step: a 'Get more fonts…' action that opens Font Book (open -b com.apple.FontBook), plus the automatic rescan from CATALOG-6. Newly downloaded fonts then show up with no private-file parsing. For in-app download, ship a small curated list of PostScript names (e.g. the CJK families users commonly want: Hei/Kai/LiHei Pro/Hiragino Sans TC/BIZ UD*/Tsukushi) rather than parsing the MobileAsset XML. Download with CTFontDescriptorMatchFontDescriptorsWithProgressHandler on a worker thread, only after explicit confirmation. Never run name-based CoreText matching (CTFontCreateWithName, CTFontDescriptorCreateMatchingFontDescriptors, or Qt QFontDatabase.font on a family Qt doesn't list) for catalog-only names.

### CATALOG-6: Catalog does not refresh when the font set changes (Font Book install/download, the app's own Install, Adobe/app activations)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:302-313`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:33-36`
- **macOS behaviour:** Qt's CoreText font database repopulates itself on NSFontSetChangedNotification in a Cocoa app. The app's own catalog is rebuilt only at launch or on manual Rescan. After the user downloads PingFang in Font Book, or installs a forged font into ~/Library/Fonts, the picker and suggestions stay out of date. The build-conflict check also works from the stale catalog.
- **Evidence:** Qt source: 'm_fontSetObserver = QMacNotificationObserver(nil, NSFontSetChangedNotification, [] { … QPlatformFontDatabase::repopulateFontDatabase(); })' (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qt/qtbase/dev/src/gui/text/coretext/qcoretextfontdatabase.mm). qt_refresh.py under offscreen QPA, with no AppKit so no notification: a font registered with kCTFontManagerScopeProcess showed up in CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames ('FPG Probe Songti SC') but not in QFontDatabase.families() ('Qt families new after registration: []'). pyobjc exposes kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification (True).
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, register for kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification with CFNotificationCenterAddObserver(CFNotificationCenterGetLocalCenter(), …). Use a 1–2 s debounce, then call start_scan(use_cache=True); the per-file (size, mtime) cache makes this cheap, about 0.5 s warm. Optionally store a fingerprint (sha1 of the sorted CT URL paths plus sizes/mtimes) in catalog.json to skip or shortcut a startup rescan when nothing changed.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Code confirms that start_scan runs only at startup, on Rescan and on Add folder (app.py:169,202,337,347). Qt observes the font set: `nm -u QtGui` lists _NSFontSetChangedNotification, and qcoretextfontdatabase.mm calls repopulateFontDatabase -> QFontDatabasePrivate::invalidate(), which emits qGuiApp->fontDatabaseChanged() (qfontdatabase.cpp). QGuiApplication.fontDatabaseChanged is exposed in PySide6 6.11.2. Caveat I verified: addApplicationFont also emits fontDatabaseChanged (qt_sig2.py: fired once per successful add), and the picker loads ~160 files on macOS (qt_sys.py: 214 faces / 162 files it can't draw by name). A naive connection would trigger a rescan storm. I couldn't observe the NSFontSetChanged path under offscreen QPA.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Simpler than a pyobjc CFNotificationCenter observer, and it works on Windows too: connect QGuiApplication.fontDatabaseChanged to a 1–2 s single-shot debounce timer. When it fires, compute a cheap fingerprint of the installed set (sorted CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs paths + st_size/st_mtime, 0.015 s for the URL list on darwin; the directory walk elsewhere) and call start_scan(use_cache=True) only if the fingerprint changed. The app's own addApplicationFont calls leave the fingerprint unchanged and cost nothing. Also make the manual 'Rescan fonts' use the cache (it passes use_cache=False today, app.py:202), because the (size, mtime) key already detects changed files.

### CATALOG-7: Recipes and cache are keyed by absolute path; macOS asset paths contain a content hash that changes on asset updates

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/model.py:133-160`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:686-693`, `fontplayground/catalog/cache.py:61-83`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:40-42`
- **macOS behaviour:** Once AssetsV2 fonts are in the catalog, PingFang's key is ('/System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/86ba2c91…asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc', 3). When macOS updates that asset it lands in a new <hash>.asset folder (the downloaded assets here have different dates, e.g. STHEITI's folder is dated Sep 27 and the others Sep 26). from_settings then silently drops the material from the saved recipe. The cache never prunes, so the old entry stays forever. Verified with a simulated move.
- **Evidence:** stale_test (a copy of Kaiti.ttc moved from old_hash.asset to new_hash.asset, then rescanned): "faces now: 7 cache entries: ['stale_test/fonts/old_hash.asset/Kaiti.ttc', 'stale_test/fonts/new_hash.asset/Kaiti.ttc']". model.py:686-693: 'materials missing from faces_by_key are dropped'. The ls -la of AssetsV2 shows <sha1>.asset folder names that differ from the catalog zip names.
- **Recommendation:** Add postscript_name to FontFace (name ID 6, bump SCHEMA) and save it with each material alongside path/index. On restore, when (path, index) is missing, look it up by PostScript name in a catalog index, using family+style as a secondary key. At the end of a full scan, drop cache entries for paths not seen, so catalog.json stops growing.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Code confirmed. Settings materials are stored as [path, index] (model.py:133-160). from_settings drops keys missing from faces_by_key (model.py:686-693). CatalogCache only put()s and never prunes (cache.py:82-83). The asset folders are content-hash named (e.g. 86ba2c91….asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc) under a versioned asset type (only com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8 exists here). I didn't see an actual asset update or OS upgrade change the paths, so that part is inferred from the naming scheme. The auditor's stale_test simulation supports the cache-growth part. A related migration issue: the user's Windows forge_last.json refers to C:\Windows\Fonts\… paths, so every material from it is silently dropped on the Mac.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed: store postscript_name (name ID 6) per material and fall back to lookup by PostScript name, then family+style, when (path, index) is missing. It also makes Windows-authored recipes portable for fonts that exist on both platforms (Arial, Times New Roman, Arial Unicode MS, …). Prune cache entries for paths not seen at the end of a full scan.

### CATALOG-M2: Native names stored only in Mac Chinese/Korean-encoded name records are ignored (STSong 华文宋体, STHeiti 华文黑体, STKaiti 华文楷体, STFangsong 华文仿宋, LiSong Pro 儷宋 Pro)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:131-160`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:452-459`
- **macOS behaviour:** _local_names reads Windows-Unicode non-English records and falls back only to Mac Japanese (1,1) records. Apple's ST* and LiSong fonts have no Windows non-English records. Their Chinese names are in Mac Simplified Chinese (1,25, lang 33) or Mac Traditional Chinese (1,2, lang 19) records, which fontTools decodes correctly. Result: local_names is () for these faces, so the picker shows no native name, and searching '华文宋体' / '华文黑体' finds nothing, because the search haystack is family + local_names. Chinese users know these fonts by exactly those names from Office/Windows. The scope asked for this platformID-1 check and the auditor didn't report on it.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-catalog/macnames.py over all 411 CoreText files: 'faces with localized family only in Mac non-Roman records: 21', incl. ('STFANGSO.ttf',0,'STFangsong',[((25,33),'华文仿宋')]), ('STHEITI.ttf',0,'STHeiti',[((25,33),'华文黑体')]), ('LiSongPro.ttf',0,'LiSong Pro',[((2,19),'儷宋 Pro')]), ('Kaiti.ttc',1,'STKaiti',[((25,33),'华文楷体')]), ('Songti.ttc',4,'STSong',[((25,33),'华文宋体')]). read_faces: 'STSong Regular local_names= ()', 'STHeiti Regular local_names= ()', 'LiSong Pro Light local_names= ()'. The other hits (Arial Hebrew (1,5), AppleGothic (1,3)) equal the English name and would be dropped anyway. Raanana's (1,5) record raises UnicodeDecodeError, which the existing try/except already handles. By contrast, PingFang SC -> ('苹方-简','蘋方-簡'), Hiragino Sans -> ('ヒラギノ角ゴシック',) and Apple SD Gothic Neo -> ('Apple SD 산돌고딕 Neo',) come out right.
- **Recommendation:** In _local_names, widen the fallback to Mac CJK encodings: collect(lambda r: r.platformID == 1 and r.platEncID in (1, 2, 3, 25)). That is Japanese, Traditional Chinese, Korean and Simplified Chinese, all of which fontTools decodes. Keep Mac Roman (1,0) excluded, keep the try/except UnicodeDecodeError, and map Mac language IDs (33 zh-Hans, 19 zh-Hant, 11 ja, 23 ko) into the LOCAL_LANGS ordering. Add fixtures with (1,25,33,'华文宋体'.encode('gb2312')). Bump the cache SCHEMA together with CATALOG-3.

### CATALOG-8: Apple bitmap font NISC18030.ttf ('bhed' instead of 'head') fails to parse, giving a permanent '1 file couldn't be read' on every Mac

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:170-197`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:46-48`
- **macOS behaviour:** /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf ('GB18030 Bitmap', 7.1 MB) has bdat/bhed/bloc and no head table. _face() does font['head'] and raises KeyError, which is reported as a failure in the picker status on every launch (shown in the screenshot footer). Failures aren't cached, so the 7 MB file is re-parsed on every warm scan.
- **Evidence:** scan_run.py: 'FAIL /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf KeyError: 'head''; tables: ['GlyphOrder','OS/2','bdat','bhed','bloc','cmap','maxp','meta','name','post']. picker_chinese_s.png footer: '795 fonts · 1 file couldn't be read'.
- **Recommendation:** In _face, use head = font['head'] if 'head' in font else font.get('bhed'). With no outline table, outline is already 'none', so the face lists as unsupported ('bitmap-only'). Alternatively, cache failures keyed by (size, mtime) and hide known Apple bitmap-only files from the failure count.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced: 'FAIL /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf KeyError: head'. The tables are GlyphOrder, OS/2, bdat, bhed, bloc, cmap, maxp, meta, name, post. fontTools parses bhed as table__b_h_e_d with unitsPerEm, so the proposed fallback works. The lazy re-open costs 0.03 s, not a meaningful 7 MB re-parse. The file is registered with CoreText. The face would be unsupported as 'bitmap-only' anyway, so the only effect is a permanent '1 file couldn't be read' note, which is cosmetic.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed: head = font['head'] if 'head' in font else font['bhed']. The face is then listed as unsupported (bitmap-only) instead of as a failure.

### CATALOG-9: Dedupe by str(path.resolve()).lower() is wrong on case-sensitive APFS and ignores Unicode normalization

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:27`
- **macOS behaviour:** This Mac is case-insensitive APFS, and both CoreText URLs and rglob return NFD filenames (e.g. 'ヒラギノ角ゴシック W3.ttc'), so the default scan is correct. On a case-sensitive APFS volume (developer setups, external disks added via Add folder), 'Foo.ttf' and 'foo.ttf' would collapse to one. A folder added by the user as an NFC path and as an NFD path (e.g. once from QFileDialog, once typed) would produce two keys for the same file, since resolve() does not normalize. The /Library/Fonts/Arial Unicode.ttf symlink is correctly deduped by resolve().
- **Evidence:** diskutil info /: 'File System Personality: APFS' (not case-sensitive). Normalization check: "NFC==NFD string? False  both exist: True True", "resolve() normalizes? False"; 'walked non-ascii paths present verbatim in CT url set: 12 of 12'. ls -la /Library/Fonts: 'Arial Unicode.ttf -> /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Arial Unicode.ttf'. The case-sensitive scenario was not reproduced: creating a volume was out of bounds.
- **Recommendation:** Dedupe on (st.st_dev, st.st_ino) from os.stat(p). This handles case, NFC/NFD, symlinks and hard links. Keep the first path seen, preferring the CoreText URL, and use that same string as the cache and recipe key.
- **Verifier (partially):** diskutil: both / and /System/Volumes/Data are plain 'APFS' (case-insensitive). NFC vs NFD: '/System/Library/Fonts/ヒラギノ角ゴシック W3.ttc' exists under both spellings, the strings differ, str(resolve()).lower() differs, and st_ino is equal. So two spellings of the same added folder would produce duplicate keys, as claimed. The case-sensitive-volume scenario wasn't reproduced. Both scenarios need unusual user action (a font pair differing only in case, or the same folder added under two Unicode spellings). The default scan is correct. Polish, not minor.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed: dedupe on (st_dev, st_ino). Python fills st_ino from the NTFS file index on Windows, so the same code works there.

### CATALOG-10: .dfont suitcases and extensionless PostScript fonts are skipped

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort M; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:9`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:200-214`
- **macOS behaviour:** FONT_EXTENSIONS leaves out .dfont. None of the CoreText-registered files on this Mac is a .dfont; 3 exist only in FontServices.framework/Resources/Fonts/ApplicationSupport (not registered). HelveLTMM and TimesLTMM are extensionless datafork PostScript Type 1 fonts that fontTools can't read, and they are hidden anyway. Users with old .dfont files in ~/Library/Fonts would see them silently ignored. fontTools can read .dfont via res_name_or_index, but read_faces can't.
- **Evidence:** getSFNTResIndices(GenevaCY.dfont) -> [1]; TTFont(p, res_name_or_index=1): 'Geneva CY Regular True 234'; read_faces(p): 'TTLibError Not a TrueType or OpenType font (bad sfntVersion)'. `file /System/Library/Fonts/HelveLTMM`: 'Mac OSX datafork font, PostScript'.
- **Recommendation:** Low priority. If wanted, accept .dfont, enumerate the faces with fontTools.ttLib.macUtils.getSFNTResIndices, and store the resource index as the face index (the engine must open it the same way). Keep skipping non-sfnt files quietly instead of listing them as failures.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** No registered font on this Mac is a .dfont. .dfont files exist only in private frameworks: FontServices …/ApplicationSupport/{HelveticaCY,GenevaCY,CharcoalCY}.dfont and Slideshows.framework. `file HelveLTMM` -> 'Mac OSX datafork font, PostScript'. is_font_file already skips the extensionless Type 1 files silently.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Low priority as stated. If implemented, the engine must open the face with the same res_name_or_index, which touches the forge dimension.

### CATALOG-11: Suggestions ignore the platform's own default font per language (native feel)

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:157-193`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:503-520`
- **macOS behaviour:** With the CoreText catalog prototype, 'Suggested for your text' for Chinese showed Lantinghei SC, BiauKaiHK and BiauKaiTC. It ranks by coverage and then fewest glyphs, so it passes over PingFang SC, the font macOS itself uses for zh-Hans. CoreText can report the system's choice per language.
- **Evidence:** Prototype picker rows: "['SUGGESTED FOR YOUR TEXT', 'Lantinghei SC', 'BiauKaiHK', 'BiauKaiTC', 'ALL CHINESE FONTS · 37', …]". CTFontCreateForStringWithLanguage(Helvetica, s, range, lang): 'zh-Hans -> PingFang SC', 'zh-Hant -> PingFang TC', 'zh-HK -> PingFang HK', 'ja -> Hiragino Sans (HiraginoSans-W3)', 'ko -> Apple SD Gothic Neo', 'ar -> Geeza Pro', 'hi -> Kohinoor Devanagari', 'th -> Thonburi'. (CTFontCopyDefaultCascadeListForLanguages only returns the virtual '.AppleSystemFallback'.)
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, at startup, compute preferred PostScript names per Language by calling CTFontCreateForStringWithLanguage with that language's sample text and language tag. Map each Language id to a BCP-47 tag (chinese_s→zh-Hans, chinese_t→zh-Hant, japanese→ja, korean→ko…). Pass the result to suggest_materials as a tie-breaker, or pin it as the first suggestion. It is a pure lookup and the result can be cached.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** CTFontCreateForStringWithLanguage(Helvetica, '永', lang) -> zh-Hans PingFang SC, zh-Hant PingFang TC, ja Hiragino Sans, ko Apple SD Gothic Neo (verified). The API needs macOS 13+, which is fine: the PySide6 6.11.2 wheel is tagged macosx_13_0.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed. Use it as a tie-breaker rather than pinning, so the user's text coverage still dominates.

### CATALOG-12: Variable fonts show as one face while macOS shows their named instances

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort M; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:170-197`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:73-91`
- **macOS behaviour:** CoreText lists each named instance as a face, e.g. SFNS.ttf 369, Skia 10, STIXTwoText 4, KefaIII 4, AnthropicMono 6. The scanner lists one face per variable file, at its default weight. Setting the wght axis works through Qt on macOS, so this only affects how families look compared with Font Book.
- **Evidence:** name_diff.py: 'face count differs /System/Library/Fonts/SFNS.ttf 369 1', 'Skia.ttf 10 1', '~/Library/Fonts/AnthropicMono-Roman.ttf 6 1'. qt_honesty.py: 'Skia wght 0.48..3.2 widths [234.125, 262.25]', 'System Font wght 1.0..1000.0 widths [208.125, 250.25]' (the axis takes effect).
- **Recommendation:** Optionally show fvar named instances in the picker, mapping each to a fixed wght (MixFont.weight). Keep one catalog face per file so the build is unchanged.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Skia.ttf: 10 fvar instances = 10 CoreText descriptors. SFNS.ttf: 369 = 369. The scanner gives one face per file. SFNS disappears anyway once the PostScript '.'-name filter from CATALOG-2 lands.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed (optional).

### CATALOG-M3: Catalog cache (regenerable, 5–13 MB JSON) is stored in Application Support instead of ~/Library/Caches

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:93-98`, `fontplayground/paths.py:27-35`
- **macOS behaviour:** catalog.json sits next to settings.json and forge_last.json in ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground. It is 5.4 MB with today's scanner and about 12.7 MB with the CoreText union (auditor's prototype). macOS convention is to keep data that can be regenerated in ~/Library/Caches/<app>, where the system and cleanup tools expect to find it and may purge it. Application Support is for user data such as settings and recipes.
- **Evidence:** app.py:95 'self.cache = CatalogCache(self.config_dir / "catalog.json")'. Measured cache size in work/verify-catalog/scan1.py: 'cache MB 5.4'. Apple File System Programming Guide, 'The Library Directory Stores App-Specific Files' (Caches = data that can be regenerated): https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/FileManagement/Conceptual/FileSystemProgrammingGuide/MacOSXDirectories/MacOSXDirectories.html
- **Recommendation:** Add paths.cache_dir(): ~/Library/Caches/FontPlayground on darwin, %LOCALAPPDATA%\FontPlayground on Windows (unchanged), and $XDG_CACHE_HOME/fontplayground on Linux. Point CatalogCache at it. A missing cache just triggers a cold scan (2–6 s), so no migration is needed.

#### Data points

- Scanner (current code) on this Mac: 379 files (213 .ttf, 128 .ttc, 38 .otf), 795 faces, 379 families, 1 failure (NISC18030.ttf KeyError 'head'); cold 2.73 s, warm 0.19 s; catalog.json 5.4 MB
- CoreText: CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs = 2988 URLs / 411 unique files in 0.04 s; CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames = 221 (no '.' names); CTFontCollectionCreateFromAvailableFonts = 624 descriptors (93 downloadable+downloaded); CTFontManagerCopyAvailablePostScriptNames = 600; the URLs cover 2,964 PostScript names, 2,150 of them '.'-hidden
- Files CoreText knows that the scanner misses: 33 (29 in AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8, 1 PingFangUI.ttc in PrivateFrameworks, NISC18030, 2 extensionless Type1)
- Hidden faces in the current catalog: 112 faces / 74 '.'-families (102 marked supported); .LastResort maps 1,114,112 code points with 7 glyphs and is the #1 suggestion
- Chinese (Simplified) picker: 10 families now (2 hidden, no PingFang) vs 37 with the CoreText enumerator plus '.'-filter prototype
- CT+dirs prototype scan: 409 files, 920 faces, cold 6.47 s, warm 0.50 s, catalog.json 12.7 MB
- Family-name mismatches vs CoreText: 33 faces / 18 families (Arabic/Indic Apple fonts) now; 0 with the patched _name_rank; test suite 339 passed with the patch
- CoreText hides 127 families with ordinary names from menus (Times, Courier, Hiragino Kaku Gothic Pro/ProN, Noto Sans rare scripts, STIX, Seravek…); Qt families() = 242 (CT 221 + fallback '.'-families)
- MobileAsset catalog: 1,289 macOS faces / 607 families; 955 faces / 425 visible families downloadable but not on disk
- CT family matching: installed families 0.0 s; not-downloaded 'Tsukushi A Round Gothic' blocked more than 90 s, 'Osaka' more than 20 s
- Qt identity check via QRawFont.fromFont().fontTable('head'): 1.6 ms per face
- Qt/CoreText: NoFontMerging stops fallback (Helvetica 'A永한' -> glyphs [36,0,0]); without it, PingFang SC and Apple SD Gothic Neo fill in
- addApplicationFont(PingFang.ttc) registers all 24 faces; face_font picks the exact TTC index for PingFang HK/MO/TC/SC, Hiragino W6 and Songti SC/TC/STSong
- Qt reports English family names even with AppleLanguages=(zh-Hans) (QFontInfo -> 'PingFang SC'), so system_font name matching is locale-safe
- Filesystem: APFS case-insensitive; Apple's Japanese font filenames are stored NFD; CoreText URLs and rglob both return NFD
- Embedding flags across the Mac scan: installable 454, preview-print 196, editable 129, restricted 16 (PingFang: preview-print; SFNS: installable)
- Evidence artifacts: work/catalog/picker_chinese_s.png (before), work/catalog/picker_chinese_s_after.png (prototype), work/catalog/merge_vs_nomerge.png, scan_faces.json, ct_descs.json, downloadable_missing.json

## Forge engine on real macOS fonts (`ENGINE-*`)

I forged about 30 realistic combinations of the Mac's own fonts. Each ran in its own process with a time limit. I also inventoried all 889 font faces on this Mac (409 files in /System/Library/Fonts, Supplemental, AssetsV2 and ~/Library/Fonts). The most important Mac combination currently fails: Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC stops at the final check after about 22 s. Helvetica Neue's character map uses an old Apple Unicode entry that the fontTools merger discards, so the result has no characters. 13 families are affected this way. Fonts that use Apple's own shaping system (AAT, the morx/kern/kerx tables) build without errors but produce broken text on macOS. 189 faces work this way, including Geeza Pro (Arabic), Devanagari MT, Thonburi (Thai) and Malayalam MN. After the forge, CoreText draws Arabic as unjoined letters, and Hindi and Malayalam lose their conjuncts and letter reordering. With my fixes applied to a copy of the code, performance is in line with the README: Latin + CJK takes 19–20 s, Korean 6.6 s, other scripts under 4 s. Two exceptions: when the CJK font needs a synthetic bold it takes 42–60 s, and if the optional uharfbuzz package is installed, the PingFang case never finishes (over 20 min). Beyond the code, two licensing issues need handling. Apple's system fonts (SF, PingFang, and everything else bundled with macOS) come under the macOS licence, which forbids derivative works. The engine also always marks the output as freely installable (fsType 0).

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| ENGINE-1 | blocker | confirmed | Helvetica Neue and 12 other Apple families lose their whole character map in the merge, so the forge fails at verify |
| ENGINE-2 | blocker | confirmed | AAT-only fonts (morx/kerx, no GSUB) forge without error but render broken: Arabic unjoined, Indic without conjuncts or reordering, Thai marks unpositioned |

| ENGINE-4 | major | confirmed | Synthetic bold on PingFang fails the whole forge: skia-pathops errors on 2 of 36,717 glyphs |
| ENGINE-5 | major | partially | Output always gets fsType=0 and a licence warning only for 'restricted' fonts; Apple's licence forbids derivative works of bundled fonts |
| ENGINE-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | Apple fonts with no OS/2 table (Courier, AppleGothic, AppleMyungjo, Hei, Kai, GungSeo) make every forge fail with KeyError or TypeError |
| ENGINE-M3 | major | (verifier-found) | The forged PostScript name can equal a real macOS font's name ('AvenirNext-Regular') or collapse to 'Forged-Regular'; CoreText then resolves the system name to the forged file |
| ENGINE-6 | minor | confirmed | Variable fonts instance correctly, but optical size is pinned at the fvar default and Apple 'trak' tracking is dropped, so forged SF/New York look different from the system |
| ENGINE-7 | minor | confirmed | Apple AAT kerning (kern version 1 state tables, kerx) is dropped silently; only format-0 kern pairs are converted |

| ENGINE-9 | minor | partially | The forge runs in the GUI process: CJK stages of 17–60 s cannot be cancelled and about 0.9 GB of memory stays with the app |

| ENGINE-11 | polish | confirmed | Vertical CJK metrics (vhea/vmtx/VORG) and BASE are dropped while the 'vert' feature is kept |

### ENGINE-1: Helvetica Neue and 12 other Apple families lose their whole character map in the merge, so the forge fails at verify

- **Severity:** blocker (auditor: blocker); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:21`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:159`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:129`
- **macOS behaviour:** fontTools.merge only reads cmap format 4 subtables with (platform, encoding) (3,1),(0,3),(0,4),(0,6), and format 12 with (3,10),(0,4),(0,6). It drops any other subtable with only a warning. Helvetica Neue's only cmap subtable is (0,1) format 4, so the merged font has an empty cmap and verify() raises. The headline use case, Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC, fails after about 22 s in the user's environment. 25 of the 878 supported faces are affected: .Keyboard, .LastResort, Big Caslon, Cochin, Didot, Geneva, Helvetica Neue, Herculanum, Webdings, Wingdings 1/2/3 and Zapfino.
- **Evidence:** Shared venv (no uharfbuzz), specs/a_helveticaneue_pingfangsc.json: 'Dropped cmap subtable from font '0': format 4, platformID 0, platEncID 1' followed by 'False [verify] coverage mismatch: 2083 missing, 0 unexpected' after 22.4 s. Helvetica Neue alone: prepared part cmap has 2085 entries, then merge_fonts() gives 'merged cmap 0 [(3, 1, 4, 0)]'. fontTools/merge/cmap.py _CmapUnicodePlatEncodings BMP={(4,3,1),(4,0,3),(4,0,4),(4,0,6)}. Scan with work/engine/cmap_scan2.py: 'supported faces: 878 whose cmap the merger drops entirely: 25'. Helvetica Neue + Geeza Pro and Helvetica Neue + Apple Symbols also fail ('coverage mismatch: 2085 missing' and '1781 missing').
- **Recommendation:** In prepare(), right after subset_font(), rebuild the cmap from font.getBestCmap() as a (3,1) format 4 subtable, plus a (3,10) format 12 subtable when any code point is above 0xFFFF, and keep any format 14 subtable as (0,5). I tested this `normalize_cmap()` in work/engine/repo_fixed/fontplayground/engine/prepare.py. Helvetica Neue alone, + Geeza Pro, + Apple Symbols and + PingFang all forge correctly, and the 48 engine tests pass. Add a fixture test with a (0,1)-only cmap font.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced with my own harness (work/verify-engine/vforge.py, fresh ForgeSpec with no smart/languages code). Helvetica Neue Regular alone: log 'Dropped cmap subtable from font '0': format 4, platformID 0, platEncID 1', then '[verify] coverage mismatch: 2085 missing'. Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC (han, cjk_symbols, kana): fails at verify after 22.7 s with '2083 missing'. Helvetica Neue Italic has a (0,3) subtable and forges fine, so only some HN faces are hit. Count correction from my cmapscan.py: 21 faces in 9 families have a non-empty cmap that the merger drops (.Keyboard, .LastResort, Big Caslon, Cochin x4, Didot x2, Geneva (12,0,3), Helvetica Neue x9, Herculanum, Zapfino). Webdings and Wingdings 1/2/3 should not be in the list. They have (3,0) symbol cmaps, getBestCmap() returns None for them, so the catalog gives them 0 code points: they contribute nothing and never reach verify. Windows ships the same (3,0) cmaps for these fonts, so the claim that this never happens on Windows is wrong for symbol fonts. Cochin and Geneva actually fail earlier, on the 'bloc' parse crash (see ENGINE-M2). I implemented normalize_cmap myself in work/verify-engine/repo_fixed/.../prepare.py and confirmed the fix. HN alone forges with 2085 code points. HN + PingFang takes 16.6 s and gives 39,252 glyphs and 10.9 MB. With M1+M2 fixes also applied, a sweep of all 909 supported faces has no cmap fail …
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Use the auditor's fix as written: rebuild the cmap from getBestCmap() as (3,1) format 4, plus (3,10) format 12 when any code point is above 0xFFFF, and keep format 14 as (0,5). Call it right after subset_font() in prepare(). Also add a regression fixture with a (0,1)-only cmap. Handling (3,0) symbol fonts is a separate cross-platform task (map 0xF0xx to their codes or mark them unsupported).

### ENGINE-2: AAT-only fonts (morx/kerx, no GSUB) forge without error but render broken: Arabic unjoined, Indic without conjuncts or reordering, Thai marks unpositioned

- **Severity:** blocker (auditor: blocker); effort M; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:70`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:131`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:170`, `fontplayground/ui/languages.py:111`
- **macOS behaviour:** The subsetter logs 'morx NOT subset; don't know how to subset; dropped', and strip_tables() keeps only the KEEP_TABLES list. All AAT shaping is lost and the report says nothing. CoreText (what every Mac app uses) then draws the forged text unshaped. 189 faces in 84 families have morx but no GSUB. The picker's 'draws it well' filter still lists them: 22 of 36 Arabic families (including Geeza Pro, the classic Arabic system font, and Baghdad, Nadeem, Al Bayan, Diwan and Farah), 5 of 39 Indic (Devanagari MT, Gujarati MT, Gurmukhi MT, Malayalam MN, Malayalam Sangam MN), 5 of 22 Thai/SE Asian (Thonburi, Sathu, Ayuthaya, Krungthep, Silom), and 5 of 18 Hebrew (Raanana, New Peninim, Corsiva Hebrew, Lucida Grande). Latin fonts lose their morx ligatures too (Helvetica Neue ffi/fi/fl).
- **Evidence:** work/engine/shape_compare.py compares the source and forged fonts with CoreText and HarfBuzz. Geeza Pro 'مرحبا بالعالم': CoreText on the source gives 13 glyphs with only 1 plain form (u0645.final.meem u0644.initial.lam ...); CoreText on the forged font gives 13 plain forms (u0645.meem u0644.lam ...), i.e. unjoined. Devanagari MT 'नमस्ते दुनिया क्षत्रिय': source 15 glyphs (saprehalfdeva, kassadeva, ...); forged 22 plain glyphs (sadeva viramadeva ...). Thonburi: source 13 glyphs with mark ligatures; forged 19. Malayalam MN: the 'mal_k.ssa' conjunct becomes 'mal_ka mal_chandra mal_ssa'. Helvetica Neue 'office fifty flow': source uses uniFB03/fi/fl ligatures; forged has 17 plain glyphs. Control cases with OpenType shaping come out identical after forging: Kohinoor Devanagari, .SF Arabic, and Damascus (which has GSUB 'arab' as well as morx). Counts from work/engine/inventory.py and picker_scan.py.
- **Recommendation:** Merging AAT tables with fontTools is not feasible, and converting morx to GSUB is not feasible in general. Instead: (1) At scan time, add to FontFace the GSUB/GPOS ScriptList tags (the scripts it has OpenType rules for) and has_morx/has_kerx, and bump the cache schema. (2) Define a helper along the lines of `ot_shapes(face, group)`, true when the GSUB ScriptList has the script tag (arab; dev2/deva, mlm2 and the other Indic tags; thai/lao/khmr/mymr; hebr). For complex groups (arabic, indic, southeast_asian, and hebrew when niqqud matters), hide or grey out fonts that fail it in the picker, with the tooltip 'Uses Apple-only shaping (AAT); text would break in the forged font'. (3) In prepare(), when a material has morx and no GSUB rules for a script it is assigned, add a report warning, and make it an error for arabic/indic/southeast_asian. (4) Suggest OpenType alternatives that ship with macOS: Arabic: Damascus, Noto Nastaliq Urdu, Arial/Times New Roman/Tahoma/Courier New (Supplemental). Hindi: Kohinoor Devanagari, ITF Devanagari, Devanagari Sangam MN, Shree Devanagari 714. Thai: Sukhumvit Set, Tahoma. Hebrew: Arial Hebrew, Times New Roman. (5) For Latin AAT fonts such as Helvetica Neue, report 'ligatures (AAT) not carried over' as a warning only.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced independently with CoreText (work/verify-engine/ctshape.py, which loads fonts through CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL with no registration). Avenir Next + Geeza Pro: the source gives 13 glyphs in joined forms (u0645.final.meem, u0644.initial.lam, ...). The forged font gives 13 isolated forms (u0645.meem, u0644.lam, ...) and is 29% wider. Devanagari MT 'नमस्ते क्षत्रिय': source 9 glyphs (saprehalfdeva, kassadeva, ivowelsignnarrowdeva placed before the consonant); forged 15 glyphs (sadeva viramadeva ..., i-matra left in logical order). Thonburi 'ปั้นป่ำ ที่นี่ ญี่ปุ่น': source 10 glyphs with mark-stack ligatures; forged 22 unstacked glyphs. Helvetica Neue 'office fifty flow' (with the ENGINE-1 fix): the source uses uniFB03/fi/fl and the forged font draws plain f+i. Kerning survives (width 237.4 vs 237.3). Controls come out identical after forging: Kohinoor Devanagari and Damascus (which has both GSUB 'arab' and morx). Counts from my aatscan.py (uses the app's own covers_well): 263 morx faces, 189 morx without GSUB, 84 families. Among families that pass 'draws it well' but have no GSUB rules for the script: Arabic 23/36, Indic 6/39, SE Asian 8/22, Hebrew 7/18. My counts include .LastResort and Arial Unicode MS, so they are close to the auditor's. covers_well() (ui/languages.py:111) counts code points only, so these fonts are all offered.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same as the auditor's. One addition: the check must be per script tag, not just 'has GSUB'. Songti SC/STSong have a GSUB and pass covers_well for Arabic but have no 'arab' script, and Arial Unicode MS has 'arab' but not 'thai' or 'hebr'. Store the GSUB/GPOS ScriptList tags in FontFace (and bump the cache schema), then use them in the picker filter and the prepare() warning. The OpenType alternatives on this Mac are confirmed: Arabic: Damascus, Noto Nastaliq Urdu, Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma, Courier New, Microsoft Sans Serif. Indic: Kohinoor Devanagari, ITF Devanagari, Devanagari Sangam MN, Shree Devanagari 714. Thai: Sukhumvit Set, Tahoma. Hebrew: Arial Hebrew, Arial, Times New Roman, Tahoma.

### ENGINE-3: PingFang reuses its locl lookups in its cv (character variant) features: dropping 'locl' has no effect, adds 12k glyphs, and hangs the forge if uharfbuzz is installed

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:58`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:61`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:26`
- **macOS behaviour:** DROPPED_FEATURES={'locl'} is meant to keep pan-CJK fonts small. But PingFang SC's cv08/cv09 point at exactly the same lookups as its locl (#16 with 9,325 mappings and #19 with 8,570); for TC these are cv04/cv05 and for HK/MO cv06/cv07. aalt (27,112 alternates) adds more, so the closure still pulls in the regional variants. The result: PingFang SC for CJK text is 46,770 glyphs instead of 34,658 (+12,112), the GSUB offsets overflow, and the full PingFang forge has 48,124 glyphs instead of 38,054. Without uharfbuzz fontTools resolves the overflow in about 23 s. With uharfbuzz importable, fontTools' HB_FT loop calls hb.repack on every overflow iteration and fails each time: PingFang alone was killed at 600 s, and Helvetica Neue + PingFang was killed at 1200 s (peak 940 MB) without ever leaving prepare. This matters if the Mac port adds uharfbuzz, for example for shaping checks or preview.
- **Evidence:** work/engine/closure2.py: 'cv08 lookups=[(16, 1, 9325)]', 'cv09 lookups=[(19, 7, 8570)]', 'locl lookups=[(16..)] / [(19..)]', 'aalt lookups=[(0, 7, 27112)]'. work/engine/closure.py: 'kept_features (as engine) glyphs= 46770 (+12112 beyond cmap)'. work/engine/locl_alias_scan.py: PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO and Arial Unicode MS (salt, smpl, trad) are affected. Baseline in my venv with uharfbuzz: out/a.err 'hb.repack failed to serialize 'GSUB'...', rc=142 after 1200 s. faulthandler stacks stuck in otBase.py:151 compile, called from TTFont.save. Same run with have_uharfbuzz=False: 24 s. Shared venv: 23 s, 48,124 glyphs. Fixed copy (drops features sharing lookups with dropped features, plus aalt): PingFang prepare 'subset=5.5s cff_to_glyf=6.3s save=2.2s', 36,722 glyphs; Helvetica Neue + PingFang 19.4 s total, 39,252 glyphs, 10.9 MB.
- **Recommendation:** In kept_features(), also drop every feature whose LookupListIndex overlaps the lookups of a DROPPED_FEATURES feature, and drop 'aalt' (it only exists for glyph palettes and pulls in every alternate). Tested in work/engine/repo_fixed/fontplayground/engine/prepare.py. Separately, protect against the hb.repack loop: in prepare/merge, set font.cfg['fontTools.ttLib.tables.otBase:USE_HARFBUZZ_REPACKER']=False, or pin an uharfbuzz version known to be good and test it. Recalibrate smart.VARIANT_FACTOR against real Mac CJK fonts.
- **Verifier (partially):** The lookup sharing is confirmed (work/verify-engine/locl.py). In PingFang SC, locl uses lookups [16,19]; cv08 shares [16] (type 1, 9,325 mappings) and cv09 shares [19]; aalt is lookup 0 with 27,112 alternates. My closure test (closure_v.py) shows both parts of the fix are needed. Full PingFang SC cmap: current 48,124 glyphs; dropping cv08+cv09 only: 48,124; dropping aalt only: 48,113; dropping both: 38,054. The uharfbuzz loop is confirmed with the auditor's venv (uharfbuzz 0.56.2) in a bounded run. Compiling that subset's GSUB made 520 overflow resolutions and was still going at 226 s when my 240 s alarm killed it. With the fixed feature set it compiled in 0.1 s with 0 resolutions. Correction: without uharfbuzz there is no overflow at all (GSUB compiled in 0.3 s, 0 resolutions). The auditor's 'fontTools resolves the overflow in about 23 s' is really just the whole forge time. The bloat matters even without uharfbuzz. An Avenir Next + PingFang SC (han) + Hiragino Sans W3 (kana) + Apple SD Gothic Neo (hangul) forge has 64,363 glyphs with current code (1,172 below the 65,535 limit, 17.9 MB, 27.3 s). With the fix it has 53,217 glyphs, 14.3 MB, 22.2 s. So adding almost any fourth script font to a pan-CJK mix fails today.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Drop 'aalt' AND every feature whose LookupListIndex overlaps a dropped feature's lookups. Either one alone does not help, as measured above. Set font.cfg['fontTools.ttLib.tables.otBase:USE_HARFBUZZ_REPACKER']=False on each prepared TTFont AND on the TTFont returned by Merger().merge(), which is a new object with default cfg. Alternatively, declare uharfbuzz unsupported in pyproject. Recalibrate smart.VARIANT_FACTOR/GLYPH_WARN on the real pan-CJK numbers.

### ENGINE-4: Synthetic bold on PingFang fails the whole forge: skia-pathops errors on 2 of 36,717 glyphs

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/synth_bold.py:36`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:165`
- **macOS behaviour:** Helvetica Neue Bold + PingFang SC Regular at weight 700 (+300 synthetic bold) fails with '[prepare (PingFang SC Regular)] PathOpsError: operation did not succeed' after 23 s. The failing glyphs are cid13022 and cid43492; one bad glyph aborts the entire build. When it works it is slow: about 25–32 s of union operations on about 36.7k CJK glyphs, and the output grows from 10.9 MB to 24 MB. With default_weight 700, SF + Hiragino Sans W6 also gets +100 synthetic bold on 13k glyphs, taking 42.3 s instead of 19.9 s.
- **Evidence:** outfix/a2_helveticaneue_bold_pingfangsc_w700.json 'PathOpsError: operation did not succeed'. work/engine/bold_probe.py: 'PingFang SC Regular delta=300: 36717 outlined glyphs, 2 PathOps failures in 32.4s; first: ['cid13022', 'cid43492']'. The Semibold face at +100 has 0 failures. After patching (try/except, simplify(), retry, else keep the regular outline): 'True forge_s 59.81 ... glyphs 39246 size_kb 24425'.
- **Recommendation:** In embolden(), catch pathops.PathOpsError for each glyph, retry once on Path.simplify(), and otherwise keep the original outline. Count these glyphs and report them ('N glyphs could not be emboldened'). Prefer a real heavier face over synthetic bold. PingFang ships Medium/Semibold and Hiragino ships W0–W9 as separate files, so choose the nearest real weight before falling back to synthetic bold.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced (work/verify-engine/boldv.py). Subsetting PingFang SC to U+45EC and U+25ED7 (cid13022/cid43492) and calling embolden(): +100 ok, +200 ok, +300 raises 'PathOpsError operation did not succeed'. End to end, Avenir Next Bold + PingFang SC Regular at weight 700 with current code fails with '[prepare (PingFang SC Regular)] PathOpsError: operation did not succeed' after 24.8 s. With my per-glyph fallback (op on simplify()'d paths, else keep the outline) it succeeds in 58.5 s, 38,365 glyphs, 24.4 MB (vs about 10.9 MB unbolded), peak 956 MB. The code is not Mac-specific, but PingFang Regular is the natural Mac CJK pick that triggers it.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** The per-glyph try/except plus simplify-and-retry, counting the failures in the report, is sound; I tested it. Also prefer the nearest real weight from the same collection: PingFang.ttc has Medium 500 and Semibold 600, and Hiragino ships W0–W9. Semibold at +100 had 0 failures in the auditor's probe. Consider warning when synthetic bold would more than double the output size.

### ENGINE-5: Output always gets fsType=0 and a licence warning only for 'restricted' fonts; Apple's licence forbids derivative works of bundled fonts

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:120`, `fontplayground/engine/forge.py:23`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:90`, `README.md`
- **macOS behaviour:** Embedding permissions (fsType) across all Mac faces: 454 faces fsType 0, 243 preview&print (4), 168 editable (8), 17 restricted (2/6/14: Songti SC Black, STFangsong, Ayuthaya, Futura, Gill Sans, Lucida Grande, Brush Script MT, Zapf Dingbats). Only those 17 produce a warning. PingFang (4), Avenir Next (4), .SF Arabic (4) and Hiragino (8) are silently marked installable in the output. More fundamentally, every font bundled with macOS is covered by the macOS licence. It allows use to display and print while running the Apple Software, restricts embedding, and forbids modifying or creating derivative works, which is exactly what a forged font is. SF fonts downloaded from developer.apple.com are licensed only for UI mock-ups. The vendor ID cannot detect Apple fonts reliably (PingFang 'DYNA', Helvetica Neue 'LINO', SF 'APPL').
- **Evidence:** work/engine/inventory.json fsType counter: {0: 454, 4: 243, 8: 168, 6: 13, 2: 3, 14: 1}. d2 (SF + .SF Arabic, source fsType 4) output os2 fsType 0 with no licence warning in the report. /Library/Documentation/License.lpdf/Contents/Resources/English.lproj/License.html §2.E: 'you may use the fonts included with the Apple Software to display and print content while running the Apple Software; however, you may only embed fonts in content if that is permitted by the embedding restrictions'. §2.N forbids to 'modify, or create derivative works of the Apple Software ... or any part thereof'. https://developer.apple.com/fonts/ licence: 'solely for creating mock-ups of user interfaces'.
- **Recommendation:** (1) Set the output fsType to the most restrictive fsType among materials that actually contribute glyphs, instead of 0; at least never raise permissions. (2) Warn for preview&print and editable fonts too, not only restricted. (3) For materials whose real path is under /System/Library/Fonts or /System/Library/AssetsV2, add a report note along the lines of 'Bundled with macOS: licensed only for use on this Mac (macOS SLA §2.E, §2.N); do not distribute the forged font', and show it next to 'Save a copy…'. (4) Update the README licence section for macOS.
- **Verifier (partially):** Code confirmed: merge.py:120 sets os2.fsType = 0 unconditionally, and forge.py:23 warns only for 'restricted'. SLA text confirmed at /Library/Documentation/License.lpdf/Contents/Resources/English.lproj/License.html. §2.E Fonts: 'use the fonts included with the Apple Software to display and print content while running the Apple Software; ... may only embed fonts ... if that is permitted by the embedding restrictions'. §2.N No Reverse Engineering: '...modify, or create derivative works of the Apple Software ... or any part thereof'. My fsType tally matches the auditor's: {0:461, 4:250, 8:171, 6:13, 2:4, 14:1, no OS/2:10}, with no 0x100 no-subsetting bits. Correction: a blanket 'bundled with macOS, so licensed only for this Mac' note is wrong for a large share of files. 140 of 398 font files under /System/Library/Fonts, Supplemental and AssetsV2 declare OFL or Apache in name ID 13/14 (all Noto Sans/Serif *, STIX*, Mukta Mahee), and those allow derivatives under their own terms. Also, forcing fsType 0 is cross-platform, not a Mac gap.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** (1) Output fsType = the most restrictive among materials that contribute glyphs, in the order restricted 0x2 > preview&print 0x4 > editable 0x8 > installable 0; never raise permissions. (2) Warn for 4 and 8 as well as 2. (3) Classify licences by name ID 13/14 (and 0/7): OFL or Apache gives 'derivative allowed under OFL/Apache: keep the licence, don't use the Reserved Font Name'. An Apple copyright or vendor 'APPL', or a file under /System/Library with no open licence, gives the macOS SLA §2.E/§2.N note. Show it next to 'Save a copy…'. (4) Update the README.

### ENGINE-M1: Apple fonts with no OS/2 table (Courier, AppleGothic, AppleMyungjo, Hei, Kai, GungSeo) make every forge fail with KeyError or TypeError

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:80`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:106`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:115`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:30`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:173`
- **macOS behaviour:** 10 faces on this Mac have no OS/2 table. Nine are real, selectable fonts: Courier.ttc x4 (the classic Mac Courier), AppleGothic, AppleMyungjo, and the AssetsV2 downloads Hei, Kai and GungSeo. The catalog tolerates the missing table (face.py falls back to weight 400 and 'installable'). The engine does not. As main font, finish() raises KeyError on base['OS/2'] in upgrade_os2 and copy_vertical_metrics. As a secondary material, fontTools.merge gets NotImplemented for the missing OS/2 and raises a TypeError. The user sees '[merge] TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'NotImplementedType' and 'int''.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-engine/sweep.py forged all 909 supported faces (sampled code points) alone and under Avenir Next. Current code: 9x '[finish] KeyError: "'OS/2' table not found"' (alone) for Courier x4, AppleGothic, GungSeo, Hei, Kai, .LastResort. As secondaries: 7x '[merge] TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for |: 'int' and 'NotImplementedType'', 1x '... for &' and 1x ''>' not supported'. Direct runs: 'Courier.ttc:0 → [finish] KeyError', 'Avenir Next + AppleGothic (hangul) → [merge] TypeError'. After adding ensure_os2() to load_face in work/verify-engine/repo_fixed (FontBuilder(font=font).setupOS2(version=4, weights from head.macStyle, typo and win metrics from hhea and head)), Courier, AppleGothic, Hei (Avenir + Hei han/cjk_symbols, 2.6 s) and AppleMyungjo all forge. The full fixed sweep has 0 real failures except one hidden font.
- **Recommendation:** In prepare.load_face(), synthesize an OS/2 v4 table when it is missing, using fontTools.fontBuilder.FontBuilder(font=font).setupOS2(...). Take usWeightClass and fsSelection from head.macStyle, sTypo* from hhea, usWin* from max(hhea, head bbox), and fsType 0 but flag 'licence unknown' in the report. The report should say 'OS/2 table synthesized'. Add a fixture test with the OS/2 table deleted.

### ENGINE-M3: The forged PostScript name can equal a real macOS font's name ('AvenirNext-Regular') or collapse to 'Forged-Regular'; CoreText then resolves the system name to the forged file

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:32`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:56`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:58`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:105`
- **macOS behaviour:** postscript_name() removes every non-ASCII character.
