The default family for Avenir Next + Damascus is 'Avenir Next دمشق', because the catalog names Damascus 'دمشق' (see ENGINE-M4). Its PostScript name becomes 'AvenirNext-Regular', which is exactly the PostScript name of the system Avenir Next Regular. Any family made only of CJK or other non-Latin text becomes 'Forged-Regular', so all such forged fonts share one PostScript name. Name ID 3 uses only the date and name ID 5 is always 'Version 1.000', so re-forging the same mix gives a font CoreText cannot tell apart from the earlier one. macOS identifies fonts by PostScript name (CTFontCreateWithName, NSFont(name:), font references in Pages/Keynote/PDF, CSS local()). I registered the forged file at process scope and looked up 'AvenirNext-Regular': it now resolves to the forged file instead of /System/Library/Fonts/Avenir Next.ttc. At user scope (install), CoreText defines kCTFontManagerErrorDuplicatedName (305). Qt's family-based preview was not affected in my test.
- **Evidence:** postscript_name('我的字体','常规') gives 'Forged-Regular'. smart.default_family_name([Avenir Next Regular, Damascus Regular]) gives 'Avenir Next دمشق', and the PostScript name is 'AvenirNext-Regular'. Geeza Pro gives the same. Forged out/av_damascus_auto.ttf: name 6 'AvenirNext-Regular'. CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURL(..., kCTFontManagerScopeProcess) returns True, and then CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize('AvenirNext-Regular') resolves to file:///…/verify-engine/out/av_damascus_auto.ttf (before registering: /System/Library/Fonts/Avenir%20Next.ttc). Two forged files with PostScript name 'VTest-Regular' both register, and the name resolves only to the first. CTFontManagerErrors.h:76: 'kCTFontManagerErrorDuplicatedName — The file could not be registered because of a duplicated font name.'
- **Recommendation:** Make the PostScript name unique and never equal to an existing one. When postscript_name() drops characters, or always, append a short stable hash of the full family and style (e.g. 'AvenirNext-FP3fa2c1-Regular', at most 63 characters, [A-Za-z0-9-] only). Check it against the catalog's PostScript names (store name ID 6 in FontFace) and change the suffix on a clash. Put a timestamp in name ID 3 and bump head.fontRevision and name ID 5 on each forge, so Font Book and CoreText treat a re-forge as a new version. The install flow should handle error 305.

### ENGINE-6: Variable fonts instance correctly, but optical size is pinned at the fvar default and Apple 'trak' tracking is dropped, so forged SF/New York look different from the system

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:35`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`
- **macOS behaviour:** Weight instancing is exact. SF (System Font, SFNS.ttf, avar v1 + HVAR) at wght 700 matches CoreText advance for advance (H 1494/1494, n 1180/1180), and so does .SF Arabic. No avar2 fonts exist on this system. But every other axis stays at its fvar default: SF opsz=28 (Display), .SF Arabic opsz=28, New York opsz=256 (the extra-large display cut). CoreText instead applies optical size automatically from the point size (New York at 13pt uses opsz=13). The trak table is dropped too, although CoreText applies it per size (SF −12 units at 13pt, +28 at 28pt; New York +120 at 4pt, −36 at 256pt; PingFang +20 at 13pt). Measured: forged New York Bold at 13pt is 300.5 wide versus 338.9 for CoreText at opsz 13 (11% narrower), and forged SF is 282.2 versus 298.9 for the system (5.6% tighter). CFF2 variable fonts (the 9 hidden .SF Indic faces) are rejected at validate.
- **Evidence:** work/engine: forged advances versus fontTools reference versus CoreText at the same axes: SFNS@700 [('H',1494,1494,1494),('n',1180,1180,1180),...]; NewYork@700 [('H',1591,1591,1554),...], a constant −36 that equals trak at 256pt. The trak dump shows New York 'track 0.0: 4pt->120, 12pt->12, ... 256pt->-36' and SFNS 'track 0.0: ... 13pt->-12 ... 28pt->28'. CoreText widths of 'Hamburgefonstiv 0123456789 The quick brown fox' at 13pt: New York forged 300.5, opsz=13 wght=700 338.9, opsz=256 302.8; SF source 298.9, forged 282.2. fvar defaults: SFNS opsz 17–28–96, New York opsz 12–256–256.
- **Recommendation:** In instance_variable(), pin opsz to a text size (for example clamp(13, min, max)) instead of the fvar default, or add an 'optical size: text/display' option. Optionally bake the trak value for the chosen size (for example 13pt, track 0) into hmtx advances. Document that SF/New York are private system fonts that follow size automatically on macOS, and that a forged static instance cannot do that.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** fvar defaults checked with read_faces: SFNS wdth 30-100-150, opsz 17-28-96, GRAD 400-400-1000, wght 1-400-1000; New York opsz 12-256-256. instance_variable (prepare.py:35-41) pins every non-wght axis to its default. CoreText applies optical size automatically: CTFontCopyVariation on NewYork.ttf at 13 pt gives {opsz: 13.0} and at 72 pt {opsz: 72.0} (work/verify-engine/optsz.py). Width per point of a fixed string: New York at 13 pt, CoreText 23.84 vs forged static 20.29 (15% narrower). SFNS at 13 pt, CoreText 22.99 vs forged 21.71 (5.6% tighter, matching the auditor). At 72 pt the forged SF is 7% wider (21.71 vs 20.29). So it is not 'tighter' in general: it is frozen at the opsz-28 design with no trak, which is wrong at every size except about 28 pt.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Pin opsz to a text size clamped to the axis (SF's minimum is 17, so clamp(13) gives 17), or expose a 'Text / Display' optical-size choice. Optionally bake the trak value for that size into the advances. Document that CoreText varies SF and New York continuously with size, and a static forge cannot.

### ENGINE-7: Apple AAT kerning (kern version 1 state tables, kerx) is dropped silently; only format-0 kern pairs are converted

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/kern.py:20`, `fontplayground/engine/kern.py:66`
- **macOS behaviour:** kern_pairs() reads only format-0 subtables. Geeza Pro's 'kern' is version 1.0 with twelve format-1 (contextual state table) subtables, so its Arabic kerning disappears without a warning. The kerx tables (51 faces, 14 of them without GPOS, e.g. Malayalam MN, Helvetica Neue Bold/Italic and Hiragino Sans W8) are dropped as well. The OpenType-style kern v0 in Helvetica Neue Regular (342 pairs) is converted correctly.
- **Evidence:** GeezaPro.ttc kern version 1.0 [(1, None, None, 0) x12]; HelveticaNeue.ttc kern version 0 [(0, 1, False, 342)]. The fixed Helvetica Neue output has a GPOS 'kern' feature with 1 lookup. Inventory: 'kerx no GPOS 14'.
- **Recommendation:** When the source has kern version 1 with subtable format ≠ 0, or has a kerx table, and the part ends up with no GPOS kern, add the report warning 'Apple (AAT) kerning not carried over'. Converting kerx format 0/2 pairs to GPOS PairPos is possible (fontTools reads kerx only as raw data, so this needs a small parser) but is low priority.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** kern.py:24-25 skips any subtable whose format is not 0, and the fontTools subsetter drops subtables without kernTable anyway. Geeza Pro: kern version 1.0 with twelve format-1 subtables, 0 pairs converted. Skia: format 3 x5, 0 pairs. Clarification: most Apple kern v1 tables use format-0 subtables and ARE converted: Helvetica 105 pairs, Times 113, Optima 184, Didot 227, Gill Sans 201, Hoefler Text 2,538. Helvetica Neue kern v0 has 342 pairs, and the forged output has a GPOS 'kern' feature with CoreText width preserved (237.4 vs 237.3). kerx: 51 faces, 14 without GPOS. I could not reproduce the 'Hiragino Sans W8' example: my kerx-without-GPOS families are Helvetica, Helvetica Neue, Malayalam MN and Malayalam Sangam MN.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Add a report warning when a source has kern subtables of format 1, 2 or 3, or a kerx table, and the part ends up without GPOS kern. Converting kern format 2/3 (class kerning) is feasible with a small parser, and is lower priority than ENGINE-2.

### ENGINE-8: Materials are stored by absolute path; PingFang lives under an AssetsV2 hash folder that macOS changes, and from_dict then drops it silently

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:73`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:87`
- **macOS behaviour:** PingFang (the main Chinese UI font) lives at /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/86ba2c91f017a3749571a82f2c6d890ac7ffb2fb.asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc. The hash and the Font8 generation change when Apple updates the font asset or macOS. A saved recipe then points to a missing file, and ForgeSpec.from_dict drops that material without saying so ('if face is None: continue'). Hiragino file names are stored on disk in NFD form ('ヒラギノ角ゴシック W8.ttc' is NFD=True, NFC=False), so comparing paths from different sources can also fail.
- **Evidence:** ls /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/*/AssetData/PingFang.ttc; spec.py:87-89. work/engine/spawn_check.py: unicodedata.is_normalized shows the NFD file name.
- **Recommendation:** Also store the PostScript name (name ID 6) and the family/style in to_dict(). In from_dict(), fall back to matching by PostScript name in the catalog, or resolve it with CoreText (CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize → kCTFontURLAttribute). Tell the user when a material could not be found instead of dropping it silently. Normalise paths to NFC (or compare with os.path.samefile) before using them as keys.
- **Verifier (partially):** Confirmed: PingFang is at /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/86ba2c91….asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc (only a Font8 generation exists here). spec.py:87-89 drops unknown materials, and model.py:687 documents that materials missing from the catalog are dropped on restore. That silent drop is intended and behaves the same on Windows. It only becomes a Mac problem once the catalog scans AssetsV2 (paths.py does not yet). I did not verify the claim that the hash changes with asset updates; it is plausible. The NFD point is speculative: Hiragino names on disk are NFD, but catalog keys and the stored JSON both come from the same scandir strings, so they compare equal. I found no failure from it. I did verify that CoreText can resolve the file by PostScript name: CTFontDescriptorCreateWithNameAndSize('PingFangSC-Regular') gives the file URL above. Caveat: an unknown name silently falls back to a default font.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Store the PostScript name and family/style with each material. On restore, fall back to catalog lookup by PostScript name, then CoreText, and accept the result only if CTFontCopyPostScriptName matches, because unknown names resolve to a fallback font. Tell the user which materials were dropped. Skip the NFD normalisation unless a mismatch is shown.

### ENGINE-9: The forge runs in the GUI process: CJK stages of 17–60 s cannot be cancelled and about 0.9 GB of memory stays with the app

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:39`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:51`, `fontplayground/engine/forge.py:45`
- **macOS behaviour:** CombineWorker is a QThread, and cancel is only checked between stages. The prepare stage for PingFang takes about 17 s, 50 s with synthetic bold, and forever in the ENGINE-3 uharfbuzz case, and cannot be interrupted. Peak memory is 817–940 MB for Latin + PingFang and stays in the app process. On macOS the default multiprocessing start method is 'spawn'. ForgeSpec pickles cleanly (5 KB), a child process forges correctly, and terminate() stops it in the middle of prepare within 0.01 s. tempfile.gettempdir() is the per-user /var/folders/.../T directory, which works fine.
- **Evidence:** forge_one peak_rss_mb: Helvetica Neue + PingFang (fixed) 817, PingFang alone 851, bold 888, baseline with uharfbuzz 940 MB ('peak memory footprint 939820256'). work/engine/spawn_check.py: 'start method: spawn', 'pickled spec bytes: 5071', 'child exit 0 result (1112, 2153) 0.7s', 'terminated mid-prepare, exitcode -15 in 0.01s'.
- **Recommendation:** Run forge() in a multiprocessing 'spawn' child process: send progress back over a Queue, cancel with terminate(), and free the memory when it exits. Guard the entry point with `if __name__ == '__main__'` and call multiprocessing.freeze_support() so py2app/PyInstaller .app bundles work. Keep the QThread only to watch the child.
- **Verifier (partially):** Cancel granularity confirmed: workers.py:50-60 raises only inside the progress callback, which forge() calls between stages, and the forge runs in a QThread (workers.py:39). Peak RSS of 800–950 MB during a CJK forge reproduced (860–956 MB). I could not reproduce 'about 0.9 GB stays with the app'. In-process run (rss.py): RSS was 561 MB after reading the materials, and 487 MB after forge() plus gc. The forge frees its peak. Most of the baseline comes from reading faces: read_faces(PingFang.ttc) alone raises RSS from 33 MB to 511 MB, and a cold full-catalog scan of this Mac leaves 1,363 MB (scanmem.py). That is the catalog dimension, not the engine. Nothing here is macOS-specific beyond 'spawn' being the default start method.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** A 'spawn' child process for forge() (progress over a Queue, terminate() to cancel, freeze_support() for py2app/PyInstaller) is sound and mainly buys prompt cancel and peak isolation. Separately, flag to the catalog owner that the in-process scan retains more than 1 GB on a stock Mac.

### ENGINE-12: Colour and CFF2 fonts are rejected cleanly; Apple bitmap strikes (bdat/bloc/EBDT) are dropped silently

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:12`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:61`
- **macOS behaviour:** Apple Color Emoji (sbix) is rejected at validate: '[validate] Apple Color Emoji Regular: colour fonts are not supported'. .SF Devanagari (CFF2) is rejected: 'CFF2 outlines are not supported'. Fonts with bdat/bloc/EBDT (Geneva, Monaco, Cochin, AppleMyungjo, Courier New, PT Sans) forge successfully using outlines only. The forged font has no emoji, but macOS falls back to Apple Color Emoji on its own.
- **Evidence:** f1 and h6 JSON errors as quoted; f3 Courier New (bdat+EBDT) ok, 2966 glyphs, output without bdat.
- **Recommendation:** No change needed. Optionally add 'bdat','EBDT' to a 'bitmaps ignored' report note. The Picker could explain sbix ('Apple Color Emoji is drawn by macOS automatically').
- **Verifier (partially):** Colour and CFF2 rejection confirmed: read_faces gives 'Apple Color Emoji Regular glyf colour fonts are not supported' and '.SF Devanagari Regular CFF2 CFF2 outlines are not supported'. Refuted: 'bdat/bloc fonts (Geneva, Monaco, Cochin, AppleMyungjo, …) forge successfully'. Geneva, all four Cochin faces and AppleMyungjo crash in prepare with 'error: unpack requires a buffer of 2 bytes' (4 bytes for AppleMyungjo). The traceback ends in fontTools E_B_L_C_.py:494 while the subsetter decompiles Apple's 'bloc'. subset_options().drop_tables includes EBDT/EBLC but not bdat/bloc. Monaco and Courier New do work. Details in ENGINE-M2, so 'no change needed' is wrong.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Add 'bdat', 'bloc' and 'bhed' to subset_options().drop_tables. I tested this: Geneva, Cochin and AppleMyungjo then forge alone and as secondaries. More robust: before subsetting, drop every table outside KEEP_TABLES ∪ {'CFF '}, which also skips the useless morx/feat/fond work. Optionally support CFF2 by drawing the instanced glyphs through the glyph set in cff_to_glyf, although only private .SF Indic fonts use CFF2 here.

### ENGINE-M2: Apple 'bloc'/'bdat' bitmap tables crash the subsetter: Geneva, Cochin (all 4 faces) and AppleMyungjo cannot be forged

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:54`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:70`
- **macOS behaviour:** subset_options() only adds DSIG to fontTools' default drop_tables, which already cover EBDT/EBLC/EBSC but not Apple's 'bdat'/'bloc'. The subsetter tries to decompile 'bloc' through the EBLC reader and crashes on these Apple tables. prepare() fails with '[prepare (Cochin Regular)] error: unpack requires a buffer of 2 bytes', whether the font is main or secondary. Monaco (also bdat/bloc) happens to parse. Geneva and Cochin are also ENGINE-1 cmap fonts, so they need both fixes.
- **Evidence:** Traceback for Geneva.ttf in subset_font: 'error unpack requires a buffer of 2 bytes [ttFont.py:817 __getitem__, ttFont.py:835 _readTable, E_B_L_C_.py:142 decompile, E_B_L_C_.py:494 decompile]'. subset_options().drop_tables = ['JSTF','DSIG','EBDT','EBLC','EBSC','PCLT','LTSH','Feat','Glat','Gloc','Silf','Sill','DSIG']. Sweep with current code: Geneva, Cochin Regular/Bold/Italic/Bold Italic and AppleMyungjo fail in prepare. With drop_tables + ['bdat','bloc','bhed'] (plus the ENGINE-1 cmap fix and M1 OS/2 fix), all six forge both alone and under Avenir Next.
- **Recommendation:** Add 'bdat', 'bloc' and 'bhed' to subset_options().drop_tables. Better: before subsetting, drop every table outside KEEP_TABLES ∪ {'CFF '}, since they are removed by strip_tables() afterwards anyway. That also saves the 'morx/feat/fond NOT subset' work and log noise. Add a regression test that forges a small font containing a malformed 'bloc'.

### ENGINE-M4: 21 Apple families (Geeza Pro, Damascus, Al Bayan, Baghdad, Gujarati MT…) appear under Urdu, Arabic or Hindi names; these names reach the report, the forged name table and the default family names

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:100`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:109`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:182`, `fontplayground/engine/forge.py:27`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:114`
- **macOS behaviour:** _name_rank() ranks any Windows-platform record (non-English ones get rank 1) above the Mac-platform English record, which gets no rank. Many Apple fonts keep their English name only in Mac-platform records (1,0,lang 0). Geeza Pro therefore shows as 'گیزا پرو' (langID 0x420, Urdu), Damascus as 'دمشق', Gujarati MT as 'गुजराती एचटी', and so on. These strings go into MaterialReport names, name ID 0 'Forged … from: گیزا پرو Regular', and smart.default_family_name, which then causes the PostScript-name clash in ENGINE-M3. This is a catalog-dimension bug, but it feeds the engine's naming.
- **Evidence:** GeezaPro.ttc name records: (1, 1,0,0x0,'Geeza Pro'), (1, 3,1,0x420,'گیزا پرو'), (1, 3,1,0x439,'गीज़ा प्रो'), (1, 3,1,0xc01,'جيزة'). read_faces display names: ['گیزا پرو Regular','گیزا پرو Bold',…]. The aat_rows.json scan lists 21 families with non-ASCII names: البيان, التاريخ, النيل, بغداد, بيروت, دمشق, ديوان ثلث, ديوان كوفي, صنعاء, فارسي, فرح, منى, مِصحفي, مِصحفي ذهبي, نديم, نسخ, وسيم, گیزا پرو, इनाईमाथी, गुजराती एचटी, गुरुमुखी एमटी.
- **Recommendation:** In _best_name(), use this order: Windows English (3,x,0x409), then Mac English (1,0,0), then other Windows languages, then other Mac records. The localized names stay available in local_names, as intended. Bump the catalog cache schema so cached names are rebuilt.

### ENGINE-10: Hidden '.'-prefixed Apple system fonts (.SF Arabic, .New York, .SF Hebrew, .Geeza Pro Interface…) are listed and forged

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:182`
- **macOS behaviour:** There are 59 hidden families. CoreText keeps them out of font menus, and their names are private and may change. Some are the best OpenType fonts for their script (.SF Arabic, .SF Hebrew), which makes them tempting to pick. They are also subject to the licence issue in ENGINE-5, and produce default family names such as '.SF Arabic …'.
- **Evidence:** inventory.json: '.SF Arabic' (SFArabic.ttf, fsType 4), '.New York' (NewYork.ttf), '.SF Hebrew'. They appear in the picker lists produced by picker_scan.py, marked [hidden .font].
- **Recommendation:** Hide faces whose family starts with '.' by default, with an 'Show system-private fonts' option. When one is used, strip the leading dot from the default family name.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** No filtering of '.'-prefixed families exists (grep finds none; face.py:182 takes the name as is). My count is 112 faces in 74 dot-prefixed families (the auditor said 59): .SF Arabic/Hebrew/Armenian/Georgian, .SF Compact, .New York, .Geeza Pro Interface/PUA, .CJK Symbols Fallback *, .LastResort, .Keyboard and others. .LastResort and .Keyboard also appear in the ENGINE-1 cmap failures, which is another reason to hide them by default.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same: hide dot-prefixed families by default behind a 'Show system-private fonts' option, and strip the dot from default names. Hide *PUA and .LastResort/.Keyboard unconditionally.

### ENGINE-11: Vertical CJK metrics (vhea/vmtx/VORG) and BASE are dropped while the 'vert' feature is kept

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort M; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:96`
- **macOS behaviour:** PingFang, Hiragino and Apple SD Gothic Neo have vmtx/VORG; the output tables contain only the basic set plus GSUB/GPOS. Vertical text on macOS (Pages, TextEdit, CoreText vertical forms) falls back to default vertical advances. BASE and Apple's trak/meta are dropped too.
- **Evidence:** b_sfns_hiraginosans out_tables: ['GPOS','GSUB','OS/2','cmap','glyf','head','hhea','hmtx','loca','maxp','name','post']. The source PingFang tables include BASE, VORG, vhea, vmtx and trak.
- **Recommendation:** If vertical writing is in scope, keep and merge vhea/vmtx: fontTools.merge supports vmtx, and prepare's scale_upem already scales it. Otherwise document the limitation.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** KEEP_TABLES (prepare.py:19) has no vhea/vmtx/VORG/BASE/meta/trak, and cff_to_glyf deletes VORG on purpose (prepare.py:97-98). The pan-CJK output tables are only the basic set plus GSUB/GPOS. The recommendation is oversimplified. fontTools.merge has a mergeMap for vhea/vmtx (merge/tables.py:90,203), but a table missing from some parts reaches the merge functions as NotImplemented. That is exactly the TypeError seen for fonts missing OS/2 (ENGINE-M1). A Latin part without vmtx would break the merge the same way. The 'meta' table (dlng/slng language designation, read by CoreText) is dropped too.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** If vertical text is in scope, synthesize vhea/vmtx for every part that lacks them (advance = ascender − descender, TSB = ascender − yMax) before merging, keep vmtx/vhea from the CJK parts, and let scale_upem scale them. There is no need to keep VORG for glyf output. Otherwise document the limitation.

#### Data points

- Font inventory: 409 font files and 889 faces across /System/Library/Fonts (including Supplemental), /Library/Fonts, ~/Library/Fonts and AssetsV2 Font8 (29 files). 1 file unreadable (NISC18030.ttf, no head table). Outlines: 692 glyf, 188 CFF, 9 CFF2. 59 variable faces. 11 unsupported (9 CFF2, 2 sbix).
- AAT: 263 faces have morx; 189 faces (84 families) have morx but no GSUB; 51 have kerx (14 without GPOS); 75 have trak.
- Faces whose cmap fontTools.merge drops entirely: 25 supported faces in 13 families, including all Helvetica Neue faces with only a (0,1) cmap.
- fsType across 889 faces: 0 → 454, 4 (preview&print) → 243, 8 (editable) → 168, restricted → 17, missing → 7.
- a) Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC, current code, shared venv (no uharfbuzz): FAILS at verify after 22.4 s ('coverage mismatch: 2083 missing'), peak RSS 890 MB.
- a) Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC, current code, venv with uharfbuzz: no result; killed at 1200 s while still preparing PingFang (peak 940 MB). PingFang alone was also killed at 600 s.
- a) Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC with the cmap + locl-alias fixes: OK in 19.4 s, 39,252 glyphs, 37,350 characters, 10.9 MB, RSS 817 MB. README reference: about 15 s on Windows.
- a3) PingFang SC alone: current code 22.7 s, 48,124 glyphs; fixed 18.5 s, 38,054 glyphs (the locl aliasing adds about 10k glyphs).
- a2) Helvetica Neue Bold + PingFang SC Regular at weight 700 (synthetic +300): current code FAILS with PathOpsError after 23 s; with the per-glyph fallback OK in 59.8 s, 24.4 MB.
- b) SF System Font (variable) + Hiragino Sans W3: OK in 19.9 s, 21,354 glyphs, 8.9 MB, RSS 445 MB.
- b2) SF at 700 + Hiragino W6 (synthetic +100 on 13k glyphs): OK in 42.3 s, 14.3 MB.
- c) Avenir Next + Apple SD Gothic Neo: OK in 6.6 s, 18,747 glyphs, 4.3 MB, RSS 337 MB.
- d) Avenir Next + Geeza Pro 0.36 s; + Devanagari MT 0.23 s; + Thonburi 0.26 s; + Malayalam MN 0.27 s; + Mshtakan and + Raanana about 0.25 s. All build, but the script text is broken (AAT tables dropped).
- d) SF + .SF Arabic: OK in 4.0 s. Avenir Next + Kohinoor Devanagari: OK in 0.64 s. Avenir Next + Damascus: OK in 0.52 s. Shaping is identical to the source in all three.
- CoreText shaping of the forged Geeza Pro: 13 of 13 glyphs are plain unjoined forms (the source has 1 of 13). Forged Devanagari MT: 22 plain glyphs versus 15 shaped in the source.
- h) New York at 700: OK in 0.54 s, but opsz is pinned at 256; 11% narrower at 13pt than CoreText's automatic optical size. SF at 13pt is 5.6% tighter than the system (trak dropped).
- Variable instancing accuracy: SF at wght 700 advances equal CoreText's (±1 unit) for H, O, n, o, a, e, g, x, 0.
- Synthetic bold cost: about 24–32 s for 36.7k PingFang glyphs (skia-pathops). 2 glyphs fail at +300 and 0 fail at +100 on the Semibold face.
- Engine tests (48, from test_forge, test_prepare, test_spec, test_planner, test_synth_bold, test_scripts) pass on the patched copy work/engine/repo_fixed.

## Install / update / uninstall on macOS (`INSTALL-*`)

On macOS today "Install for me" does not work at all. `install.is_supported()` is Windows-only, so the action bar's primary button falls back to "Save…". Every layer under it is Windows-specific: the registry, gdi32, WM_FONTCHANGE, %LOCALAPPDATA% and %WINDIR% paths, and "Windows already has…" wording. On macOS the name-clash check would let through system fonts such as Helvetica and PingFang: I ran it here and it returned ("none","") for both. The native design I recommend, and prototyped, copies the file into ~/Library/Fonts, which is what Font Book's "Current User" install and Homebrew font casks do. fontd then activates the font and posts kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification, so there is nothing to broadcast. Uninstall moves the file to the Trash. CoreText (via pyobjc) answers the name-clash questions that the file-scan catalog cannot: AssetsV2 and FontServices fonts, fonts offered for download, native/localized family names, and PostScript names. That last one matters a lot on macOS because every CJK-named forged font currently gets the same PostScript name, "Forged-Regular". The prototype MacInstaller works with injectable scope and folder, so tests use kCTFontManagerScopeProcess and a temp folder. With the `build.py` changes wired in, its 7 tests pass and the full suite passes (346 passed; only the 2 existing `test_paths` failures remain). Two things are still unverified because they need the real ~/Library/Fonts: fontd activation latency, and whether fontd re-reads a file replaced at the same path. An opt-in probe script for both is provided.

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| INSTALL-1 | blocker | confirmed | "Install for me" is Windows-only: on macOS the bar silently degrades to Save… |
| INSTALL-2 | major | confirmed | No installer abstraction: the UI is bound to a Windows module with an implicit duck-typed contract |
| INSTALL-3 | major | partially | build.conflict() has Windows-only paths and wording, so on macOS a font named after a system font is not blocked |
| INSTALL-4 | major | confirmed | The catalog-based check cannot see many fonts macOS has; use CoreText to find names system-wide |
| INSTALL-5 | major | partially | PostScript-name collisions: every CJK-named forged font is 'Forged-Regular', and duplicates coexist silently |
| INSTALL-7 | major | confirmed | Mechanism: copy into ~/Library/Fonts; do not rely on a persistent CTFontManager registration |
| INSTALL-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | Recommended MacInstaller deletes the user's own non-forged fonts that share the full name (installed_path ignores is_forged) |
| INSTALL-6 | minor | partially | There is no WM_FONTCHANGE equivalent to send; the app's own font list does not refresh after an install |
| INSTALL-8 | minor | confirmed | Update overwrites in place: the Windows 'locked, so use <stem>-n' fallback never runs on macOS |
| INSTALL-9 | minor | confirmed | Uninstall on macOS should move the file to the Trash like Font Book; replacing during Update should just delete |
| INSTALL-10 | minor | partially | Tracking 'fonts this app installed' without a registry: scan the user font folder by name ID 4 plus FORGED_NOTICE |
| INSTALL-12 | minor | confirmed | test_install.py is Windows-only; restructure it for macOS with CoreText process scope and a temp folder |
| INSTALL-14 | minor | confirmed | Dependencies and distribution: pyobjc on macOS only; an unsandboxed Developer-ID build is needed to write ~/Library/Fonts |
| INSTALL-M2 | minor | (verifier-found) | Update removes the old installed copy before the new one is in place: a failed rename leaves the user with neither |
| INSTALL-M3 | minor | (verifier-found) | No check that macOS can actually load the forged file before it is copied into ~/Library/Fonts (Font Book validates; a plain copy does not) |
| INSTALL-11 | polish | confirmed | _is_under compares paths case-sensitively and without resolving symlinks on macOS |
| INSTALL-13 | polish | confirmed | User-facing strings and 'Show file' are Windows-flavoured; use macOS wording and Show in Finder |

### INSTALL-1: "Install for me" is Windows-only: on macOS the bar silently degrades to Save…

- **Severity:** blocker (auditor: blocker); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:32`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:36`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:92`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:140`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:155`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:282`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:122`, `README.md:7`, `README.md:42`
- **macOS behaviour:** `is_supported()` returns `sys.platform.startswith("win")`, so `BuildController.can_install()` is False. The primary button reads "Save…", the idle text is "Saves a .ttf file you can install yourself.", and Install, Update installed font and Uninstall do not exist. Calling the module directly raises `InstallNotSupported`. `user_fonts_dir()` returns ~/AppData/Local/Microsoft/Windows/Fonts.
- **Evidence:** exp/e9_bar.py on the original clone printed: `installer: fontplayground.ui.install | can_install: False | primary: Save… | save visible: False`. With the prototype: `installer: MacInstaller | can_install: True | primary: Install`. exp/e6.out: `install.is_supported(): False` and `install.user_fonts_dir(): ~/AppData/Local/Microsoft/Windows/Fonts`. README.md:42 lists 'installing on macOS/Linux' under not supported.
- **Recommendation:** Add a MacInstaller. The prototype is at work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py and passes its tests. Mapping from the Windows functions:
- `is_supported()` returns `sys.platform == 'darwin'`.
- `user_fonts_dir()` returns `~/Library/Fonts`.
- `full_name_of()` and `is_forged()` are shared and unchanged.
- `registry_value_name`, `FONTS_KEY` and `broadcast_font_change` stay Windows-only. There is nothing to broadcast on macOS: fontd posts kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification itself.
- `installed_path(full)` scans ~/Library/Fonts (not recursive; .ttf/.otf/.ttc/.otc; dotfiles skipped) for a name ID 4 that matches after casefold. It may raise only `InstallNotSupported` or `OSError`.
- `install_font_for_user(path, full)`:
  1. Check the source; a missing file raises FileNotFoundError.
  2. `previous = installed_path(full)`.
  3. Target is ~/Library/Fonts/<staged name>. Use '<stem>-<n>' only when a file that is not ours already holds that name; do not overwrite it.
  4. Copy to ~/Library/Caches/FontPlayground/<uuid>-<name> and fsync.
  5. If `previous` is a different file, unlink it.
  6. `os.replace(tmp, dest)`, then return `dest`.
- `uninstall_font_for_user(full)`: find the file with `installed_path`. On an explicit Uninstall, move it to the Trash (`NSFileManager.trashItemAtURL_resultingItemURL_error_`). When replacing it during Update, unlink it. Return True, or False when nothing was found.
- Tests only: CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs / UnregisterFontURLs with kCTFontManagerScopeProcess. Tolerate error 105 on register and 201 on unregister.
- New methods: `os_name = 'macOS'`, `system_font_dirs()`, `find_family()`, `find_postscript()` (see INSTALL-4).

Errors and what the user sees:
- PermissionError (for example a sandboxed build) shows "Couldn't install the font: [Errno 13] Permission denied: …".
- EXDEV (staging folder on another volume) is an OSError, and the install fails cleanly.
- 'No free file name' is an OSError.

Also update README (Install section, and 'Windows-only' on line 7).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** I ran work/verify-install/v1_conflict.py against the unmodified clone. It printed `is_supported: False`, `user_fonts_dir: ~/AppData/Local/Microsoft/Windows/Fonts`, `installed_path raises: InstallNotSupported`, `can_install: False`, `primary: Save… | status: Saves a .ttf file you can install yourself.` In the code, install.py:32-33 and action_bar.py:282-283 turn the button into Save…, and README.md:7 and :42 say Install for me is Windows-only. I ran the prototype's tests/test_install_macos.py together with test_build.py (`30 passed in 1.93s`). The real ~/Library/Fonts still holds 10 files. "Blocker" is right for the feature: it is missing entirely on macOS, even though the app falls back to Save on purpose.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** The mapping is fine overall, but the prototype it points to has two defects that must be fixed before anyone copies it. (a) `installed_path()` matches name ID 4 only and never calls is_forged, so install and uninstall delete non-forged fonts the user owns (see INSTALL-M1). (b) The old copy is removed before the new one is renamed into place (see INSTALL-M2). Do not add a `to_trash` keyword to the shared interface. Keep `uninstall_font_for_user(full)` exactly as it is and let the macOS backend always use the Trash. That leaves WindowsInstaller and FakeInstaller untouched (see INSTALL-9).

### INSTALL-2: No installer abstraction: the UI is bound to a Windows module with an implicit duck-typed contract

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:82`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:171`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:254`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:89`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:117`, `tests/fakes.py:11`
- **macOS behaviour:** `BuildController(installer=install)` defaults to the Windows module, and `MainWindow(installer=install)` passes the module itself. `build.py:173` catches `install.InstallNotSupported` and `OSError` from `installed_path` and nothing else. A CoreText-backed lookup that raised `objc.error` would therefore escape into the Install click handler. FakeInstaller is the only other implementation of the contract.
- **Evidence:** The contract the UI actually uses (read from build.py, action_bar.py, app.py, fakes.py):
- `is_supported() -> bool`: any exception counts as False (build.py:122-127).
- `user_fonts_dir() -> Path`: any exception counts as None (build.py:155-158).
- `is_forged(path) -> bool`.
- `installed_path(full) -> Path|None`: only `InstallNotSupported` and `OSError` are tolerated (build.py:171-174).
- `install_font_for_user(staged_path, full) -> Path`: any exception becomes 'Couldn't install the font: {e}'. The staged file is deleted right after the call (build.py:263-273), so a backend must copy it, not register it in place.
- `uninstall_font_for_user(full) -> bool`: False gives 'That font was no longer installed.'; an exception gives REMOVE_FAILED_TEXT.

Conflict logic (build.py:142-179):
- A name this controller installed itself is never a conflict.
- Block: a catalog face with the same family (English or native name) under the system folder, or under the user folder but not forged; or a file registered under the full name that is not forged.
- Replace (ask first): a forged face in the user folder with the same family and style, or a forged registered file.
- Files that no longer exist never count.

'Installed earlier by us' means the registry value `"<Family Style> (TrueType)"` plus name ID 0 starting with `"Forged with Font Playground"` (merge.py:47,55).

Update (build.py:274-279): inst …
- **Recommendation:** Create a package `fontplayground/install/`:
- `__init__.py`: `get_installer(platform=sys.platform)`, `InstallNotSupported`, the `ExistingFont` dataclass, `full_name_of`, `is_forged`, and an `Installer` Protocol with: `os_name`; `is_supported()`; `user_fonts_dir()`; `system_font_dirs()`; `is_forged()`; `installed_path()`; `install_font_for_user()`; `uninstall_font_for_user(full, *, to_trash=True)`; and the optional `find_family()` / `find_postscript()`.
- `windows.py`: today's code, unchanged.
- `macos.py`: MacInstaller.
- `linux.py`: LinuxInstaller. Uses $XDG_DATA_HOME/fonts (default ~/.local/share/fonts), the same file-scan tracking as macOS, `fc-cache -f <dir>` after each change (a missing fc-cache is not fatal), `find_family` via `fc-list ':family=<name>' family style file` (fontconfig matches localized names), and system dirs /usr/share/fonts and /usr/local/share/fonts.

Keep `fontplayground.ui.install` as a thin shim so imports keep working. `MainWindow` should default to `get_installer()`. Every backend must turn platform errors into `OSError`. As a belt-and-braces measure, `conflict()` should catch `Exception` around installer lookups, as `can_install()` already does.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** I read the code. build.py:82 defaults to `installer=install`, app.py:89 and :117 pass the module, and build.py:171-174 catches only `(install.InstallNotSupported, OSError)`. objc.error is not an OSError, so a CoreText failure would escape through `_on_primary`. FakeInstaller (fakes.py:11-51) is the only other implementation. The contract description (staged file deleted after the call at build.py:268-273; Update removes the old name only after the new install at :274-279) matches the code. The auditor's preview evidence (e5) proved nothing, because Qt did not list the registered font in that run. I re-tested properly in v5_preview_collision.py: I registered the old copy before QGuiApplication existed, so Qt lists it (`Qt lists 'Zq Mix' before app font: True`), and then loaded the new build the way make_font does. Under both offscreen and cocoa, `draws 'x' (only NEW has it): True`. So the conclusion stands: the preview does not depend on install.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** The package split is fine. A smaller change also works: keep `fontplayground/ui/install.py` as the shared helpers plus `get_installer()`, and add `install_windows.py` and `install_macos.py` next to it. Whichever layout you pick, `conflict()` should catch `Exception` around every installer lookup, as `can_install()` already does, and every backend should turn platform errors into OSError.

### INSTALL-3: build.conflict() has Windows-only paths and wording, so on macOS a font named after a system font is not blocked

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:38`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:60`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:87`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:155`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:164`
- **macOS behaviour:** `system_fonts_dir()` returns `Path(os.environ.get('WINDIR', r'C:\Windows')) / 'Fonts'`. On macOS that is the relative path 'C:\Windows/Fonts', which resolves under the current working directory. `_is_under(face.path, windows_fonts_dir)` is therefore never true. `installer.user_fonts_dir()` points at ~/AppData/…, so user fonts are never classified either. A recipe named 'Helvetica' gets ('none','') even though /System/Library/Fonts/Helvetica.ttc is in the catalog. Installing it would shadow system Helvetica: in exp/e2b.out, a process-scope font named Helvetica made CTFontCreateWithName('Helvetica') resolve to that file. The block message also says 'Windows'.
- **Evidence:** exp/e6.out, run with the real install module and a catalog of Helvetica.ttc + Geneva:
- `build.system_fonts_dir(): C:\Windows/Fonts -> abspath …/work/install/exp/C:\Windows/Fonts`
- `conflict() for family 'Helvetica': ('none', '')`, and the same for 'PingFang SC', '苹方-简', 'Arial Unicode MS' and 'Songti SC'.

exp/e2b.out section 3: `register: []  'Helvetica' lookup: ('Helvetica-Regular', 'd8b482-helv.ttf')`.
- **Recommendation:** Replace `windows_fonts_dir` / `system_fonts_dir()` with `installer.system_font_dirs()`. For macOS: /System/Library/Fonts, /System/Library/AssetsV2, /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework and /Library/Fonts. Keep the constructor argument for tests. Word the block message per platform: `SYSTEM_HAS_TEXT = '{os} already has a font called “{name}” — choose another name.'` with os taken from `installer.os_name`. Add a different message for the local domain (/Library/Fonts): 'A font called “{name}” is already installed for everyone on this Mac…'. Then add the CoreText-wide check from INSTALL-4. After these changes the prototype's `test_controller_blocks_names_macos_already_has` passes for 'Helvetica', 'helvetica', '苹方-简' and 'PingFang SC'.
- **Verifier (partially):** The core claim holds. v1_conflict.py printed `system_fonts_dir: C:\Windows/Fonts abs: <cwd>/C:\Windows/Fonts`, and conflict() returned ('none','') for Helvetica, helvetica, Geneva, 'PingFang SC' and '苹方-简', even with Helvetica.ttc and Geneva.ttf in the catalog. The "shadow system Helvetica" claim is overstated. v2_shadow.py showed that after a process-scope registration of a forged 'Helvetica Regular', `CTFontCreateWithName('Helvetica')` does return the forged file. But `NSFont.fontWithName_size_('Helvetica')` still gives system Helvetica, and family-descriptor matching ('Helvetica' / 'Helvetica','Regular') still resolves to Helvetica.ttc. What happens in the user domain (a file in ~/Library/Fonts) is untested. The concrete harm is a duplicate family in font menus plus ambiguous lookups by name, not a system-wide takeover. It is still major, because it is the macOS form of the Windows 'block' rule.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Adding AssetsV2 and FontServices to `system_font_dirs()` does nothing on its own, because paths.py does not scan those folders, so no catalog face ever lives there. Either fix paths.py (another dimension) or rely on the CoreText lookup from INSTALL-4. Per-OS wording through `installer.os_name` is fine.

### INSTALL-4: The catalog-based check cannot see many fonts macOS has; use CoreText to find names system-wide

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:24`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:160`
- **macOS behaviour:** `conflict()` only looks at scanned catalog faces. On macOS the scan covers /System/Library/Fonts, /Library/Fonts and ~/Library/Fonts. It misses 93 AssetsV2 faces and 24 FontServices faces, which is 43 families including PingFang SC/TC/HK, Kaiti, YuGothic and Toppan. It also misses fonts activated by font managers (Adobe Fonts and similar), and 191 Apple families that are offered for download but not installed; downloading one later would collide. CoreText answers all of these in under a millisecond, is case-insensitive, and matches localized names.
- **Evidence:** exp/e1.out:
- `descriptors (faces) CoreText knows: 624`
- by location: 492 /System/Library/Fonts, 93 OUTSIDE:/System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8, 24 OUTSIDE:/System/Library/PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework, 15 ~/Library/Fonts
- `families only reachable outside the scanned dirs: 43`
- `PostScript names backed by >1 file: 24` (PingFang in both AssetsV2 and FontServices)

exp/e1b.out:
- `'苹方-简' 0.2 ms -> 1 faces [('PingFang SC', '/System/Library/AssetsV2/…/PingFang.ttc')]`
- `'helvetica'` matches Helvetica.ttc
- `downloadable descriptors: 1170 families: 332`

exp/e8.out:
- `downloadable families: 332 not currently installed: 191`
- full snapshot of 624 faces: 21 ms; all PostScript names (600): 2 ms
- display-name lookup '苹方-简 常规体' → PingFangSC-Regular
- **Recommendation:** Add `MacInstaller.find_family(family)` and `find_postscript(ps)`:
- Use `CTFontDescriptorCreateMatchingFontDescriptors(CTFontDescriptorCreateWithAttributes({kCTFontFamilyNameAttribute: family}), {kCTFontFamilyNameAttribute})`, plus a second query that adds `kCTFontDownloadableAttribute: True`.
- For PostScript names, match on `kCTFontNameAttribute` as the mandatory key.
- Read `kCTFontURLAttribute`, family, style and PostScript name from each descriptor.
- Classify the realpath: under fonts_dir (casefold) is user; `/System/Library/` or `/Library/Apple/` is system; `/Library/Fonts/` is local; no URL but downloadable is downloadable; anything else is other.

In `conflict()`:
- system, local or downloadable: block.
- user and not forged, or other: block with YOU_HAVE_TEXT.
- user and forged with the same style: replace.

The catalog check stays for fonts from 'Add folder…'. This is implemented in the prototype build.py `_system_wide_conflict`, and `test_system_wide_lookups_see_what_the_catalog_cannot` passes.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** v3_lookup.py found 624 faces in total: 492 in /System/Library/Fonts, 93 in AssetsV2 Font8, 24 in FontServices.framework, and 15 in ~/Library/Fonts. 43 families exist only outside the scanned folders (Baoli, Kaiti, Klee, Lantinghei…). A '苹方-简' lookup took 4.7 ms and found 6 PingFang SC faces in AssetsV2; 'pingfang sc', '宋体-简' → Songti SC, and 'ヒラギノ角ゴシック' → Hiragino Sans all matched. For downloadables: 1170 descriptors, 332 families, 191 not installed (114 ms). I also tested the no-new-dependency route (v4_qtdb.py): `QFontDatabase.hasFamily('PingFang SC')` and 'Kaiti SC' are True, so Qt already sees AssetsV2 English names case-insensitively. But `hasFamily('苹方-简')` and `hasFamily('宋体-简')` are False, and Qt has no PostScript-name or downloadable lookup. That justifies CoreText.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Keep the CoreText design. If pyobjc cannot be imported, fall back to `QFontDatabase.hasFamily(family)` for English names (verified to cover AssetsV2) rather than skipping the system-wide check. Blocking the 191 families that are offered for download but not installed is a product decision; a warning is enough there.

### INSTALL-5: PostScript-name collisions: every CJK-named forged font is 'Forged-Regular', and duplicates coexist silently

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:32`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:50`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:142`
- **macOS behaviour:** `postscript_name()` removes every non-ASCII character. '我的字体' and '你的字体' both become 'Forged-Regular'. Mixed names lose their CJK part too: 'Noto 我的' becomes 'Noto-Regular'. On macOS the PostScript name identifies a font: CTFontCreateWithName/NSFont, and Pages, Keynote and TextEdit documents, all refer to fonts by it. CoreText accepts a second file with the same PostScript name without any error, and lookups by that name then go to the newest one. So when a user installs two CJK-named forged fonts, documents made with the first one render in the second. Two files for one family (an old and a new copy) are also both listed with no warning, which is why Update must never leave the old file behind. `conflict()` never checks PostScript names.
- **Evidence:** exp/e2d.out:
- both files report PostScript name `('Forged-Regular', '我的字体')` / `('Forged-Regular', '你的字体')`
- after both are registered: `'Forged-Regular' -> ('Forged-Regular', '7f5b56-cjk2.ttf')`
- `a document saved with font 'Forged-Regular' now renders with family: 你的字体`

exp/e2b.out section 2:
- `register old: []  register new: []`
- `family files: ['d8b482-new.ttf', 'd8b482-old.ttf']  PS lookup: (…, 'd8b482-new.ttf')`
- **Recommendation:** Engine (the owner of merge.py): when the family is not pure ASCII, build a unique, stable ASCII PostScript name. For example `f"{ascii_part or 'Forged'}{sha1(family.encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}-{style_ascii}"[:63]`, so it is the same for the same family and different across families.

Installer: in `conflict()`, call `find_postscript(postscript_name(family, style))` and block anything that is not our own forged file in the user folder: 'Another installed font already uses the internal name “{ps}” — choose another name.' (prototype `INTERNAL_NAME_TEXT`).

Install: make sure there is only ever one file per full name. The prototype removes a previous copy even when it has another file name; `test_an_earlier_copy_under_another_file_name_is_replaced_not_duplicated` passes.
- **Verifier (partially):** The collision is confirmed: postscript_name('我的字体') == postscript_name('你的字体') == 'Forged-Regular', and 'Noto 我的' → 'Noto-Regular' (merge.py:32-36). Both register without an error, and a lookup by that PostScript name then returns only one of them. The claim that lookups 'go to the newest one' is wrong. In v2b_ps.py the same registration order gave different winners between runs (xy→乙, yx→乙, xy→甲, yx→甲; NSFont agreed with CTFontCreateWithName each time). Which font wins is arbitrary, which is worse. More importantly, the prototype's installer-side guard misses the main case this finding describes. v10_ps_own.py installed forged '甲字体' (PS Forged-Regular) with MacInstaller, and conflict() for the different family '乙字体' returned ('none',''). `_system_wide_conflict` exempts any PostScript match that is 'user + forged', so two of the app's own CJK fonts are never caught. The engine's style fallback collides as well: any non-ASCII style becomes 'Regular', so '粗体' and '细体' of one family share a PostScript name.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Engine (cross-platform, and required): derive non-ASCII parts from a stable hash of the family and of the style separately, for example `f"{fam_ascii or 'F'}{sha1(family)[:8]}-{sty_ascii or 'S'}{sha1(style)[:4] if style is not ASCII else ''}"[:63]`. Keep it deterministic so Updates keep the same PostScript name. Installer: in conflict(), a PostScript match should block, or ask, whenever the matching file's full name differs from the one being installed, including the app's own forged files ('Your font “甲字体 Regular” already uses this internal name'). Only the same full name counts as 'replace'.

### INSTALL-7: Mechanism: copy into ~/Library/Fonts; do not rely on a persistent CTFontManager registration

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:85`
- **macOS behaviour:** There are three options. (1) Copy into ~/Library/Fonts: this is Font Book's default 'Current User' location and what Homebrew's font stanza does, and fontd activates the file. (2) CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs with kCTFontManagerScopePersistent: fontd keeps a registration that points at the file wherever it is. (3) Both. The header and docs show: kCTFontManagerScopeUser is simply the old name for Persistent (both = 2) and is not deprecated. CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURL (singular) is not deprecated; the plural RegisterFontsForURLs was deprecated in 10.15 in favour of the block-based CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs. Registered files 'shouldn't be moved or renamed'. The man page for `atsutil databases -removeUser` (still shipped on macOS 27) warns that resetting the databases loses 'fonts activated outside the standard font directories', while fonts in the standard folders come back. The com.apple.developer.user-fonts entitlement is iOS-only, so an unsandboxed Mac app needs no entitlement.
- **Evidence:** - CTFontManager.h in MacOSX27.0.sdk: `kCTFontManagerScopePersistent … = 2`, `kCTFontManagerScopeUser … = 2`; 'Persistent: The font is available to all processes for the current user session and will be available in subsequent sessions unless unregistered'; 'Once fonts have been registered from a file, it shouldn't be moved or renamed.'; `CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURLs … CT_DEPRECATED_WITH_REPLACEMENT("CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs", macos(10.6, 10.15))`; `kCTFontRegistrationUserInfoAttribute … API_UNAVAILABLE(macos…)`.
- `man atsutil`: 'Removing databases may cause the loss of font registration state: fonts activated outside the standard font directories…'
- Apple doc JSON (developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/documentation/xcode/configuring-custom-fonts.json): 'The Fonts capability is only available to use with iOS apps that target iOS 13 and later.'
- Font Book settings (https://support.apple.com/guide/font-book/change-font-book-settings-fntbk1004/mac): Current User = ~/Library/Fonts.
- https://docs.brew.sh/Cask-Cookbook: font stanza 'moved into the ~/Library/Fonts folder'.

Only the header and man text were read locally. No persistent registration was run, per the safety rules.
- **Recommendation:** Use option 1: copy into ~/Library/Fonts with no CTFontManager call in production. Do not register fonts persistently from Application Support: that is invisible to the user, lost on a database reset, and left behind if the app is deleted. Use kCTFontManagerScopeProcess only in tests. Consider option 3 (also call RegisterFontURLs(persistent) on the ~/Library/Fonts copy and treat 105 AlreadyRegistered as success) only if the opt-in probe (exp/manual_fontd_probe.py) shows fontd activation is too slow. Its double-registration behaviour is not known. Do not offer an 'All Users' (/Library/Fonts) install. /Library/Fonts is group-admin writable here (`drwxrwxr-t root admin`), but it affects every account and needs an admin on standard accounts.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** MacOSX27.0 SDK CTFontManager.h:134-145: Persistent = 2 and User = 2, and neither carries a deprecation macro. :171-174: `CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURL` is CT_AVAILABLE(macos(10.6)) and not deprecated. :256: the plural RegisterFontsForURLs is deprecated in favour of CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs (macos 10.15). The 'shouldn't be moved or renamed' text is at :160/:242/:288, and :153 marks kCTFontRegistrationUserInfoAttribute API_UNAVAILABLE(macos). `man atsutil` line 23 reads 'Removing databases may cause the loss of font registration state: fonts activated outside the standard font directories'. The saved Apple doc (configuring-custom-fonts) says 'The Fonts capability is only available to use with iOS apps that target iOS 13 and later.' `ls -ld /Library/Fonts` shows `drwxrwxr-t root admin`. The auditor honestly marked this not empirical (no persistent registration was run).
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Copying into ~/Library/Fonts is the right choice. For test-only registration, the singular synchronous `CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURL(url, kCTFontManagerScopeProcess, None)` / `CTFontManagerUnregisterFontsForURL` is simpler and deterministic. The block API's handler 'may be called multiple times' (header :313), and the prototype's `_ct_errors` assumes it has already run by the time the call returns.

### INSTALL-M1: Recommended MacInstaller deletes the user's own non-forged fonts that share the full name (installed_path ignores is_forged)

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:72`, `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:105`, `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:121`, `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:129`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:102`
- **macOS behaviour:** `installed_path(full)` returns any file in ~/Library/Fonts whose name ID 4 matches the full name, forged or not. `install_font_for_user` then permanently unlinks that 'previous' file (`_remove(previous, to_trash=False)`), and `uninstall_font_for_user` trashes or deletes it. The UI's conflict() usually blocks this first. It does not when the controller already installed that name in this session: conflict() returns NO_CONFLICT early at build.py:153, and Update then installs straight away. So if the user adds a same-named commercial font with Font Book during a session and then clicks Update, it is deleted. The installer itself has no guard at all.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-install/v6_proto_dataloss.py (temp folder, process scope, no Trash):
`theirs forged? False | installed_path('Victim Regular') -> …/Fonts/their-font.ttf`
`installed at Victim-Regular.ttf | user's own file still exists? False`
`uninstall_font_for_user('Other Regular') on a non-forged font -> True | exists? False`
- **Recommendation:** Make 'ours' mean exactly what INSTALL-10 defines: `installed_path()` returns only files where the name ID 4 matches AND `is_forged(p)` is true. Give conflict() a separate read-only lookup (`files_named(full)`) so it can still show YOU_HAVE_TEXT for foreign files. In install_font_for_user, never unlink or trash a file that is not forged; if a foreign file holds the target name, pick '<stem>-n'. Add a regression test that puts a non-forged same-named font in the temp fonts folder and asserts it survives both install and uninstall.

### INSTALL-6: There is no WM_FONTCHANGE equivalent to send; the app's own font list does not refresh after an install

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:140`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:169`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:94`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:37`
- **macOS behaviour:** Other apps are notified by macOS itself. fontd watches ~/Library/Fonts and posts kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification: on the distributed center for session and persistent scope, and on the local center for process scope. Many Cocoa apps update their font menus as they run; apps such as Office and Adobe need to be reopened. Inside this app, Qt did not update after a runtime registration under either platform plugin. In the cocoa test no NSFontSetChangedNotification arrived, which is the notification QtGui imports. So the new font appears in the picker only after 'Rescan fonts'. The `_SYSTEM` cache in fonts.py would also be stale.
- **Evidence:** - exp/e2b.out: process-scope register causes `notifications: [('CTFontManagerFontChangedNotification', 4.6)]` on the local center and nothing on the distributed center, matching the CTFontManager.h comment ('register … with the distributed notification center for changes in session or persistent scopes and with the local notification center for changes in process scope').
- exp/e5.out (offscreen): `fontDatabaseChanged fired: 0 | Qt lists it: False`.
- exp/e5b.out (cocoa, background, activation policy 2): `NSFontSetChangedNotification seen: 0 | fontDatabaseChanged fired: 0 | Qt lists it: False`.
- `nm -u QtGui` shows the imports `_NSFontSetChangedNotification` and `_CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames`, and does not import kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChanged.
- Firefox listens for the same notification on the local center: https://searchfox.org/mozilla-central/source/gfx/thebes/CoreTextFontList.cpp
- **Recommendation:** MacInstaller broadcasts nothing; document that in place of `broadcast_font_change`. In the app: after a successful install or uninstall, call `start_scan(use_cache=True)` so the picker and catalog include the change, and clear `fonts._SYSTEM`. Also connect `QGuiApplication.fontDatabaseChanged` to the same rescan, which covers fonts installed elsewhere, for example with Font Book. Change INSTALLED_DETAIL to 'Choose it in any app's font list — apps that were already open may need to be reopened.' If a real-folder test shows fontd is slow, add an optional wait: poll `CTFontDescriptorCreateMatchingFontDescriptors` for the PostScript name with URL == dest for up to about 2 s, and say 'Installed — activating…' until it appears.
- **Verifier (partially):** `nm -u QtGui` confirms the imports `_NSFontSetChangedNotification`, `_CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL/Data` and `_CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames`, and the binary contains the string 'Fonts have changed'. The auditor's Qt experiments used process-scope registration, which does not represent a ~/Library/Fonts install. NSFont.h:140 says NSFontSetChangedNotification 'is posted when the available font set is modified as a result of activation/deactivation', which is what fontd does. v7_repopulate.py posted that notification in-process under the cocoa plugin: `fontDatabaseChanged fired: 1`, and the application font (the preview's build) survived (`draws x: True` before and after). So a running Qt app most likely does see real installs. The in-app picker/catalog is a separate file scan that is not refreshed after install on Windows either, so this is parity, not a macOS regression. The recommendation to 'connect QGuiApplication.fontDatabaseChanged to the same rescan' is harmful. v8_appfont_signal.py shows `fontDatabaseChanged` fires once per `addApplicationFont` (3 adds → 3 signals) and per `removeApplicationFont` (→4), under both offscreen and cocoa. fonts.py:26/42 call these for every picker face and every preview, so the hook would start a rescan loop.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** After this app's own install or uninstall, update the catalog incrementally (read_faces(dest) → model catalog; drop the removed path; clear the fonts._SYSTEM entry) instead of running a full rescan. For fonts installed elsewhere, observe `kCTFontManagerRegisteredFontsChangedNotification` on NSDistributedNotificationCenter via pyobjc, or NSFontSetChangedNotification, which Qt does not emit for app fonts. Or rescan with the cache when the app becomes active, if the modification times of ~/Library/Fonts or /Library/Fonts changed. Never hook fontDatabaseChanged without debouncing it and ignoring the app's own addApplicationFont calls. Changing the INSTALLED_DETAIL wording is fine.

### INSTALL-8: Update overwrites in place: the Windows 'locked, so use <stem>-n' fallback never runs on macOS

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:160`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:102`
- **macOS behaviour:** `_copy_into` calls `shutil.copyfile` over the existing file. That truncates and rewrites the same inode, which fontd may index and other apps have memory-mapped. macOS has no mandatory lock, so the copy always succeeds and the '<stem>-<n>' branch is never used. In my tests CoreText survived a truncating overwrite of a mapped 23 MB font. Even so, a watcher (fontd) can see a half-written file. `_copy_into` also overwrites whatever file already has that name, even one that is not ours. On Windows, when the fallback name is used, the old file is left on disk: only RemoveFontResource is called.
- **Evidence:** exp/e4b_inplace.out: after process-scope registration, vmmap shows `mapped file … [ 44.4M …] r--/rwx S` for the file. `shutil.copyfile(small, installed)` succeeded (no lock), and the process then read 3769 of 3770 glyph outlines without crashing. exp/e4b_replace.out: `os.replace` behaved the same (the old inode stays alive).
- **Recommendation:** On macOS:
1. Write the new file in a staging folder on the same volume (~/Library/Caches/FontPlayground) and fsync it.
2. If `previous` is a different file, unlink it.
3. `os.replace` into ~/Library/Fonts: an atomic rename to a new inode.

Never overwrite a file with that name that is not ours: pick '<stem>-<n>' instead. The prototype does all of this; `test_another_font_holding_the_file_name_is_left_alone` passes. Whether fontd re-reads a same-path replacement is the open question for manual_fontd_probe.py. If it does not, do 'unlink old, then rename new' for a same-name Update.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** install.py:160-173 overwrites with shutil.copyfile and falls back to '<stem>-n' only on OSError. On macOS nothing locks the file, so the fallback never runs. I checked the inode: `copyfile keeps inode: True`, `os.replace keeps inode: False`. install.py:102-104 on Windows only calls RemoveFontResource on the old path and leaves the file. I did not re-run the 23 MB mmap experiment; the recommendation does not depend on it.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Staging, fsync and os.replace are right, but the order must change. Rename the new file into place first, then remove the old one. The prototype unlinks the old copy before the rename, and a failed rename leaves neither (INSTALL-M2). Also check that the staging folder is on the same device as the fonts folder (`os.stat(a).st_dev == os.stat(b).st_dev`) before choosing it, and fall back to a dot-file in ~/Library/Fonts.

### INSTALL-9: Uninstall on macOS should move the file to the Trash like Font Book; replacing during Update should just delete

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:113`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:218`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:209`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:274`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:36`
- **macOS behaviour:** The Windows sequence (RemoveFontResourceW up to 16 times, delete the registry value, a 5-try unlink loop for the GDI lock) does not apply. On macOS a file in use can be unlinked straight away: running apps keep the old inode, and fontd deregisters the font. Font Book's Remove sends fonts to the Trash, and its duplicate resolution does too ('Resolve duplicates by moving files to the Trash'). BuildController uses the same `uninstall_font_for_user` call for an explicit Uninstall and for removing the previous name on Update.
- **Evidence:** exp/e4b_replace.out: after `os.replace` swapped the inode under a registered, mapped font, the process kept rendering glyphs and exited normally, so a font in use can be removed safely. Apple Font Book guide: https://support.apple.com/guide/font-book/install-and-validate-fonts-fntbk1000/mac. The Trash path itself was NOT run, to keep the user's Trash untouched; the prototype tests use `use_trash=False`.
- **Recommendation:** `uninstall_font_for_user(full, *, to_trash=True)`: find the file with `installed_path`, then `NSFileManager.defaultManager().trashItemAtURL_resultingItemURL_error_(url, None, None)`. Turn a failure into OSError, which the UI shows as "Couldn't remove the font: …". BuildController's rename-Update path should pass `to_trash=False` so each Update does not leave a copy in the Trash. After an explicit Uninstall, say 'Moved to the Trash.' (or 'Removed from your fonts — it's in the Trash.') instead of REMOVED_TEXT.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Apple's Font Book guide says removed fonts are moved to the Trash (https://support.apple.com/guide/font-book/remove-deactivate-or-activate-fonts-fntb2bcb512d/mac). The Windows retry and unload logic (install.py:210-227) has no macOS counterpart. I did not touch the Trash. `QFile.moveToTrash` exists in PySide6 6.11 (`hasattr` → True) and uses NSFileManager on macOS.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Do not change the shared signature to `uninstall_font_for_user(full, *, to_trash=True)`. BuildController calls it for both Uninstall and Update, so the Windows module and FakeInstaller would also need the keyword, or Update would fail with TypeError and loop on REPLACE_FAILED_TEXT. The simplest sound option is for the macOS backend to always move to the Trash. That is harmless and matches Font Book, which also uses the Trash when it resolves duplicates. Removal must also only ever touch files that is_forged() confirms (INSTALL-M1). Use NSFileManager `trashItemAtURL` through pyobjc; `QFile.moveToTrash` also works if the install module may import Qt.

### INSTALL-10: Tracking 'fonts this app installed' without a registry: scan the user font folder by name ID 4 plus FORGED_NOTICE

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:133`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:183`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:63`
- **macOS behaviour:** `installed_path()` reads the registry value '<full> (TrueType)', which does not exist on macOS. CoreText's per-registration user info (kCTFontRegistrationUserInfoAttribute) is unavailable on macOS, so there is no OS-level tag to use. A manifest file would drift when the user removes fonts in Font Book or uses 'Restore Standard Fonts'.
- **Evidence:** exp run: scanning with `installed_path()` took `6 ms` for 10 files in ~/Library/Fonts and `37 ms` for 80 files in /System/Library/Fonts (read-only). The CTFontManager.h annotation for kCTFontRegistrationUserInfoAttribute is `API_UNAVAILABLE(macos…)`. Prototype test `test_controller_install_update_rename_uninstall`: after `controller.reset()`, which stands in for a new session, `conflict()` returns `('replace', 'Replace the “Mac Flow Bold” you installed earlier?')` from the file scan and CoreText alone.
- **Recommendation:** Define 'installed by us' on macOS as: a file directly in ~/Library/Fonts whose name ID 4 matches the full name after casefold (`installed_path`), and whose name ID 0 starts with FORGED_NOTICE (`is_forged`, unchanged). This matches the Windows meaning, needs no state, and survives Font Book edits. A manifest in ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground is optional and should only ever be a hint. If ~/Library/Fonts can grow to thousands of files, cache the scan by (mtime, size).
- **Verifier (partially):** The definition proposed ('a file in ~/Library/Fonts with a matching name ID 4 AND FORGED_NOTICE') is sound, and it matches is_forged at install.py:63-75 and merge.py:47/55. The prototype does not implement it: install_macos.py:72-89 `installed_path()` matches name ID 4 only. v6_proto_dataloss.py showed that a NON-forged 'Victim Regular' in the fonts folder is returned by installed_path, then permanently unlinked by install_font_for_user (`user's own file still exists? False`). It also showed `uninstall_font_for_user('Other Regular')` removing a non-forged font (`True | exists? False`). See INSTALL-M1.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Make `installed_path(full)` return only files that are both a name-ID-4 match and is_forged(). Add a separate read-only `files_named(full)` for conflict() so it can still block when a foreign file uses the name.

### INSTALL-12: test_install.py is Windows-only; restructure it for macOS with CoreText process scope and a temp folder

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `tests/test_install.py:12`, `tests/test_install.py:44`, `tests/test_install.py:80`, `tests/test_install.py:91`, `tests/fakes.py:11`
- **macOS behaviour:** 3 tests are skipped (`windows_only`). `test_user_fonts_dir_follows_localappdata` passes on macOS only because `user_fonts_dir()` has no platform check. None of the macOS behaviour is tested. FakeInstaller has no `os_name`, `system_font_dirs`, `find_family` or `find_postscript`, so the macOS conflict paths cannot be tested in test_build.py.
- **Evidence:** Baseline on this Mac: 3 skipped in tests/test_install.py. Prototype work/install/repo/tests/test_install_macos.py: `7 passed in 1.90s`. It uses `MacInstaller(tmp_path/'Fonts', scope=kCTFontManagerScopeProcess, use_trash=False)` and a `naming_forge` that writes a correctly named forged file, and it checks `CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames` before and after. Full suite on the patched copy: `2 failed (test_paths, pre-existing), 346 passed, 3 skipped`. The real ~/Library/Fonts was left alone (still 10 files).
- **Recommendation:** Split the tests into four files:
- `test_install_common.py`: full_name_of, is_forged, and 'no Qt'.
- `test_install_windows.py`: registry tests, `skipif not win`; target WindowsInstaller explicitly, including the LOCALAPPDATA test.
- `test_install_macos.py`: `skipif not darwin` plus `importorskip('CoreText')`. Use a temp fonts_dir, process scope and no Trash, and cover the round trip, rename-Update removing the old file, a foreign file name left alone, and system-wide lookups (Helvetica, 苹方-简, PS Helvetica-Bold).
- An opt-in real-folder test gated by `FONTPLAYGROUND_TOUCH_REAL_FONTS=yes` (see exp/manual_fontd_probe.py). Unlike the Windows tests, do not touch the user's real font folder by default.

Add a backend contract test, parametrized over every backend available on the host. Extend FakeInstaller with `os_name`, `system_font_dirs`, and scripted `find_family` / `find_postscript` results, and add test_build cases for the 'macOS already has', downloadable, local and PostScript-name blocks.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Baseline: test_install.py has 3 Windows-only tests skipped. I read the prototype tests: they use tmp_path/Fonts, process scope and use_trash=False, and never touch the real folders. I re-ran them together with test_build.py: 30 passed. ~/Library/Fonts still had 10 files afterwards. FakeInstaller (fakes.py) has no os_name, system_font_dirs or find_* methods, as stated.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Also add tests that pin the defects found here: a non-forged file with the same full name must never be removed (INSTALL-M1); a failed rename must keep the old copy (INSTALL-M2); and two different forged CJK families must not both install under one PostScript name (INSTALL-5). In fixtures, use the synchronous RegisterFontsForURL for process-scope registration.

### INSTALL-14: Dependencies and distribution: pyobjc on macOS only; an unsandboxed Developer-ID build is needed to write ~/Library/Fonts

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:12`
- **macOS behaviour:** The CoreText lookups and the Trash call need pyobjc-framework-CoreText, which pulls in pyobjc-core, Cocoa and Quartz (about 15 MB). A Mac App Store build would be sandboxed and could not write ~/Library/Fonts without a temporary-exception entitlement. The Fonts entitlement (com.apple.developer.user-fonts) exists only for iOS. A plain Developer-ID (or unsigned local) build can write ~/Library/Fonts directly, with no TCC prompt expected, since that folder is not a protected location.
- **Evidence:** `uv pip show pyobjc-framework-CoreText`: `Requires: pyobjc-core, pyobjc-framework-cocoa, pyobjc-framework-quartz`. Installed sizes: objc 10M, Quartz 1.9M, AppKit 1.9M, Foundation 1.1M, CoreText 248K. `os.access(~/Library/Fonts, W_OK)`: True; permissions `drwx------+ <user> staff`. For the entitlement, see the Apple configuring-custom-fonts doc quoted in INSTALL-7.
- **Recommendation:** Add `"pyobjc-framework-CoreText>=10; sys_platform == 'darwin'"` to the dependencies, and import it lazily inside the macOS backend so the module still imports elsewhere. If pyobjc cannot be imported, MacInstaller should still install via the file copy and simply skip the system-wide lookups; `find_family` then returns [] and logs a warning. Ship an unsandboxed, notarized Developer-ID app (py2app, PyInstaller or Briefcase). Record in the README that a sandboxed or App Store build cannot offer 'Install for me'.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** `uv pip show pyobjc-framework-CoreText` 12.2.2: `Requires: pyobjc-core, pyobjc-framework-cocoa, pyobjc-framework-quartz`. Sizes: objc 10M, Quartz 1.9M, AppKit 1.9M, Foundation 1.1M, CoreText 248K. That is small next to PySide6. pyproject.toml:13-17 lists fonttools, skia-pathops and PySide6 only (the auditor cited line 12, off by one). ~/Library/Fonts is `drwx------+ <user> staff` and writable.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Adding the marker dependency is right. For the fallback when pyobjc is missing, use QFontDatabase.hasFamily for English family names (verified to see AssetsV2) instead of silently skipping the system-wide check.

### INSTALL-M2: Update removes the old installed copy before the new one is in place: a failed rename leaves the user with neither

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:120`, `work/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py:122`
- **macOS behaviour:** When the earlier copy has a different file name, the prototype unlinks it first and then runs `os.replace(tmp, dest)`. If the replace fails (EXDEV because the staging folder is on another volume, a permission change, a full disk), the font vanishes from the system and the UI says 'Couldn't install the font'. The user loses the working version they had installed.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-install/v9_order.py monkeypatches os.replace to raise EXDEV:
`install failed: [Errno 18] Cross-device link (simulated EXDEV)`
`old copy still installed? False | files now in Fonts: []`
- **Recommendation:** Rename the new file into place first, then remove the old one. Two files sharing a PostScript name for a few milliseconds does no harm. Alternatively, move the old one aside and restore it if the replace fails. Check that staging and the fonts folder are on the same device (`st_dev`) before using the Caches folder; otherwise stage as a dot-file inside ~/Library/Fonts.

### INSTALL-M3: No check that macOS can actually load the forged file before it is copied into ~/Library/Fonts (Font Book validates; a plain copy does not)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:264`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:108`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:206`
- **macOS behaviour:** Copying into ~/Library/Fonts skips the validation Font Book runs when it installs. If a build produces a file CoreText rejects (the auditor saw error 103 'unrecognized format' in experiments), it sits in the fonts folder unused, and the app still reports '✓ Installed'. CoreText can check this in advance: `CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL` returns no descriptors for such a file, and for a good file it also returns the family and PostScript name, which can be compared against what the recipe asked for.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-install/v9_order.py: `Ord Mix-Regular.ttf -> CoreText descriptors: 1`, `junk.ttf -> CoreText descriptors: 0`.
- **Recommendation:** In MacInstaller.install_font_for_user, before the copy: run `descs = CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL(url)`. If it is empty, raise OSError("macOS can't read this font file"). Otherwise confirm that kCTFontNameAttribute equals the expected PostScript name. On Windows, treat `AddFontResourceW(...) == 0` as a failure the same way.

### INSTALL-11: _is_under compares paths case-sensitively and without resolving symlinks on macOS

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:65`
- **macOS behaviour:** `os.path.normcase` does nothing on POSIX, but APFS is case-insensitive by default. So `_is_under('/system/library/fonts/Helvetica.ttc', '/System/Library/Fonts')` is False even though the file exists under that spelling. `abspath` does not resolve /tmp → /private/tmp or other firmlinks and symlinks; CoreText returned file:///tmp/… and file:///private/tmp/… URLs for files in the same folder.
- **Evidence:** exp/e6.out: `_is_under('/system/library/fonts/Helvetica.ttc', '/System/Library/Fonts') = False | file exists via that spelling: True`. exp/e2.out error URLs show `file:///tmp/claude-501/…` for a file whose path was <audit-scratch>
- **Recommendation:** Use `os.path.realpath` on both paths and casefold on darwin, as the prototype `_is_under` does. For a case-sensitive APFS volume, `os.path.samefile` on the candidate ancestor is stricter still.
