- **Verifier (confirmed):** v1_conflict.py: `_is_under('/system/library/fonts/Helvetica.ttc', '/System/Library/Fonts') = False` although the path exists (APFS is case-insensitive). In practice, catalog paths come from scanning the canonical roots in paths.py, and CoreText returns canonical /Users/... paths, so a case or symlink mismatch almost only shows up in tests (/tmp vs /private/tmp) or with oddly typed 'Add folder…' paths. That makes it polish, not minor.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Use realpath on both sides. For the case question, `os.path.samefile` on the candidate ancestor is more accurate than a blanket casefold, which would give false positives on case-sensitive APFS volumes.

### INSTALL-13: User-facing strings and 'Show file' are Windows-flavoured; use macOS wording and Show in Finder

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:38`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:33`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:37`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:86`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:173`, `README.md:30`
- **macOS behaviour:** The block message says 'Windows already has a font called …'. 'Show file' opens the parent folder (~/Library/Fonts, inside the hidden Library folder) without selecting the file. The README describes a Windows-only install.
- **Evidence:** Source strings: `WINDOWS_HAS_TEXT = "Windows already has a font called “{name}” — choose another name."` (build.py:38) and `self.show_file_link = self._link("Show file")` together with `open_folder(str(Path(shown).parent))` (action_bar.py:173 and 313). Not run: it would open a visible Finder window.
- **Recommendation:** - Wording by domain: system: 'macOS already has a font called “X” — choose another name.'; local: 'A font called “X” is already installed for everyone on this Mac — choose another name.'; downloadable: 'macOS can download a font called “X” — choose another name.'; a PostScript clash: see INSTALL-5.
- On macOS, label the link 'Show in Finder' and reveal the file with `NSWorkspace.sharedWorkspace().activateFileViewerSelectingURLs_([url])` (or `subprocess.run(['open', '-R', path])`).
- IDLE_TEXT ('Installs for your account only — no admin rights needed.') is true on macOS as well and can stay.
- Update README lines 7, 30 and 42.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** The strings are confirmed: build.py:38 WINDOWS_HAS_TEXT, action_bar.py:173 'Show file', and :313 `open_folder(str(Path(shown).parent))`. README.md:30 says 'installed for your Windows user account' and :42 lists macOS installing as unsupported. Not run, to avoid opening Finder.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** `subprocess.run(['open', '-R', path])` needs no pyobjc and reveals the file with it selected. Use `explorer /select,<path>` on Windows for the same behaviour.

#### Data points

- CoreText knows 624 faces: 492 in /System/Library/Fonts, 93 in /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8, 24 in /System/Library/PrivateFrameworks/FontServices.framework and 15 in ~/Library/Fonts. 43 families (PingFang SC/TC/HK/MO, Kaiti, YuGothic…) live only outside the dirs the app scans.
- 24 PostScript names are backed by more than one file on this Mac: PingFang is in both AssetsV2 PingFang.ttc and FontServices PingFangUI.ttc.
- Apple offers 332 font families for download; 191 of them are not installed right now.
- CoreText lookup times: family, display-name or PostScript lookup 0.1–4.3 ms (case-insensitive, matches localized names such as 苹方-简 → PingFang SC); snapshot of all 624 faces 21 ms; all 600 PostScript names 2 ms.
- Process-scope CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs: the handler ran synchronously in 2.7 ms. Local CTFontManagerFontChangedNotification arrived after about 3–5 ms; nothing came on the distributed center.
- CoreText error codes seen: 105 already registered (registering the same file twice), 201 not registered, 101 file not found, 103 unrecognized format. A second file with the same PostScript name, or a font named 'Helvetica', registered with no error.
- merge.postscript_name('我的字体','Regular') and ('你的字体','Regular') are both 'Forged-Regular'. With both registered, a lookup of 'Forged-Regular' returns the second font (你的字体).
- A 23 MB registered font was memory-mapped (44.4M region). Both an in-place shutil.copyfile truncation and os.replace left the process running, with 3769 of 3770 glyph outlines still readable.
- Qt 6.11.2 did not fire fontDatabaseChanged for runtime registrations under either offscreen or cocoa (background, activation policy 2). QtGui imports NSFontSetChangedNotification but not the CTFontManager changed notification.
- installed_path() scan of a folder by name ID 4: 6 ms for 10 files (~/Library/Fonts), 37 ms for 80 files (/System/Library/Fonts).
- conflict() on this Mac with the real install module returned ('none','') for Helvetica, PingFang SC, 苹方-简, Arial Unicode MS and Songti SC. system_fonts_dir() resolved to <cwd>/C:\Windows/Fonts.
- Prototype: 7 new macOS tests passed in 1.90 s. Full suite on the patched copy: 346 passed, 3 skipped, 2 failed (the pre-existing test_paths failures). The real ~/Library/Fonts still holds 10 files, untouched.
- Artifacts (scratch, not the user's repo): <audit-scratch>/install/repo/fontplayground/ui/install_macos.py, …/work/install/repo/tests/test_install_macos.py, …/work/install/macos_installer_prototype.patch (580 lines), …/work/install/exp/*.py with outputs in …/work/install/exp/*.out, and the opt-in probe …/work/install/exp/manual_fontd_probe.py (refuses to run unless FONTPLAYGROUND_TOUCH_REAL_FONTS=yes).

## UI/UX conventions, theming & rendering on macOS (`UI-*`)

I rendered the real PySide6 window with Qt's native cocoa backend without ever showing it (no Dock icon, no visible window) and captured it at Retina scale. On macOS the app looks fairly native: it uses the native 'macos' style, the system font (SF at 13 pt), crisp 2x text, and light/dark theme following that works. When you pick Light or Dark, Qt sets the whole app's appearance, so the title bar, menus and dialogs match. The biggest problems are elsewhere. There is no menu bar: every command lives in a '⋯' popup, and the only app menu is Qt's default one, which reads 'Quit __main__.py'. There are no keyboard shortcuts at all, including Cmd+W, Cmd+, and Cmd+F. When run unbundled, the Dock and menu bar show the app as 'python'. The font picker lists 74 hidden Apple system families (names starting with '.', plus .LastResort). One of them, '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI', sorts to the top and becomes the default choice for the main font. The default window (1280×820) is taller than this Mac's usable screen height (764 pt), so the bottom action bar with Save… ends up behind the Dock. Stylesheets written for Windows only partly style combo boxes and spin boxes, which draw broken under the macOS style. Smaller issues: the accent colour does not follow the macOS accent, sizes mix points and pixels (tuned for 96 dpi), a few strings are Windows-specific, dialogs are app-modal instead of sheets, and VoiceOver labels are missing. Chinese/Japanese input, trackpad scrolling and Retina rendering need no changes.

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| UI-1 | major | confirmed | Picker lists hidden Apple system fonts ('.'-prefixed, .LastResort, 'System Font'); one becomes the default main-font choice and one is suggested for Chinese |
| UI-2 | major | confirmed | No macOS menu bar: all commands hide in a '⋯' popup; the default app menu has no About or Settings, and there is no Edit, View, Window or Help menu and no Cmd+W or Cmd+, |
| UI-3 | major | confirmed | Unbundled app is named 'python' in the Dock and menu bar, and '__main__.py' in Hide/Quit/About; no app icon |
| UI-5 | major | partially | Stylesheets written for Windows/Fusion only partly style QComboBox/QSpinBox, which render broken under the native macOS style |
| UI-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | Apple fonts whose English family name is only in a Mac-Roman record show up under Urdu/Arabic/Hindi names and cannot be found by their English name |
| UI-M2 | major | (verifier-found) | Preview is not honest for Apple AAT fonts: morx ligatures/Indic shaping shown in the preview are dropped from the forged font |
| UI-4 | minor | partially | Default window is taller than a MacBook's usable screen, so the action bar (Save…/Install) lands behind the Dock; window size and position are not remembered |
| UI-6 | minor | confirmed | No keyboard shortcuts; the picker keys and hint texts assume a PC keyboard (Ctrl+Z, Enter, Home/End) |
| UI-7 | minor | confirmed | Type scale mixes points (tuned for 96 dpi) with stylesheet pixels: on macOS 1 pt = 1 px, so picker headers and tags fall to 9 px and the Sample button renders at 10 pt |
| UI-8 | minor | confirmed | Theme following works on macOS, but the accent, selection and window colours ignore the macOS accent colour and neutral window greys |
| UI-10 | minor | confirmed | Message boxes lose their titles on macOS, use Yes/No instead of verb buttons, and open app-modal instead of as sheets |

| UI-M4 | minor | (verifier-found) | Custom theme ignores macOS 'Increase contrast'; hairline borders are 1.2–1.4:1 |

| UI-14 | polish | partially | macOS overlay scroll bars cover the right edge of the selected picker row |
| UI-15 | polish | confirmed | Window-management extras expected on a Mac: Advanced as an inspector window, drag-and-drop of fonts, quit-on-close decision |

| UI-17 | polish | confirmed | IME, trackpad scrolling and Retina glyph rendering need no changes (verified) |

### UI-1: Picker lists hidden Apple system fonts ('.'-prefixed, .LastResort, 'System Font'); one becomes the default main-font choice and one is suggested for Chinese

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:573`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:593`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:157`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py`
- **macOS behaviour:** Scanning the default macOS font folders plus AssetsV2 finds 888 faces in 422 families; 74 families start with '.', which Apple uses for private system fonts. The picker sorts them first because '.' sorts before letters. In 'Choose your main font', '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI' is the current row and the button reads 'Use .Apple SD Gothic NeoI'. For Chinese, '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface' shows under SUGGESTED FOR YOUR TEXT. '.LastResort' (a font of placeholder boxes) is listed for every language.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/scan_all.py -> 'faces 888 failed 1 secs 4.9 / families 422 dot-hidden 74'. work/ui/hidden_count.py -> 'latin listed=199 hidden=13', 'chinese_s listed=39 hidden=2 [.Hiragino Sans GB Interface, .LastResort]', 'any listed=411 hidden=64'. work/ui/ct_families.py -> CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames: 221 families, 0 dot-prefixed; NSFontManager.availableFontFamilies: 0 dot-prefixed; '.LastResort', 'System Font' and '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI' are in neither list. Screenshots: work/ui/shots/cocoa_light_picker_latin_left.png (top row selected, 'Use .Apple SD Gothic NeoI') and cocoa_light_picker_chinese.png (suggested '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface').
- **Recommendation:** When the catalog is built (or in FontPicker._build_rows and smart.suggest_materials), drop faces whose family starts with '.', plus families named 'LastResort' or 'System Font', on darwin (or on every platform; it does no harm). Better still, add a `hidden: bool` field to FontFace at scan time so the picker, suggestions and the Advanced dialog all agree. Sort with key `f.lstrip('.').casefold()` as a safety net. Do not use the CoreText family list as a whitelist: it leaves out 223 scanned families (for example Courier and Times), which needs its own investigation.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** I rescanned the folders the app actually uses, default_font_dirs('darwin') without AssetsV2 (work/verify-ui/scan_hidden.py): 795 faces, 379 families, 74 of them '.'-prefixed, plus 'System Font'. Feeding them to FontPicker offscreen (picker_first.py) gives these current rows: latin -> '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI' ('Use .Apple SD Gothic NeoI'; 14 of 156 rows hidden); chinese_s -> '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface' (10 listed, 2 hidden); japanese and korean -> '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI'; any -> '.ADT Slab Numeric' (65 of 368 hidden). On the Qt cocoa backend, QFontDatabase.isPrivateFamily() returns True for '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI', '.LastResort', '.SF NS' and '.AppleSystemUIFont', and False for 'System Font'. QFontDatabase.families() (222) contains 1 dot family and no 'System Font'; CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames and NSFontManager (221) contain none. There is a knock-on bug: the dot name leaks into the default font name and file name (see UI-M3).
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Filter at catalog level with a Qt-free rule so the scan worker thread, picker, suggestions and Advanced all agree: hidden = family.startswith('.') or family in {'System Font', 'LastResort'}. That rule is exactly Qt's own darwin rule (QFontDatabase.isPrivateFamily) plus 'System Font', and it does nothing on Windows. Keep hidden faces in faces_by_key() so an old forge_last.json that references one still restores, but never list or suggest them.

### UI-2: No macOS menu bar: all commands hide in a '⋯' popup; the default app menu has no About or Settings, and there is no Edit, View, Window or Help menu and no Cmd+W or Cmd+,

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:171-199`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:167-174`
- **macOS behaviour:** Nothing creates a QMenuBar or QAction.MenuRole. Qt's automatic app menu contains About, About Qt and Preferences… (all three hidden), then Services, Hide, Hide Others, Show All and Quit. Cmd+Q works. Cmd+W, Cmd+, and Cmd+F do nothing. With no menu titled 'Edit', macOS cannot add its Emoji & Symbols and Dictation items. Rescan, Add folder, Start over, Advanced, Theme and Open settings folder are only reachable through the '⋯' button at the top right of the preview.
- **Evidence:** grep for QMenuBar|menuBar|MenuRole|QKeySequence|setShortcut in fontplayground/ returns nothing. work/ui/bundle_probe.py (cocoa backend): menu 0 = ['About …','About Qt','','Preferences…','','Services','','Hide …','Hide Others','Show All','','Quit …'], with About, About Qt and Preferences… hidden. work/ui/menu_probe2.py 'menubar' mode (a QMenuBar with actions set to AboutRole, PreferencesRole and QuitRole): the app menu becomes ['About Font Playground'(visible), 'Settings…' key ',', …, 'Quit Font Playground' key 'q']. work/ui/misc_probe.py lists the native shortcuts: Preferences ⌘, / Close ⌘W / Find ⌘F / SaveAs ⇧⌘S / New ⌘N / ZoomIn ⌘+ / FullScreen ⌃⌘F.
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, build the menus with self.menuBar() and route everything through the same QActions the '⋯' menu uses; then hide the ⋯ button on darwin or shrink its menu.
- App menu: About Font Playground (AboutRole) and Settings… ⌘, (PreferencesRole). Settings opens a small window with Appearance (Auto/Light/Dark), the list of extra font folders with +/−, and 'Show Settings Folder in Finder'. Quit (QuitRole) is provided automatically.
- File: Start Over ⌘N (QKeySequence.StandardKey.New), Add Font Folder… ⌘O, Rescan Fonts ⌘R, Save a Copy… ⇧⌘S (StandardKey.SaveAs), Install / Update Installed Font once supported, Show in Finder, Close Window ⌘W (StandardKey.Close).
- Edit: Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All sent to QApplication.focusWidget(), plus Find Font… ⌘F (StandardKey.Find) to open the picker or focus its search.
- Font: Choose Main Font…, an 'Add Font for' submenu of languages, and Advanced… (⌥⌘A or ⌘I).
- View: Colour by Font (checkable), Bigger ⌘+ / Smaller ⌘− / Actual Size ⌘0 for the preview size, a Sample Text submenu, and Enter Full Screen ⌃⌘F.
- Window: Minimize ⌘M, Zoom, Advanced.
- Help: Font Playground Help, opening the GitHub README.
Give every ordinary action setMenuRole(QAction.MenuRole.NoRole), so that Qt's text heuristics never move items such as 'Settings…' or 'About…' into the app menu.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** grep for QMenuBar|menuBar|MenuRole|QKeySequence|setShortcut|QShortcut|StandardKey in fontplayground/ returns nothing (rc=1). Cocoa probe (cocoa1.py, never shown, QT_MAC_DISABLE_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION_TRANSFORM=1) with argv0 '/Users/x/fontplayground/__main__.py': menu 0 = About __main__.py (hidden), About Qt (hidden), Settings… (hidden), Services, Hide __main__.py, Hide Others, Show All, Quit __main__.py, plus an empty 'QtWindowMenu'. Correction: on macOS 27 Qt's hidden item is titled 'Settings…', not 'Preferences…'. Launched through the `fontplayground` console script, the items read 'Quit fontplayground'. Native bindings (keys.py): Preferences ⌘, / Close ⌘W / Find ⌘F / SaveAs ⇧⌘S / New ⌘N / Open ⌘O / FullScreen ⌃⌘F / HelpContents ⌘?.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed: one set of QActions shared by the ⋯ menu (Windows) and a menu bar (darwin), with explicit MenuRoles and NoRole on everything else. Two additions: (1) the AboutRole panel is the natural place for the Qt LGPL notice that README.md:55 requires for bundled builds; (2) QLineEdit/QTextEdit already handle ⌘C/⌘V/⌘Z internally through ShortcutOverride, so Edit-menu actions should call focusWidget().copy()/paste()/undo() and stay disabled when the focused widget cannot do them.

### UI-3: Unbundled app is named 'python' in the Dock and menu bar, and '__main__.py' in Hide/Quit/About; no app icon

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405-415`, `fontplayground/__main__.py`, `pyproject.toml`
- **macOS behaviour:** `python -m fontplayground` runs a bare interpreter with no bundle. The Dock and the bold menu-bar title use the process name 'python'. Qt builds the app-menu items from argv[0], so they read 'Hide __main__.py' and 'Quit __main__.py'. Calling app.setApplicationName() after, or even before, constructing QApplication does not change them. No window icon is set, so the Dock shows a generic executable icon.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/bundle_probe.py: NSBundle.mainBundle().bundleIdentifier() = None, CFBundleName None, NSRunningApplication.localizedName 'python'. work/ui/menu_probe.py with argv0 '/x/fontplayground/__main__.py': 'asis' (what app.py does) -> ['About __main__.py', …, 'Hide __main__.py', …, 'Quit __main__.py']; 'fixed' (QCoreApplication.setApplicationName before construction) -> the same. work/ui/menu_probe2.py: 'argv' (QApplication(['Font Playground'])) -> 'Hide Font Playground', 'Quit Font Playground'. 'hack' (NSBundle.mainBundle().infoDictionary()['CFBundleName']='Font Playground' before QApplication) -> the same fixed titles.
- **Recommendation:** (1) Quick fix: in main(), construct the application as QApplication(['Font Playground', *sys.argv[1:]]) and call app.setApplicationDisplayName('Font Playground') and app.setWindowIcon(QIcon(<bundled .png/.icns>)). For development runs on darwin, optionally set CFBundleName through pyobjc before creating QApplication. (2) Proper fix: ship a .app (PyInstaller --windowed, py2app or Briefcase) with an Info.plist containing CFBundleName/CFBundleDisplayName 'Font Playground', CFBundleIdentifier (for example io.github.kciceblue.fontplayground), CFBundleIconFile (.icns), NSHighResolutionCapable true and LSApplicationCategoryType public.app-category.graphics-design. Replace run.bat with a Makefile/script target that builds the app.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** name_probe.py (cocoa, never shown), argv0 '/x/fontplayground/__main__.py'. mode none: 'Quit __main__.py', localizedName 'python'. mode plist (NSBundle.mainBundle().infoDictionary()['CFBundleName'] set before QApplication): 'Quit Font Playground', but localizedName still 'python'. mode procname (NSProcessInfo.setProcessName_): menu unchanged, localizedName 'python'. The main bundle is the venv bin dir. So the argv0 and CFBundleName tricks fix only the app-menu item titles. The Dock, ⌘Tab and Force Quit name stay 'python' whatever the process does at runtime; only a real .app bundle fixes the name and the icon.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Treat a .app bundle (PyInstaller --windowed or Briefcase, with Info.plist CFBundleName/CFBundleDisplayName/CFBundleIdentifier/.icns) as the real fix and make it the documented way to run on a Mac. For source runs, only QApplication(['Font Playground', *sys.argv[1:]]) is worth keeping; skip the pyobjc CFBundleName hack, which fixes nothing the argv0 trick doesn't.

### UI-5: Stylesheets written for Windows/Fusion only partly style QComboBox/QSpinBox, which render broken under the native macOS style

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:96-98`, `fontplayground/ui/advanced.py:55-58`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:86-88`
- **macOS behaviour:** The rules set only background, border, border-radius and padding, so the macOS style still draws the drop-down and spinner sub-controls. The result is a hard, square-cornered dark frame over the right end of every rounded combo (Style, Weight, Show fonts for, Line spacing from, Default boldness). Spin boxes (Size, Default size) get tiny squashed arrow dots inside a hard frame. It shows in both light and dark.
- **Evidence:** Cocoa-backend grabs at DPR 2: work/ui/shots/zoom_combo_spin.png (crop of cocoa_light_recipe_two.png), cocoa_light_advanced.png and zoom_dark_picker.png (the 'Letters (Latin)' combo). work/ui/cocoa_probe.py -> style 'macos'.
- **Recommendation:** Pick one of three:
(a) Native (recommended): on darwin, leave the QComboBox/QSpinBox rules out of the sheets (keep them as a separate block that is added only when not darwin). Native controls already follow light/dark, because Qt sets NSApp.appearance (see UI-8).
(b) Keep the custom look: style every sub-control. For combos: QComboBox::drop-down { border:none; width:20px; subcontrol-origin:padding; subcontrol-position:center right } and QComboBox::down-arrow { image:url(:/chevron-$theme.svg) }. For spin boxes: QSpinBox::up-button/::down-button plus their ::up-arrow/::down-arrow images.
(c) Call app.setStyle('Fusion') on every platform for a uniform custom look (the least native option).
- **Verifier (partially):** I reproduced it (combo_grab.py; work/verify-ui/combo2_native.png, combo2_Fusion.png, combo2_Windows.png, DPR 2). Under the macOS style the recipe sheet (recipe.py:96-98) draws a square hard frame over the rounded combo's drop-down and squashed spin arrows, and the same with the advanced.py:55-58 and picker.py:86-88 rules. Refuted: 'The Windows and Fusion styles cope with partial sheets'. The same sheet under Fusion and under the 'Windows' style also shows the hard square drop-down frame and cramped spin buttons. The root cause is the partial stylesheet: once border/background are set on a complex control, QStyleSheetStyle expects ::drop-down/::up-button to be styled too. It is not macOS-specific. The windows11 style could not be tested here. So option (c), Fusion everywhere, would NOT fix it. My quick test of option (b) with only `::drop-down {border:none}` and `::up-button {border:none}` removed the arrows entirely, so (b) needs arrow images.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Either (a) on darwin, drop the QComboBox/QSpinBox rules to get native popup buttons and steppers (combo2_native.png 'no sheet' row looks correct and follows NSApp.appearance); or (b) finish the custom look on every platform: QComboBox::drop-down {subcontrol-origin: padding; subcontrol-position: center right; width: 20px; border: none} + QComboBox::down-arrow {image: url(<chevron-{light,dark}.svg>)} and QSpinBox::up-button/::down-button {border: none; width: 16px} + ::up-arrow/::down-arrow images, generated per theme through Theme.render. Do not rely on setStyle('Fusion'): it shows the same defect.

### UI-M1: Apple fonts whose English family name is only in a Mac-Roman record show up under Urdu/Arabic/Hindi names and cannot be found by their English name

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:100-129`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:182`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:457-459`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:559-565`
- **macOS behaviour:** _name_rank ranks every Windows-platform (3,1) record, whatever its language, above the Mac-Roman English record (1,0,lang 0). Many Apple fonts carry English only in the Mac record. As a result 'Geeza Pro' is listed as 'گیزا پرو' (the Urdu name, langID 0x420), 'Al Bayan' as 'البيان', and 'Gujarati MT' as 'गुजराती एचटी' (a Hindi name on a Gujarati font). The English name is neither the family nor in local_names, so searching the Arabic picker for 'al bayan' or 'baghdad' finds nothing, and 'geeza' finds only the hidden '.Geeza Pro Interface'. 21 families in the default folders have no Latin letters in their name. macOS itself (CoreText, Font Book, font menus) calls them 'Geeza Pro' and 'Al Bayan'.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-ui (scan of default_font_dirs('darwin')): GeezaPro.ttc name records [(1,0,0x0,'Geeza Pro'), (3,1,0x420,'گیزا پرو'), (3,1,0x439,'गीज़ा प्रो'), (3,1,0xc01,'جيزة')] -> FontFace.family 'گیزا پرو'; AlBayan.ttc [(1,0,0x0,'Al Bayan'), (3,1,0xc01,'البيان')] -> 'البيان'; GujaratiMT.ttc -> 'गुजराती एचटी'. 'families with no Latin letters in their name: 21'. search.py (offscreen picker, arabic): search 'al bayan' -> [] 'No fonts match “al bayan”.'; 'baghdad' -> []; 'geeza' -> ['.Geeza Pro Interface']. CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames contains 'Geeza Pro' and 'Al Bayan'.
- **Recommendation:** In _name_rank, rank (3,*,0x409) 0, then a (1,0,langID 0) Mac-Roman English record 1 when its decoded text is pure ASCII (this avoids the EPSON Shift-JIS-in-MacRoman mojibake the comment warns about), then the other Windows languages 2, then Mac Japanese. Always add the English name to the picker haystack and to local_names when it differs from the display family. Add a fixture test with a font whose only English name record is Mac-Roman. Bump the catalog cache version so existing caches are rebuilt.

### UI-M2: Preview is not honest for Apple AAT fonts: morx ligatures/Indic shaping shown in the preview are dropped from the forged font

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:130-133`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:55-65`, `README.md:24`
- **macOS behaviour:** Qt on macOS shapes source fonts with their AAT 'morx' tables: ligatures, contextual forms, Indic reordering and conjuncts. The engine keeps only KEEP_TABLES = {…GSUB, GPOS, GDEF, kern} and strip_tables deletes morx/kerx/feat/trak. 65 families in the default macOS folders have morx but no GSUB, among them Hoefler Text, Baskerville, Didot, Cochin, Optima, Helvetica, Helvetica Neue, Menlo, Times, Devanagari MT, Gujarati MT, Gurmukhi MT, Malayalam MN, Kailasa and all the Apple Arabic fonts. For these the mix preview shows shaping the result will not have, which breaks the README promise that the preview is 'drawn exactly the way the result will draw it'. Once built, the preview switches to the built font and the text changes. Indic and Arabic fonts can lose their shaping entirely. The build report does not mention it (grep morx/AAT in the engine finds nothing).
- **Evidence:** work/verify-ui/aat.py: 'families with morx but no GSUB: 65' (list above). indic.py (cocoa backend; the same face with only morx/kerx/mort/feat/trak removed, drawn with the app's own fonts.make_font for the source): 'Devanagari MT: preview(source) 10 glyphs w=184.2 | without AAT 9 glyphs w=173.8 | identical=False'; 'Gujarati MT: 12 glyphs w=209.6 | without AAT 12 glyphs w=209.5 | identical=False'; 'Hoefler Text: 9 glyphs w=173.8 | without AAT 12 glyphs w=178.4' (the 'ffi'/'fi' ligatures are lost). Control: Helvetica Neue Bold 'AVATAR Toyota WAVE' is identical with and without kerx (411.92), because its legacy kern already covers it; the problem is morx, not kerning.
- **Recommendation:** (1) At scan time, record an aat_only flag on FontFace (morx without GSUB, or kerx without GPOS/kern). Show a note on the picker row and recipe card ('Apple-only ligatures/shaping won't carry over'), push morx-only fonts down in the Indic, Arabic and SE-Asian lists, and list them as a warning in the build report. (2) To make the preview honest, preview a material through its prepared (AAT-stripped) file instead of the original, or at least compare against it when aat_only. (3) Optionally, alongside kern_to_gpos, convert kerx format-0 pairs to GPOS; there is no practical morx→GSUB conversion, so warn instead.

### UI-4: Default window is taller than a MacBook's usable screen, so the action bar (Save…/Install) lands behind the Dock; window size and position are not remembered

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:412-413`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:28`
- **macOS behaviour:** main() calls resize(1280, 820) and setMinimumSize(1000, 640). On this Mac the primary screen is 1352×878 pt and availableGeometry is (0, 29, 1352×764). Adding a 28 pt title bar gives an 848 pt frame, about 84 pt more than the usable height. Those 84 pt are exactly BAR_HEIGHT, the action bar holding the name field and the Save… button. AppKit only keeps the title bar on screen. Nothing calls saveGeometry or restoreGeometry, so the window reopens at the same oversize every time.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/cocoa_probe.py -> 'screen Built-in Retina Display logicalDpi 72.0 dpr 2.0 QRect(0, 0, 1352, 878) avail QRect(0, 29, 1352, 764)'. app.py:412 `window.resize(1280, 820)`. action_bar.py:28 `BAR_HEIGHT = 84`. grep finds no saveGeometry/restoreGeometry/QSettings.
- **Recommendation:** Take the size from QGuiApplication.primaryScreen().availableGeometry(): width = min(1280, avail.width() - 40), height = min(820, avail.height() - 40), centred. Keep the 1000×640 minimum (it fits). In closeEvent store base64(self.saveGeometry()) in settings.json and call restoreGeometry() at startup; restoreGeometry already clamps the window to the current screens. Do the same for the Advanced window.
- **Verifier (partially):** Confirmed: nothing saves or restores geometry (grep), and 1280×820 (+28 pt title bar = 848) is taller than availableGeometry 1352×764 here. Refuted: 'the action bar ends up behind the Dock'. Apple's NSWindow.constrainFrameRect(_:to:) docs say it is 'invoked automatically … whenever a titled NSWindow object is placed onscreen', and that a resizable window taller than the screen gets its height adjusted. My constrain.py test (NSWindow created with defer, never ordered front) passed the 1280×848 resizable frame at y=30/1/500 and always got back origin.y=85, height=764, i.e. inside visibleFrame and above the Dock. Qt does not override that selector (`strings libqcocoa.dylib | grep constrainFrame` finds nothing). What actually goes wrong: QPlatformWindow::initialGeometry (qtbase 6.11 qplatformwindow.cpp:728-738) centres a window only when it is smaller than 8/9 of the available area (679×1201 here). 820>679 and 1280>1201, so the window is not centred: it opens at the top-left edge and AppKit squeezes it to 736 pt of content. The same happens on the common 1512×982 MacBook Pro 14 default. I did not show a real window to confirm the final frame.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed: size = min(1280, avail.width()*0.9) × min(820, avail.height()*0.9), then move the window to the centre of availableGeometry explicitly, and saveGeometry()/restoreGeometry() through settings.json (restoreGeometry already clamps to the connected screens). This is a cosmetic, first-launch fix, not a data-loss or blocked-action issue.

### UI-6: No keyboard shortcuts; the picker keys and hint texts assume a PC keyboard (Ctrl+Z, Enter, Home/End)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:730-746`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:162`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:41`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:197`
- **macOS behaviour:** The tooltip says 'Ctrl+Z brings yours back', but on macOS undo is ⌘Z and ⌃Z does nothing. The trial banner says 'Enter uses it'; Mac keyboards call that key Return. The picker handles Home and End only in the list, and MacBooks have no such keys (⌘↑/⌘↓ is the Mac convention). It cancels only on Esc, not on ⌘. (StandardKey.Cancel). Nothing binds ⌘F to the picker search.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/misc_probe.py (cocoa backend): Undo ['⌘Z'], Cancel ['⎋','⌘.'], MoveToStartOfDocument ['⌘↑','↖'], MoveToEndOfDocument ['⌘↓','↘'], Find ['⌘F']; QKeySequence('Ctrl+Z').toString(NativeText) = '⌘Z' while QKeySequence('Meta+Z') = '⌃Z'. picker.py:733-745 tests only Key_Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown/Home/End/Return/Enter/Escape.
- **Recommendation:** In FontPicker.eventFilter, also move on event.matches(QKeySequence.StandardKey.MoveToStartOfDocument/MoveToEndOfDocument/MoveToPreviousPage/MoveToNextPage) and cancel on event.matches(QKeySequence.StandardKey.Cancel). Build hint texts from native key names: undo = QKeySequence(QKeySequence.StandardKey.Undo).toString(QKeySequence.SequenceFormat.NativeText), and 'Return' instead of 'Enter' on darwin (for example TRY_HINT = f"↑ ↓ try the next font, {'Return' if sys.platform == 'darwin' else 'Enter'} uses it."). Add a ⌘F action (StandardKey.Find) that opens or focuses the picker search (see UI-2).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** keys.py (cocoa): Undo ⌘Z, Cancel ⎋ / ⌘., MoveToStartOfDocument ⌘↑ / Home, MoveToEndOfDocument ⌘↓ / End, MoveToPreviousPage includes ⌃↑. cmdup.py (offscreen FontPicker with the real default fonts): after 5×Down the row is 6; ⌘↑ (Key_Up+ControlModifier) in the search box goes to row 5, one row up instead of the top. ⌘↓ in the list moves one row. ⌘. leaves the picker open. Overstated: 'MacBooks have no such keys'. Fn+←/→ produce Key_Home/Key_End, which on mac are the MoveToStart/EndOfDocument bindings, so Home/End already work in the list. The hint strings are confirmed at preview_pane.py:162 ('Ctrl+Z…') and app.py:41 ('Enter uses it').
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** In FontPicker.eventFilter, test event.matches(StandardKey.MoveToStartOfDocument/MoveToEndOfDocument/MoveToPreviousPage/MoveToNextPage/Cancel) BEFORE the bare `key in steps` lookup. The current code matches Key_Up whatever the modifiers, so ⌘↑ would otherwise still be eaten as one step. Build the hints from QKeySequence(StandardKey.Undo).toString(NativeText) and a Return/Enter word per platform.

### UI-7: Type scale mixes points (tuned for 96 dpi) with stylesheet pixels: on macOS 1 pt = 1 px, so picker headers and tags fall to 9 px and the Sample button renders at 10 pt

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:38-47`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:51`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:63-67`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:40-46`, `fontplayground/ui/widgets.py:79`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:409-410`
- **macOS behaviour:** logicalDpi is 72, and the app font is .AppleSystemUIFont 13 pt (13 px). Point-based sizes come out 25% smaller than on Windows, while the px-based stylesheet sizes stay the same, so the proportions change. The picker's section header and 'in your font' tag (HEADER_PT/TAG_PT 8.5 pt) become 9 px; macOS SmallestReadableFont is 9 pt. Family names in picker rows (NAME_PT 13) end up the same size as body text. The recipe card's family name (17 pt → 17 px) is now smaller than the 18 px 'Your font' heading, which reverses the Windows layout. Qt's macOS theme gives the QToolButton class a 10 pt font, so 'Sample ▾' (and the Disclosure button) looks visibly smaller than its 13 pt neighbours. The app correctly skips the Segoe UI override on mac.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/font_probe.py (cocoa backend): 'QToolButton .AppleSystemUIFont 10.0pt', 'QHeaderView 11pt', 'QListView 12pt'. misc_probe: polished QToolButton font = 10.0 pt. 'HEADER_PT 8.5pt -> here 9px … on Windows 96dpi ~11.3px', 'NAME_PT 13pt -> 13px … ~17.3px', 'recipe NAME_PT 17pt -> 17px … ~22.7px'. cocoa_probe: SmallestReadableFont 9.0 pt, FixedFont Menlo 10 pt. Crop: work/ui/shots/zoom_toolbar_right.png (small 'Sample ▾').
- **Recommendation:** Express every size relative to the app font: base = QApplication.font().pointSizeF(), with the picker header and tag at max(10, base * 0.85), picker name at base * 1.0–1.15, sample at base * 1.4, card name at base * 1.3. Alternatively use one unit throughout, either px everywhere (setPixelSize in the delegates) or pt everywhere in the sheets. Add an explicit 'font-size: 13px' to QToolButton#sampleButton and #disclosure, or call app.setFont(app.font(), 'QToolButton') on darwin. Use 11 pt Menlo/SF Mono for the Advanced report on mac.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** cocoa1.py: logicalDpi 72, app font .AppleSystemUIFont 13 pt, polished QToolButton 10.0 pt, QPushButton/QComboBox/QLabel 13 pt. The sampleButton sheet (preview_pane.py:40-41) sets no font-size, so 'Sample ▾' stays at 10 pt next to 13 pt controls. The recipe card's NAME_PT 17 (17 px on mac) against QLabel#h1 18px holds, and so do picker HEADER_PT/TAG_PT 8.5. Nit: CoreText renders fractional sizes, so 8.5 pt is 8.5 px, not 9 px, which is still below SmallestReadableFont 9 pt.

### UI-8: Theme following works on macOS, but the accent, selection and window colours ignore the macOS accent colour and neutral window greys

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:99-125`, `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:213-228`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:42`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:184`, `README.md:7`, `README.md:34`
- **macOS behaviour:** Works: with Qt 6.11, ThemeManager's setColorScheme sets NSApp.appearance (Light → NSAppearanceNameAqua, Dark → NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua, System → nil), so the native title bar, QMenus, message boxes and file panels match the chosen theme. colorSchemeChanged fired when the effective appearance changed. Doesn't follow macOS: the app overwrites the whole palette with fixed blues (#1a6bd8 light, #4c8ef5 dark), so the user's accent choice (system #007aff here; purple, graphite and so on elsewhere) is ignored. The text-selection highlight is a strong #4c8ef5 where macOS uses #3f638b in dark mode. The window ground is a bluish #1b1e24 next to the neutral #323232 of the native dark title bar, which leaves a visible seam.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/theme_probe.py (cocoa): 'pref light hints=Light NSApp.appearance=NSAppearanceNameAqua … theme=light', 'pref dark … NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua … theme=dark', 'pref system … NSApp.appearance=None effective=NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua theme=dark'. theme_flip2.py: hints colorSchemeChanged emitted 'Light' after the effective appearance changed. accent_probe.py: system palette before override Accent #0a60ff, Highlight #314f78; after ThemeManager Accent/Highlight #4c8ef5; NSColor.controlAccentColor #007aff, selectedTextBackgroundColor #3f638b, windowBackgroundColor #323232. Dark grab: work/ui/shots/cocoa_dark_recipe_two.png.
- **Recommendation:** Update the README: automatic theme following is verified on macOS. On darwin, read the platform Accent/Highlight before the first setPalette (QGuiApplication.palette() at startup) and build accent, accent_hover and accent_soft* from it (for example QColor.lighter()/darker()). Re-derive them on QEvent.ThemeChange, or on NSSystemColorsDidChangeNotification through pyobjc. Leave Highlight/HighlightedText as the platform values rather than the accent. Move the window, surface and border tokens towards the neutral system greys (window #ececec/#323232, controls #ffffff/#1e1e1e). Optional: setWindowFlag(Qt.WindowType.ExpandedClientAreaHint) plus NoTitleBarBackgroundHint (both in PySide6 6.11) so the left column runs under a transparent title bar, Finder-style. Rename the menu 'Theme' → 'Appearance' and 'System' → 'Auto' (the macOS wording).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** theme_v.py (cocoa): the platform palette before the override has Accent #0a60ff, Highlight #314f78, Window #323232; NSColor controlAccent #007aff, selectedTextBackground #3f638b, windowBackground #323232. ThemeManager light -> NSApp.appearance NSAppearanceNameAqua; dark -> NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua; system -> None (effective DarkAqua). colorSchemeChanged was emitted ['Light','Dark']. The app palette's Accent afterwards is #4c8ef5. PySide6 6.11.2 has Qt.WindowType.ExpandedClientAreaHint and NoTitleBarBackgroundHint, but pyproject allows PySide6>=6.8, so they need a hasattr guard or a version bump.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Once QGuiApplication.setPalette() has set Accent/Highlight, QGuiApplication.palette() returns the app's own colours, so 're-derive on ThemeChange by reading the palette' will read back #4c8ef5. Either capture the platform palette once before the first setPalette and re-read NSColor.controlAccentColor (pyobjc) on QEvent.ThemeChange/ApplicationPaletteChange, or on darwin leave Accent/Highlight/HighlightedText out of Theme.palette(). Qt then resolves them from the platform palette and updates them on accent changes; derive the $accent tokens from palette().color(Accent) at render time.

### UI-10: Message boxes lose their titles on macOS, use Yes/No instead of verb buttons, and open app-modal instead of as sheets

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:296-303`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:380-388`
- **macOS behaviour:** QMessageBox.warning(self, 'Choose another name', …), question(self, 'Replace the font?', …) and the quit question titled 'Font Playground' all drop the title, as Apple's guidelines require. Only the sentence shows, with Yes/No buttons and no informative text, in a free-floating app-modal alert. Positive: ⌘Q goes through closeEvent, so 'Your font is still being built. Quit anyway?' is asked and a No keeps the app running.
- **Evidence:** https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/qmessagebox.html: 'On macOS, the window title is ignored (as required by the macOS Guidelines).' and 'if you want your message box to appear as a Qt::Sheet of its parent, set the message box's window modality to Qt::WindowModal or use open().' work/ui/quit_probe.py (offscreen, sending the QEvent.Quit that the Cocoa Quit item delivers): "asked: ['Your font is still being built. Quit anyway?'] window still visible: True aboutToQuit: []"; when not busy the window closes.
- **Recommendation:** Add a small helper: box = QMessageBox(self); box.setIcon(...); box.setText('Replace “X”?') for the short bold line; box.setInformativeText(detail); use verb buttons (addButton('Replace', AcceptRole) with Cancel; 'Quit' with 'Keep Building'); box.setWindowModality(Qt.WindowModality.WindowModal); box.exec(). exec() still blocks, so closeEvent logic is unchanged, and the box becomes a sheet on mac. Keep the self-contained sentences, since the titles are gone.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** doc.qt.io/qt-6/qmessagebox.html says 'On macOS, the window title is ignored (as required by the macOS Guidelines)' and 'if you want your message box to appear as a Qt::Sheet of its parent, set the message box's window modality to Qt::WindowModal or use open()'. Code at action_bar.py:296-303 and app.py:380-388. On macOS today only the quit question can appear: conflict() 'block'/'replace' is reached only when installer.is_supported(), and install.py:32-33 returns True only on win.

### UI-11: 'Open settings folder' and 'Show file' should become Finder actions that reveal the item

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:197-208`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:86-88`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:173`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:310-313`
- **macOS behaviour:** 'Open settings folder' opens a Finder window on ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground; ~/Library is hidden in Finder, and power users are the only audience. 'Show file' opens the parent folder via QDesktopServices without selecting the saved .ttf. Mac apps use 'Show in Finder', which reveals and selects the item.
- **Evidence:** action_bar.py:313 `open_folder(str(Path(shown).parent))`; app.py:208 `QDesktopServices.openUrl(QUrl.fromLocalFile(str(self.config_dir)))`; paths.config_dir('darwin') = ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground.
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, add reveal(path) that runs subprocess.run(['open', '-R', str(path)]), or NSWorkspace.sharedWorkspace().activateFileViewerSelectingURLs_ through pyobjc; use `explorer /select,` on Windows. Label the link 'Show in Finder' (darwin) or 'Show in Explorer'. Move 'Open settings folder' into the Settings window or Help menu as 'Show Settings Folder in Finder'.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Checked by reading the code only; I did not open Finder. action_bar.py:310-313 calls open_folder(parent). app.py:207-208 uses QDesktopServices.openUrl on config_dir, which on darwin is ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground (paths.py:33-34).

### UI-12: Windows-specific user-visible strings and README text

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:38`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:60-62`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:33`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:36`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:173`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:157`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:162`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:41`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:197`, `README.md:7`, `README.md:11`, `README.md:19`, `README.md:26`, `README.md:30`, `README.md:34`, `README.md:42`, `README.md:47-51`
- **macOS behaviour:** Current strings and suggested macOS wording:
- build.py:38 'Windows already has a font called “{name}” — choose another name.' → 'macOS already has a font called “{name}” — choose another name.' This is unreachable today (install is unsupported on mac), and system_fonts_dir() resolves to PosixPath('C:\\Windows/Fonts').
- action_bar.py:33 'Installs for your account only — no admin rights needed.' → 'Installs in your Fonts folder — only for you, no password needed.'
- action_bar.py:36 '…for your account.' is fine.
- action_bar.py:173 'Show file' → 'Show in Finder'.
- install.py:157 '…only supported on Windows.' → drop it once mac install exists.
- preview_pane.py:162 'Ctrl+Z' → '⌘Z' (native text).
- app.py:41 'Enter uses it' → 'Return uses it'.
- app.py:197 'Open settings folder' → 'Show Settings Folder in Finder'.
- 'Theme / System' → 'Appearance / Auto'.
- README: 'System follows the Windows "Choose your mode" setting' → '…follows macOS Appearance (Light, Dark, Auto)'. Replace the `.venv\\Scripts` commands and run.bat with `.venv/bin/…`. Update the 'installed for your Windows user account' paragraph and the Windows-only install notes.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn -E 'Enter|Ctrl|admin|Windows|Show file|settings folder' fontplayground/ui/*.py (output quoted above). tests/test_build.py:338 asserts the exact 'Windows already has…' string, so tests must change along with it.
- **Recommendation:** Move platform wording into one module (for example ui/platform_text.py) keyed on sys.platform: OS name, file manager name ('Finder'/'Explorer'), Return/Enter, a native_shortcut(StandardKey) helper, and the install-scope sentence. Point the tests at those constants rather than literals. Add a macOS section to the README.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** My grep of ui/*.py matches the list. system_fonts_dir() on this Mac returns PosixPath('C:\\Windows/Fonts') (ran it). Of these strings, only preview_pane.py:162 ('Ctrl+Z…') and app.py:41 ('Enter uses it') are visible on macOS today. build.py:38 and action_bar.py:33 appear only when install is supported. README.md:42 tells mac users to 'use Open folder', but the link is labelled 'Show file' (action_bar.py:173).

### UI-13: VoiceOver: icon-only and custom-painted controls have no useful accessible names

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:138-156`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:147-174`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:226-236`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:342-360`
- **macOS behaviour:** VoiceOver reads the '⋯' buttons as '⋯' ('midline horizontal ellipsis') and the colour dot as '●'. The size slider, preview editor, picker list and picker search have no name. Picker rows expose only the family name: no native name, sample or 'in your font' tag. Section headers are exposed as ordinary, enabled list items.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/a11y_probe.py (QAccessible.queryAccessibleInterface): "pane ⋯ menu button role=ButtonMenu name='⋯'", "card colour dot role=StaticText name='●'", "size slider role=Slider name=''", "preview editor role=EditableText name=''", "picker list role=List name=''", "item 0 ListItem 'ALL CHINESE FONTS · 11' state.disabled 0", "item 1 ListItem '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface'".
- **Recommendation:** Call setAccessibleName on: the pane ⋯ ('More'), the card ⋯ (f'More actions for {family}'), the dot (f'Colour {n}'), size_slider ('Preview size'), preview ('Preview text'), search ('Search fonts'), view ('Fonts'). In PickerRows.data, return Qt.ItemDataRole.AccessibleTextRole = '{family}, {native}, {tag}' and AccessibleDescriptionRole = the sample. For header rows, return a heading-like text such as 'Section: All Chinese fonts, 11'.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** a11y.py (offscreen): pane ⋯ role Button name '⋯'; size slider name ''; preview EditableText name ''; picker list name ''; the search has only its placeholder as description; picker children: 'ALL CHINESE FONTS · 10' is an ordinary ListItem, followed by '.Hiragino Sans GB Interface' and '.LastResort' (the hidden fonts from UI-1).

### UI-M3: A leading '.' in the font name is accepted: the default save file becomes a hidden dotfile, and the installed family would be hidden by macOS

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:105-125`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:758-769`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:305-308`
- **macOS behaviour:** When a hidden system font becomes the main font (the default row, UI-1), the automatic name is '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI Forged' and the proposed save path is ~/Documents/.Apple SD Gothic NeoI Forged-Regular.ttf, a dotfile Finder hides. A name the user types, like '.MyFont', also passes validity. macOS treats '.'-prefixed families as private (QFontDatabase.isPrivateFamily True; CoreText and NSFontManager list 0 such families), so a font installed under that name would not appear in any app's font menu.
- **Evidence:** Offscreen ForgeModel with the '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI' face added: family '.Apple SD Gothic NeoI Forged', output /Users/…/Documents/.Apple SD Gothic NeoI Forged-Regular.ttf, validity ''. After set_family('.MyFont'): validity '', output …/Documents/.MyFont-Regular.ttf. cocoa1.py: isPrivateFamily('.Apple SD Gothic NeoI') = True. The CT/NSFontManager family lists have 0 dot-prefixed names. Nothing was written to disk.
- **Recommendation:** Strip leading dots and spaces in smart.default_family_name and default_output_path. Make the spec/model validation reject a family name that starts with '.', with a message like 'A font name can't start with a dot.', on all platforms. Fixing UI-1 removes the automatic path; this guard covers typed names.

### UI-M4: Custom theme ignores macOS 'Increase contrast'; hairline borders are 1.2–1.4:1

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:99-125`, `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:169-232`
- **macOS behaviour:** With Accessibility › Display › Increase contrast on, native controls get strong borders, but the app's fixed tokens and full-palette override keep border/border_soft at about 1.2–1.4:1 against the surface (WCAG non-text contrast wants 3:1). ThemeManager reacts only to colorSchemeChanged. Qt 6.10+ exposes the preference: QStyleHints.accessibility().contrastPreference() and contrastPreferenceChanged are present in PySide6 6.11.2, and libqcocoa reads accessibilityDisplayShouldIncreaseContrast.
- **Evidence:** contrast.py: LIGHT border_soft vs surface 1.28, border 1.41, faint 1.74; DARK border_soft 1.17, border 1.38. `QGuiApplication.styleHints().accessibility().contrastPreference()` -> ContrastPreference.NoPreference; attributes include contrastPreferenceChanged. `strings libqcocoa.dylib | grep accessibilityDisplayShouldIncreaseContrast` finds a hit. grep for contrast in ui/ finds only theme.contrast_ratio. Qt docs: https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/qaccessibilityhints.html (Qt 6.10). I did not toggle the system setting.
- **Recommendation:** Add high-contrast variants of LIGHT and DARK (border/border_soft/faint at ≥3:1, muted text at ≥4.5:1). In ThemeManager, pick them when hasattr(styleHints, 'accessibility') and contrastPreference() == Qt.ContrastPreference.HighContrast, and connect contrastPreferenceChanged to apply(). Guard for PySide6 < 6.10 or raise the minimum version.

### UI-M7: The macOS save-only flow gives no native way to install the result (no 'Open in Font Book') and the README text does not match the UI

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:32-33`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:34`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:239`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:310-313`, `README.md:42`
- **macOS behaviour:** Because install.is_supported() is False on darwin, the bar hides 'Save a copy…', the primary button reads 'Save…', and the idle text says 'Saves a .ttf file you can install yourself.' After saving, the only follow-up is 'Show file' (which opens the folder). The README tells mac users to 'use Open folder and install by hand', a label that does not exist. The native Mac install path, opening the .ttf in Font Book, which shows its own Install button, is never offered. It needs no private API and does not touch the user's Fonts folder silently.
- **Evidence:** install.py:32-33 `return sys.platform.startswith("win")`; action_bar.py:239 `self.save_button.setVisible(supported and not building)`; action_bar.py:270 shows IDLE_SAVE_TEXT when not supported; README.md:42 '(use Open folder and install by hand)'. I did not open Font Book (that would launch an app).
- **Recommendation:** Until a real CoreText install exists (install dimension), add an 'Open in Font Book' link next to 'Show in Finder' once the state is SAVED on darwin. Implement it with NSWorkspace.openURLs:withApplicationAtURL:… for com.apple.FontBook, or `subprocess.run(['open', '-b', 'com.apple.FontBook', path])`. Change IDLE_SAVE_TEXT on darwin to 'Saves a .ttf you can install with Font Book.' and fix the README wording.

### UI-9: File dialogs are app-modal panels whose titles macOS never shows, and nothing guarantees a .ttf extension on save

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:339-347`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:305-308`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:192-200`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:122-125`
- **macOS behaviour:** The static functions QFileDialog.getExistingDirectory and getSaveFileName open native NSOpenPanel/NSSavePanel as app-modal windows, not as sheets on the main window. Their captions ('Add font folder', 'Save the font as') are not shown, so Add folder… opens a generic 'Open' panel. Qt's documentation says the filter is ignored by the native save dialog on macOS, and BuildController.save_copy uses the returned path as is, so a name typed without '.ttf' may be saved without an extension. The default location is ~/Documents/<Family>-<Style>.ttf, which is fine on a Mac.
- **Evidence:** https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/qfiledialog.html: 'On Windows and macOS, this static function uses the native file dialog'; 'Note that the macOS native file dialog does not show a title bar.'; for getSaveFileName: 'On macOS, with its native file dialog, the filter argument is ignored.' https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/qt.html#WindowType-enum (Qt::Sheet): the recommended way is QWidget::setWindowModality() or QDialog::open(). build.py:197-199 passes Path(path) through unchanged. Not run on screen, because the native panel would appear on the user's display.
- **Recommendation:** Use dialog instances. dlg = QFileDialog(self); dlg.setWindowModality(Qt.WindowModality.WindowModal) to get a sheet; dlg.setFileMode(Directory) and dlg.setLabelText(QFileDialog.DialogLabel.Accept, 'Add Folder') for Add folder…; for saving, dlg.setAcceptMode(AcceptSave), dlg.setDefaultSuffix('ttf'), dlg.setNameFilter('TrueType font (*.ttf)'), dlg.selectFile(model.output); then dlg.open() with a fileSelected slot. As a safety net, append '.ttf' in save_copy when Path(path).suffix.lower() != '.ttf'. Remember the last folder used for saves in settings.json.
- **Verifier (partially):** Sheet/title part confirmed from Qt source (qtbase 6.11 qcocoafiledialoghelper.mm:175-181): only WindowModal plus a parent gives beginSheetModalForWindow; QDialog::exec (the static getters) runs an app-modal runModal. Refuted: the .ttf extension claim. The same file's computeAllowedContentTypes (l.523-547) turns the '*.ttf' filter into allowedContentTypes (UTType.typeWithFilenameExtension('ttf') = public.truetype-ttf-font, checked via pyobjc), so NSSavePanel appends and enforces .ttf. selectedFiles (l.405-423) also applies the filter's suffix when none was typed. The Qt doc sentence that the filter is ignored is outdated for Qt 6.x. Default ~/Documents is fine. What remains is sheet vs app-modal, and Add folder… showing a generic 'Open' button: polish.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Use QFileDialog instances with setWindowModality(Qt.WindowModal) and open() plus a fileSelected slot for sheets. For Add folder, setLabelText(QFileDialog.DialogLabel.Accept, 'Add Folder') (Qt maps it to NSSavePanel.prompt). The '.ttf' safety net in save_copy is harmless but not needed on macOS.

### UI-14: macOS overlay scroll bars cover the right edge of the selected picker row

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:41-44`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:359-369`
- **macOS behaviour:** macOS transient scroll bars sit on top of the viewport instead of taking up width, so the selected row's rounded box and border run underneath the scroller.
- **Evidence:** work/ui/shots/zoom_picker_row.png (crop of cocoa_light_picker_chinese.png): the scroll bar overlaps the selected row's right border. The picker itself uses ScrollPerPixel (picker.py:366), and style SH_ItemView_ScrollMode = 1 (per-pixel) on mac, so trackpad scrolling is smooth.
- **Recommendation:** On darwin, add a right inset to the row box equal to the scroll bar's extent (style().pixelMetric(PM_ScrollBarExtent), roughly 8–10 px), or call view.setViewportMargins(0, 0, 8, 0).
- **Verifier (partially):** scroll.py (cocoa): NSScroller.preferredScrollerStyle = 1 (overlay); the list's scroll bar reports SH_ScrollBar_Transient = 1. The overlay scroller appears only while scrolling and then fades, exactly like NSTableView/Finder rows, which also run under it. The auditor's grab shows it permanently only because a never-shown widget never runs the fade. With legacy scrollers (mouse attached, or 'Always show'), Qt reserves the width and nothing overlaps. This is native behaviour, not a defect.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** No change needed. Adding a permanent 8–10 px right inset would waste space and look less native.

### UI-15: Window-management extras expected on a Mac: Advanced as an inspector window, drag-and-drop of fonts, quit-on-close decision

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:286-292`, `fontplayground/ui/advanced.py:167-171`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405-415`
- **macOS behaviour:** Closing the main window quits the app (quitOnLastWindowClosed is True by default); for a single-window utility this is the right choice and matches System Settings and Calculator. The Advanced window is a non-modal QDialog with a 'Close' button and no ⌘W. You cannot drop font files or folders onto the window or the Dock icon. Full screen through the green button works by default.
- **Evidence:** cocoa_probe: quitOnLastWindowClosed True. advanced.py:167 QDialogButtonBox(Close). grep finds no dragEnterEvent/dropEvent/QFileOpenEvent. Qt default menu probe shows no Window menu items.
- **Recommendation:** Keep quit-on-close. Give the Advanced window a ⌘W QAction (StandardKey.Close) and on darwin drop the Close button; store its geometry. Accept drops of .ttf/.otf/.ttc/.otc files and folders on the main window, handing them to add_folder or extra_dirs. Once bundled, handle QEvent.FileOpen so fonts dropped on the Dock icon are added the same way.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** From qtbase 6.11 qcocoawindow.mm:1677-1678 and 681-682: a Qt::Dialog such as AdvancedDialog already becomes a QNSPanel without a minimize button, which behaves like an inspector. QDialog closes on Esc and ⌘. (StandardKey.Cancel). ⌘W does nothing. grep finds no dragEnterEvent/dropEvent/FileOpen handling.

### UI-16: No Windows font names are hard-coded in the package; README examples should use macOS fonts' native names

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `README.md:26`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:409-410`, `tests/test_smart.py:25-33`, `tests/test_advanced.py:55-59`, `tests/test_build.py:331-338`
- **macOS behaviour:** The package has no Windows font defaults or fallbacks. The only one is Segoe UI in app.py, and it is guarded by sys.platform.startswith('win'), so macOS uses SF. Tests use 'Segoe UI' and 'Microsoft YaHei' only as labels on fake faces, which is harmless. The picker's native names on this Mac are 苹方-简 (PingFang SC), 冬青黑体简体中文 (Hiragino Sans GB), ヒラギノ角ゴシック (Hiragino Sans), Apple SD 산돌고딕 Neo, 宋体-简 (Songti SC) and 黑体-简 (Heiti SC). Helvetica and Menlo have no native name.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn -i 'segoe|yahei|dengxian|malgun|yu gothic|consolas|微软|等线|맑은' fontplayground → only app.py:410 (win-guarded) plus comments. work/ui/native_names.py: "PingFang SC local=('苹方-简','蘋方-簡')", "Hiragino Sans GB local=('冬青黑体简体中文',…)", "Hiragino Sans local=('ヒラギノ角ゴシック',)", "Apple SD Gothic Neo local=('Apple SD 산돌고딕 Neo',)", "Songti SC local=('宋体-简','宋體-簡')".
- **Recommendation:** Make README.md:26 platform-neutral, for example '(苹方-简 / 微软雅黑, ヒラギノ角ゴシック, Apple SD 산돌고딕 Neo / 맑은 고딕)'. The macOS equivalents of the Windows fonts are PingFang SC for Microsoft YaHei, Hiragino Sans for Yu Gothic/Meiryo, Apple SD Gothic Neo for Malgun Gothic, and Menlo/SF Mono for Consolas; use them if you add default suggestions later. Keep in mind that PingFang lives under /System/Library/AssetsV2 (the paths finding covers this).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** grep -i for segoe|yahei|dengxian|malgun|yu gothic|consolas|meiryo|simsun|arial|calibri in fontplayground finds only app.py:410 (inside the win guard), a docstring and comments. Native names on this Mac from the scan: Hiragino Sans ('ヒラギノ角ゴシック'), Hiragino Sans GB ('冬青黑体简体中文', …), Apple SD Gothic Neo ('Apple SD 산돌고딕 Neo'), Songti SC ('宋体-简'). PingFang SC is not in the default folders.

### UI-17: IME, trackpad scrolling and Retina glyph rendering need no changes (verified)

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:730-746`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:159-180`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:366`
- **macOS behaviour:** While an input method is composing (Pinyin, Kotoeri), Qt's cocoa backend sends no QKeyEvent. The picker's eventFilter therefore cannot steal ↑/↓/Return/Esc from the candidate window, and search runs on commit, because QLineEdit.textChanged ignores preedit. The preview is a plain QTextEdit with a QSyntaxHighlighter, so macOS IME preedit and undo work as-is. List scrolling is per-pixel, which gives smooth trackpad scrolling with momentum. Grabs at devicePixelRatio 2 render the CJK and Latin samples crisply.
- **Evidence:** Qt 6.11 src/plugins/platforms/cocoa/qnsview_keys.mm (fetched to work/ui/qnsview_keys.mm) lines 83-141: shortcut handling happens only `if (m_composingText.isEmpty())`, the event goes to `[self.inputContext handleEvent:nsevent]`, and a key event is sent only `if (m_sendKeyEvent && m_composingText.isEmpty())`. misc_probe: SH_ItemView_ScrollMode = 1 and QTableWidget defaults to ScrollPerPixel. Grabs: 2560×1640 pixmaps at dpr 2.0 (work/ui/shots/zoom_picker_row.png).
- **Recommendation:** No code change needed. Add a manual check to the mac release checklist: type 'nihao' with Pinyin into the picker search and the preview, press ↑/↓ in the candidate window, and confirm the picker selection does not move.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Re-read the auditor's copy of qnsview_keys.mm lines 83-141: shortcuts are handled only when m_composingText is empty, the event goes to inputContext handleEvent, and a QKeyEvent is sent only if m_sendKeyEvent && m_composingText.isEmpty(). A plain ↑/↓ that the IME does not consume comes back through doCommandBySelector, so the picker still navigates. I did not run a real IME.

### UI-M5: Pointing-hand cursor on ordinary buttons is a web idiom on macOS

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:112`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:142`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:165`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:171`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:180`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:234`, `fontplayground/ui/advanced.py:127`, `fontplayground/ui/widgets.py:90`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:338`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:204`
- **macOS behaviour:** The 'Colour by font' chip, 'Sample ▾', both ⋯ buttons, the missing-characters button, 'Show all scripts', the Disclosure button and the add button all switch the cursor to a pointing hand. Native macOS controls keep the arrow; the hand is reserved for hyperlinks.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn PointingHandCursor fontplayground/ui/*.py -> 10 sites (listed in files). Only action_bar.py:204 (_link) and picker.py:338 (Back link) are link-styled.
- **Recommendation:** Keep PointingHandCursor only on #link-styled buttons (action_bar._link, picker back_button) and remove it from chips, tool buttons and Disclosure, at least on darwin.

### UI-M6: No trackpad pinch or ⌘-scroll zoom on the preview; the slider is the only way to resize text

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:159-322`, `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:147-153`
- **macOS behaviour:** Mac users expect pinch-to-zoom (and ⌘+/⌘−, covered in UI-2) on a text preview canvas, as in Preview, Font Book and TextEdit. MixPreview ignores ZoomNativeGesture. QTextEdit's own Ctrl(⌘)+wheel zoom applies only to read-only editors, so it does nothing either.
- **Evidence:** zoom.py (offscreen): slider, preview pt and document pt are (30, 30, 30.0) before, (30, 30, 30.0) after a ⌘+wheel QWheelEvent on the viewport, and (30, 30, 30.0) after a QNativeGestureEvent ZoomNativeGesture of 0.5.
- **Recommendation:** In MixPreview, override viewportEvent: on QEvent.NativeGesture with ZoomNativeGesture, and on wheel events with ControlModifier, emit a zoomRequested(delta) signal. PreviewPane maps it onto size_slider (clamped 10–96) so the value is persisted like a slider change.

### UI-M8: No Dock attention when a long build finishes while the app is in the background

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:274-284`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py`
- **macOS behaviour:** A build takes about 15 s (README:30), and users switch apps meanwhile. Mac apps bounce the Dock icon or badge it when a background task completes; this app does nothing, so the user has to check back.
- **Evidence:** grep for QApplication.alert/alert(/setBadge in fontplayground returns nothing. MainWindow._show_built (app.py:274) is the completion hook.
- **Recommendation:** In the resultReady/resultFailed handlers, when QGuiApplication.applicationState() != Qt.ApplicationActive, call QApplication.alert(self). On macOS this bounces the Dock icon; on Windows it flashes the taskbar. It is one line and cross-platform.

#### Data points

- Cocoa backend (hidden, activation policy Prohibited): style 'macos', app font .AppleSystemUIFont 13 pt, logicalDpi 72, DPR 2.0, FixedFont Menlo 10 pt, SmallestReadableFont 9 pt, QToolButton class font 10 pt, QHeaderView 11 pt, QListView 12 pt
- Offscreen backend is not representative on mac: Fusion style, 'Sans Serif' 9 pt, 96 dpi, DPR 1
- Screen: 1352x878 pt, availableGeometry 1352x764 at y=29; app default 1280x820 plus a 28 pt title bar = 848 pt (84 pt too tall = BAR_HEIGHT)
- Font scan of the macOS default dirs + AssetsV2: 888 faces, 422 families, 74 dot-prefixed hidden families, 1 unreadable file (NISC18030.ttf), 4.9 s with a cold cache
- Hidden families listed in the picker: latin 13/199, chinese_s 2/39, japanese 5/67, korean 2/8, arabic 6/36, symbols 20/95, any 64/411; CoreText/NSFontManager list 221 families, 0 dot-prefixed
- Picker Latin list: 169/199 families drawn by installed name (fonts.system_font), 30 need a file load (checked in 0.20 s)
- Theme: setColorScheme(Light/Dark) -> NSApp.appearance Aqua/DarkAqua; unsetColorScheme -> nil (effective follows system: DarkAqua on this Mac)
- System colours (dark): controlAccentColor #007aff, Qt palette Accent #0a60ff / Highlight #314f78, selectedTextBackgroundColor #3f638b, windowBackgroundColor #323232; app overrides with #4c8ef5 accent and highlight, and a #1b1e24 window
- App menu without a bundle: process 'python'; items 'Hide __main__.py' / 'Quit __main__.py' with argv0 from `python -m`; fixed by QApplication(['Font Playground', …]) or by setting CFBundleName via pyobjc
- Delegate text on mac vs 96-dpi Windows: header/tag 8.5 pt -> 9 px vs 11.3 px; picker name 13 pt -> 13 px vs 17.3 px; sample 18 pt -> 18 px vs 24 px; card name 17 pt -> 17 px vs 22.7 px
- Screenshots (dpr 2) in <audit-scratch>/ui/shots/: cocoa_{light,dark}_{empty,picker_latin,picker_latin_left,picker_chinese,recipe_two,advanced}.png, zoom_combo_spin.png, zoom_picker_row.png, zoom_toolbar_right.png, zoom_dark_picker.png
- Experiment scripts are in <audit-scratch>/ui/ (render.py, theme_probe.py, font_probe.py, a11y_probe.py, menu_probe*.py, quit_probe.py, accent_probe.py, hidden_count.py, ct_families.py)
- Transparency note: the first cocoa render run called MainWindow.show_advanced(), which called AdvancedDialog.show() for about 0.3 s before it was closed. The app was running with the Prohibited activation policy, but a window may still have flashed on screen. The harness was then changed to build the dialog without show(), and no later run showed any widget.

## Packaging, distribution, dev tooling, tests, docs + full Windows-assumption sweep (`TOOLING-*`)

The code base is mostly portable. There is no multiprocessing, no subprocess, no __file__ or package-data lookups and no CRLF line endings, the index is all LF, and every test builds its own fixture fonts. Because of this, freezing it for macOS was easy: a PyInstaller onedir .app built in about 9 s, is 86 MB (36.7 MB as a DMG), and starts headless in about 0.25 s with a full scan of 795 faces in about 2.7 s. The blocking gap is distribution. The repo has no packaging, no icon, no version or Info.plist metadata and no CI. Gatekeeper rejects an ad-hoc bundle, and the only signing identity on this Mac is "Apple Development", not the "Developer ID Application" certificate needed for notarization. The sweep found one real macOS-specific hazard beyond the known install.py, paths.py and theme gaps: the code overwrites font files in place with shutil.copyfile. On Windows, file locks prevent this; on macOS it makes any process that has the font mapped crash with SIGBUS (reproduced). The Windows system-font name-collision check silently never matches on macOS. For dev tooling, the README is Windows-only and still points at the gitignored run.bat. The dependency on the full PySide6 package pulls in 885 MB of unused Qt Addons; PySide6-Essentials is enough (the test suite passes with it). Two tests fail on POSIX; a PureWindowsPath fix is verified. Recommended target: one cross-platform codebase on master, uv for dev, a PyInstaller onedir .app signed with Developer ID and Hardened Runtime (no entitlements needed), notarized and stapled, shipped as a DMG and optionally as a Homebrew cask. Build arm64 by default with a minimum of macOS 13, which Qt 6.11 requires. The Mac App Store is not realistic for this app.

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| TOOLING-1 | major | partially | No distributable macOS build: nothing builds a .app, ad-hoc builds are rejected by Gatekeeper, and there is no Developer ID identity |
| TOOLING-7 | major | confirmed | The system-font name-collision check is Windows-only: system_fonts_dir() is 'C:\Windows/Fonts' on macOS and _is_under is case- and symlink-sensitive |
| TOOLING-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | The README's bootstrap steps fail on a stock Mac: there is no `python`, `python3` is 3.9.6 with pip 21.2.4, and zsh treats `.[dev]` as a glob |
| TOOLING-M2 | major | (verifier-found) | PyInstaller run from an editable dev env (pip -e / uv sync) silently builds a broken .app that lacks the fontplayground package |
| TOOLING-2 | minor | partially | Font files are overwritten in place (shutil.copyfile); on macOS this crashes any process that has the font mapped (SIGBUS) |
| TOOLING-3 | minor | confirmed | The test suite is red on macOS: test_paths builds Windows paths with pathlib.Path on a POSIX host |
| TOOLING-4 | minor | confirmed | No CI at all; nothing checks Windows and macOS together |
| TOOLING-5 | minor | confirmed | Depends on the full PySide6 package; 885 MB of Qt Addons are installed but never used |
| TOOLING-6 | minor | partially | README and the dev workflow are Windows-only (.venv\Scripts, run.bat, 'Windows-only' claims, a missing 'Open folder' action) |
| TOOLING-8 | minor | confirmed | Bundle metadata: PyInstaller defaults give version 0.0.0 and no LSMinimumSystemVersion, category or copyright; the version string is duplicated |
| TOOLING-9 | minor | confirmed | No app icon; macOS 26+ needs an Icon Composer .icon compiled into Assets.car to draw it full size |
| TOOLING-10 | minor | confirmed | Architecture: builds are arm64-only by default; universal2 needs a universal2 Python and forced universal2 wheels |
| TOOLING-11 | minor | confirmed | LGPL/Qt compliance: the bundle ships no license texts or notices |
| TOOLING-12 | minor | partially | Packager choice: PyInstaller onedir recommended; onefile .app is on the way out; Briefcase, Nuitka and py2app compared |
| TOOLING-13 | minor | confirmed | Mac App Store is not realistic; distribute a Developer ID DMG and optionally a Homebrew cask, which must be notarized |
| TOOLING-14 | minor | confirmed | A bundled app has no diagnostics (stderr only, no excepthook), and closing can block the GUI for up to 180 s |
| TOOLING-15 | minor | confirmed | User-facing text and shortcuts assume Windows (Ctrl+Z, 'Windows already has…', 'no admin rights needed') |
| TOOLING-16 | minor | confirmed | Tests: install coverage is Windows-only, macOS behaviour is not asserted, and offscreen runs at 96 dpi so macOS rendering is never exercised |
| TOOLING-17 | minor | confirmed | pyproject hygiene: license table deprecated (hard deadline 2027-02-18), no platform markers, no dependency groups, console-script entry for a GUI |
| TOOLING-18 | minor | confirmed | Scanner and catalog assumptions: case-folded dedup, an Apple bitmap font reported as unreadable, and absolute paths as keys for MobileAsset fonts whose paths change |
| TOOLING-19 | minor | confirmed | Point-based font sizes render about 25% smaller relative to px-based stylesheet chrome on macOS (72 vs 96 logical dpi) |
| TOOLING-M3 | minor | (verifier-found) | TCC privacy prompts: the bundled app rescans user-added folders at every launch and defaults saves to ~/Documents; no purpose strings, and ad-hoc rebuilds reset the grants |
| TOOLING-20 | polish | confirmed | Missing macOS shell conventions: 'Show file' opens the folder instead of revealing the file, and there is no native menu bar, Settings…, or About |
| TOOLING-21 | polish | confirmed | Git migration: clone into the empty target dir, keep one cross-platform codebase, delete the stale branch, add .gitattributes and .gitignore entries |
| TOOLING-22 | polish | partially | Naming heuristics tuned for Windows vendors and filenames |
| TOOLING-23 | polish | confirmed | Sweep: remaining Windows and platform touchpoints that are benign on macOS (inventory for completeness) |
| TOOLING-M4 | polish | (verifier-found) | On macOS, QFontDatabase.addApplicationFont rejects relative paths (returns -1); FontLoader passes paths through unresolved |
| TOOLING-M5 | polish | (verifier-found) | Settings, forge_last.json and the 5.4 MB catalog.json are written non-atomically |

### TOOLING-1: No distributable macOS build: nothing builds a .app, ad-hoc builds are rejected by Gatekeeper, and there is no Developer ID identity

- **Severity:** major (auditor: blocker); effort M; files: `pyproject.toml:1-30`, `README.md:5-20`, `.gitignore:6-7`
- **macOS behaviour:** The only way to run is 'python -m fontplayground' from a venv. A PyInstaller .app I built is signed ad hoc, and Gatekeeper rejects it ('spctl -a -vv -t exec' prints 'rejected'). A downloaded copy would be blocked, and on current macOS the only way past that is System Settings > Privacy & Security > Open Anyway. The keychain has only an 'Apple Development' identity, which cannot be used for notarized distribution outside the App Store.
- **Evidence:** Built in a private venv: `pyinstaller --noconfirm --clean --windowed --onedir --name "Font Playground" --osx-bundle-identifier io.github.kciceblue.fontplayground launch.py` finished in 8.9 s. Result: 86M .app, Mach-O thin arm64, `codesign -dv` shows flags=0x2(adhoc), `spctl -a -vv -t exec` prints 'rejected'. `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` lists 1 identity: "Apple Development: <redacted>"; there is no Developer ID Application. Hardened-runtime test: `codesign --force --deep --options runtime -s -` followed by launch failed with 'Failed to load Python shared library ... mapping process and mapped file (non-platform) have different Team IDs'. That is ad-hoc library validation, because ad-hoc signatures have no Team ID. Re-signed with only com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation, it ran (exit 0), including a PyObjC CoreText block callback (CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs, process scope): 'callbacks=[(0, True)] visible families=[FP Probe Font]'. So neither allow-unsigned-executable-memory nor allow-jit is needed. Tools present: xcrun notarytool, stapler, actool (Xcode 27.0), and iconutil. PyInstaller docs (https://pyinstaller.org/en/stable/feature-notes.html): 'When codesign identity is specified, PyInstaller also turns on hardened runtime'.
- **Recommendation:** 1) Join the paid Apple Developer Program if needed and create a 'Developer ID Application' certificate. 2) Add packaging/macos/: launch.py (from fontplayground.__main__ import main; main()) and a committed FontPlayground.spec. Use onedir with EXE(console=False, codesign_identity=os.environ.get('CODESIGN_ID'), entitlements_file=None) and BUNDLE(name='Font Playground.app', bundle_identifier='io.github.kciceblue.fontplayground', icon=..., version=__version__, info_plist={...}; see TOOLING-8). 3) Release script: pyinstaller spec, then hdiutil create -format ULFO with an /Applications symlink, then codesign the dmg, then `xcrun notarytool submit FontPlayground.dmg --keychain-profile fp-notary --wait`, then `xcrun stapler staple FontPlayground.dmg`, then `spctl -a -vv -t open --context context:primary-signature` as a check. 4) Ship no entitlements with Developer ID, since all binaries then share one Team ID.
