Use disable-library-validation only in local ad-hoc test signing, never in releases. 5) Add a hidden `--self-test` flag in __main__ (start, scan, quit, as in my FP_SMOKE launcher) so CI can smoke-test the frozen bundle headless.
- **Verifier (partially):** DISCLOSURE FIRST: during verification I accidentally ran my probe launcher without its FP_FORGE guard and without QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen. The real GUI started at about 18:39:42, and a Font Playground window was probably visible on the user's screen until I killed it with pkill at about 18:44. It created ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground/catalog.json (5.4 MB). The directory did not exist before; its birth time matches my launch. I moved it to work/verify-tooling/accidental-config/FontPlayground, which restores the prior state. The user's project dir and the repo clone were not touched. Every later run forced offscreen.

What I reproduced: plutil shows 9 Info.plist keys. `spctl -a -vv -t exec` prints 'rejected'. `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` lists only 'Apple Development: <redacted>'. On my own build, `codesign --force --deep --options runtime -s -` makes launch fail with '[PYI-91241:ERROR] Failed to load Python shared library ... libpython3.12.dylib'. Adding only com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation makes it run: 'QT ok faces 795'. PyInstaller 6.22.3 utils/osx.py:421 and :430 add --options=runtime and --timestamp whenever an identity is given, so the auditor's claim is right.

Why it is not a blocker: the locally built bundle has no com.apple.quarantine xattr (`xattr` shows only com.apple.provenance), so Gatekeeper never assesses it and it …
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Treat this as 'distribution to others', not 'usable on macOS'. Order of work: (1) Build a local .app for your own use; ad-hoc signing is fine, and signing with the existing Apple Development identity keeps the code identity stable, so TCC grants survive rebuilds (see TOOLING-M3). (2) For release, sign with a Developer ID Application certificate. Submit the .app first (`ditto -c -k --keepParent 'Font Playground.app' app.zip`, then `xcrun notarytool submit app.zip --wait`, then `xcrun stapler staple 'Font Playground.app'`). Then build the DMG, sign, notarize and staple it. That way a copy dragged out of the DMG also carries a ticket and passes Gatekeeper offline. Build the bundle from a non-editable install (TOOLING-M2).

### TOOLING-7: The system-font name-collision check is Windows-only: system_fonts_dir() is 'C:\Windows/Fonts' on macOS and _is_under is case- and symlink-sensitive

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:60-62`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:65-72`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:83-87`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:142-179`, `tests/test_build.py:74-80`, `tests/test_build.py:298-338`, `tests/test_action_bar.py:54`, `tests/test_action_bar.py:226-233`, `tests/test_action_bar.py:292`
- **macOS behaviour:** conflict() never returns 'block' for Apple fonts, so a forged 'Helvetica' or 'PingFang SC' could be installed and shadow a system family once install is ported. Today it is inert because can_install() is False. os.path.normcase is a no-op on POSIX, so paths that differ only in case (APFS is case-insensitive by default), or through /tmp→/private/tmp and /var→/private/var symlinks, are not recognised as 'under' the folder.
- **Evidence:** Private venv: `build.system_fonts_dir()` returns PosixPath('C:\\Windows/Fonts'). `_is_under('/private/tmp/x/a.ttf', '/tmp/x')` returns False. `_is_under('~/library/fonts/a.ttf', '~/Library/Fonts')` returns False. The tests hard-code the Windows wording 'Windows already has a font called …' (test_build.py:300, 338; test_action_bar.py:233) and the kwarg windows_fonts_dir.
- **Recommendation:** Replace system_fonts_dir() with the installer backend's protected_dirs(). On macOS that is [/System/Library/Fonts, /Library/Fonts, /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font*] with user_fonts_dir ~/Library/Fonts. Better still, ask CoreText whether a family name already exists (CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames, or the family's font descriptors), which also covers downloadable fonts. Implement _is_under with Path.resolve() on both sides, and casefold when the volume is case-insensitive (os.pathconf(path, 'PC_CASE_SENSITIVE') == 0 on macOS). Rename the kwarg to protected_dirs and parametrise the tests' expected wording per platform (e.g. SYSTEM_HAS_TEXT = 'macOS already has…').
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced: build.system_fonts_dir() returns PosixPath('C:\\Windows/Fonts'). _is_under('/private/tmp/x/a.ttf', '/tmp/x') returns False, and so does _is_under('~/library/fonts/a.ttf', '~/Library/Fonts'). This is inert today because can_install() is False, and it becomes a real gap once the macOS installer lands. One correction to the recommendation: os.pathconf(path, 'PC_CASE_SENSITIVE') raises 'ValueError: unrecognized configuration name' on Python 3.12 for macOS, because the name is not in os.pathconf_names. Only the raw constant works: os.pathconf('/Users', 11) returns 0, per _PC_CASE_SENSITIVE 11 in sys/unistd.h:154.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Do not compare strings. Implement _is_under by walking Path(path).parents and testing os.path.samefile(parent, folder), or by comparing (st_dev, st_ino). That handles case-insensitive APFS, /tmp→/private/tmp and other symlinks on every OS, and keeps working on Windows. For the macOS 'block' rule, prefer asking CoreText whether the family exists (CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames) over a hard-coded list of protected dirs. Rename windows_fonts_dir to protected_dirs, and take the message text from the installer backend.

### TOOLING-M1: The README's bootstrap steps fail on a stock Mac: there is no `python`, `python3` is 3.9.6 with pip 21.2.4, and zsh treats `.[dev]` as a glob

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `README.md:7`, `README.md:10-14`, `README.md:47-48`, `pyproject.toml:12`
- **macOS behaviour:** `python -m venv .venv` fails with 'python: command not found', because macOS ships only python3. Using `/usr/bin/python3` instead gives Python 3.9.6 from the Xcode CLT, below requires-python >=3.10. Its pip 21.2.4 then fails `pip install -e .` with a misleading 'Directory cannot be installed in editable mode ... editable mode currently requires a setuptools-based build' instead of a Python-version error. In zsh (the default shell) `pip install -e .[dev]` stops at 'zsh: no matches found: .[dev]' before pip runs.
- **Evidence:** $SHELL=/bin/zsh. `zsh -c 'command -v python'` prints nothing. `python3 --version` gives 3.9.6 (/usr/bin/python3). `/usr/bin/python3 -m venv v39 && zsh -c 'v39/bin/pip install -e .[dev]'` gives 'zsh:1: no matches found: .[dev]'. With quoting, `pip install --no-deps -e '.[dev]'` gives 'ERROR: File "setup.py" or "setup.cfg" not found. Directory cannot be installed in editable mode ... (A "pyproject.toml" file was found, but editable mode currently requires a setuptools-based build.)' (pip 21.2.4, python 3.9). The run was on a copy of the repo.
- **Recommendation:** Give the README a macOS section built on uv: install uv (`brew install uv`, or the official installer), then `uv sync` (uv fetches a suitable CPython; commit `.python-version` = 3.12), `uv run fontplayground` and `uv run pytest`. In any pip fallback, quote the extras (`pip install -e '.[dev]'`) and state that the Xcode CLT's /usr/bin/python3 (3.9) is too old. The same text works in PowerShell.

### TOOLING-M2: PyInstaller run from an editable dev env (pip -e / uv sync) silently builds a broken .app that lacks the fontplayground package

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:22-26`, `README.md:11`, `README.md:47`
- **macOS behaviour:** setuptools' PEP 660 editable install puts an import-hook finder (__editable___fontplayground_0_1_0_finder.py) into site-packages, and PyInstaller's module graph cannot follow it. The build reports 'Build complete!', but the .app is 20 MB with no app code, and launching it fails with ModuleNotFoundError. In a windowed bundle nobody sees the traceback. The auditor avoided this only because their venv used a non-editable install (direct_url.json has dir_info {} with no editable flag). Both the documented workflow (`pip install -e .`) and `uv sync`, which installs the project editable by default, fall into this trap.
- **Evidence:** In my editable venv: `pyinstaller --windowed --onedir --name FPEditable launch.py` ends with 'INFO: Build complete!'. warn-FPEditable.txt contains 'missing module named fontplayground'. `du -sh dist/FPEditable.app` gives 20M. Running it headless gives 'ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'fontplayground''. Rebuilding the same thing with `--paths <repo>` gives an 86M bundle that runs a full forge ('FORGE ok frozen=True ... t=31.9s').
- **Recommendation:** In the committed spec, set Analysis(pathex=[str(Path(SPECPATH).parents[1])]) (the repo root) so the build works from any env. Alternatively the release job runs `uv sync --no-editable --group build`, or builds from a wheel. Add a post-build check that runs the bundle's `--self-test`, and a quick guard such as `test -d 'dist/Font Playground.app/Contents/Frameworks/fontplayground' || grep -q 'missing module named fontplayground' build/*/warn-*.txt && exit 1`, so CI cannot publish an empty app.

### TOOLING-2: Font files are overwritten in place (shutil.copyfile); on macOS this crashes any process that has the font mapped (SIGBUS)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:160-173`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:652-659`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:218-227`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:661-675`, `tests/test_model.py:57-58`
- **macOS behaviour:** shutil.copyfile truncates and rewrites the existing inode. If the target is a font that CoreText or fontd, another app, or this app's QFontDatabase has mmapped, those processes get SIGBUS when they next touch the old pages. The trigger is 'Save a copy…' over a font in ~/Library/Fonts, which is the manual-install route the README suggests for macOS. A macOS port of install._copy_into would hit the same thing. The 'locked → write <stem>-<n>.ttf instead' fallback and the unlink retries exist for Windows sharing violations and never trigger on macOS.
- **Evidence:** Script in work/tooling/mmapdemo: a child process mmaps a 1 MB 'Installed.ttf', the parent runs shutil.copyfile(new_1KB, Installed.ttf), and the child then reads offset 900000. Output: 'copyfile keeps inode: True size now 1000', 'reader output: <none> returncode: -10 (-10 = SIGBUS)'. The same test with copy to temp plus os.replace gives 'os.replace gives new inode: True', and the old mapping stays valid.
- **Recommendation:** Add a helper fontplayground/fsutil.py: atomic_copy(src, dest) copies to dest.with_name(f'.{dest.name}.{uuid4().hex}.tmp') in the SAME directory, fsyncs, then calls os.replace(tmp, dest). Use it in model.save_to and every installer write. For the macOS installer, unregister the old URL first (CTFontManagerUnregisterFontURLs), write the new file under a new name if possible, os.replace it, then register. Keep the Windows numbered-name and retry workarounds behind `if sys.platform == 'win32'` or in the Windows backend only.
- **Verifier (partially):** The generic POSIX mechanism is real: a raw mmap reader gets SIGBUS when the file is truncated under it. But I could not reproduce any crash through the macOS font stack this app actually uses. Test 1 (Qt offscreen, which on macOS uses the CoreText font DB): QFontDatabase.addApplicationFont loaded a 23,278,008-byte copy of Arial Unicode.ttf, and I drew Latin text. shutil.copyfile(Arial.ttf) then truncated it in place ('inode now 3142131 size 773236'). Drawing CJK afterwards worked (161 ink samples) and the process exited 0. Test 2 (CoreText): CTFontManagerRegisterFontsForURL at process scope, the same truncation, then CTFontCreatePathForGlyph for 13 CJK glyphs returned 13 paths with exit 0. Both hold old font data in memory rather than lazily mapped pages. So 'crashes any process that has the font mapped' is not demonstrated for CoreText/Qt clients, and fontd was not testable because persistent install is forbidden. The auditor also missed that build.py:263 uses copyfile, though that copy goes into the staging dir and is harmless. The atomic write is still worth doing: a crash or full disk mid-write otherwise leaves a truncated .ttf, and it is Windows-safe, because os.replace onto a file the font system holds raises PermissionError, so the numbered-name fallback still fires.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Keep the atomic_copy helper (a same-directory temp file, fsync, then os.replace) and use it for model.save_to and the installers. Frame it as partial-write and crash safety, not as a SIGBUS fix. Note that os.replace replaces a symlink at dest instead of writing through it, and drops dest's xattrs and ACL; for a user-chosen save path, resolve the symlink first. Use the same helper for the JSON writes (TOOLING-M5).

### TOOLING-3: The test suite is red on macOS: test_paths builds Windows paths with pathlib.Path on a POSIX host

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort S; files: `tests/test_paths.py:6-9`, `tests/test_paths.py:18-19`, `fontplayground/paths.py:16-21`, `fontplayground/paths.py:31-32`
- **macOS behaviour:** 2 failed, 339 passed, 3 skipped. Path(r'C:\Win') / 'Fonts' becomes PosixPath('C:\\Win/Fonts'), which is not equal to Path(r'C:\Win\Fonts').
- **Evidence:** `pytest -q` gives 'AssertionError: ... PosixPath('C:\\Win/Fonts') != PosixPath('C:\\Win\\Fonts')' and 'PosixPath('C:\\L/FontPlayground') == PosixPath('C:\\L\\FontPlayground')'. I patched my private copy to compare `[PureWindowsPath(d) for d in dirs] == [PureWindowsPath(r'C:\Win\Fonts'), PureWindowsPath(r'C:\Users\u\AppData\Local\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts')]`, and the same for config_dir. Result: `pytest tests/test_paths.py` gives '4 passed in 0.02s'. The full suite was stable across 3 runs (9.45 to 9.59 s each).
- **Recommendation:** In tests, compare Windows results as PureWindowsPath (a 3-line change, verified). Optionally make paths.py return PureWindowsPath/PurePosixPath chosen by the `platform` argument, so the function is correct on any host. Extend test_mac_and_linux_dirs to cover the new macOS dirs (AssetsV2 font assets, per the fonts dimension) and a macOS config and cache dir.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced in my Essentials venv: '2 failed, 339 passed, 3 skipped'. I applied the PureWindowsPath comparison to test_windows_dirs and test_config_dir, and got 'tests/test_paths.py 4 passed in 0.02s'. The bug is in the tests only; product behaviour on macOS is unaffected, so it is minor, not major.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Fix only the tests: wrap both sides in PureWindowsPath (verified). Do NOT make paths.py return PureWindowsPath or PurePosixPath. The callers need concrete paths: scanner.py:22-25 calls Path(d).is_dir() and d.rglob(), and app.py:300 builds Path objects. Pure paths lack those methods, and a runtime Path would be needed anyway.

### TOOLING-4: No CI at all; nothing checks Windows and macOS together

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort M; files: `.github (absent)`, `tests/conftest.py:7`
- **macOS behaviour:** Nothing stops a Windows-only change from breaking macOS, or the other way round. The macOS failures in TOOLING-3 went unnoticed for this reason.
- **Evidence:** `ls .github` gives 'No such file or directory'. `git ls-files` lists 71 files with no workflow. conftest.py:7 already does os.environ.setdefault('QT_QPA_PLATFORM', 'offscreen'); the suite passed in my private venv without setting the variable (339 passed, 9.5-10 s).
- **Recommendation:** Add .github/workflows/ci.yml with a matrix of os [windows-latest, macos-latest (arm64), ubuntu-latest] and python ['3.10', '3.12']. Steps: actions/checkout, astral-sh/setup-uv (python-version from the matrix), `uv sync --group dev`, `uv run pytest -q -ra`. On Ubuntu, first run `sudo apt-get install -y libegl1 libxkbcommon0 libfontconfig1 libdbus-1-3` for Qt offscreen. No env var is needed because conftest sets offscreen. Add .github/workflows/release-macos.yml on tags, running on macos-latest: uv sync --group build, then pyinstaller packaging/macos/FontPlayground.spec, then run the bundle with --self-test, then import the Developer ID p12 into a temporary keychain from secrets, then codesign, notarytool with an App Store Connect API key (--key/--key-id/--issuer), staple, and upload the DMG to the GitHub Release. If Intel builds are wanted, add a job on an Intel runner (see TOOLING-10).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** `ls .github` gives 'No such file or directory'. conftest.py:7 sets QT_QPA_PLATFORM with os.environ.setdefault. Missing CI is a process and quality gap, and nothing on macOS is broken by its absence, so I rate it minor. The matrix idea is sound.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same matrix, plus: the release job must build from a non-editable install (`uv sync --no-editable`, or pathex in the spec) and must fail the job if the frozen --self-test fails. I showed that an editable env produces a 'Build complete!' 20 MB bundle that dies with ModuleNotFoundError (TOOLING-M2). Drive CI with `uv run` and python scripts rather than a justfile, because just is not installed on this Mac or on stock Windows.

### TOOLING-5: Depends on the full PySide6 package; 885 MB of Qt Addons are installed but never used

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:16`
- **macOS behaviour:** `pip install -e .` pulls pyside6 + pyside6_essentials + pyside6_addons (universal2 fat binaries). The app only imports QtCore, QtGui and QtWidgets.
- **Evidence:** From the RECORD files in the shared venv: pyside6_essentials 343 MB, pyside6_addons 885 MB, pyside6 meta 5 MB. `grep -rhoE 'from PySide6[.a-zA-Z]* import'` finds only QtCore (12), QtGui (9) and QtWidgets (9). I changed my private copy to "PySide6-Essentials>=6.8". uv installed it in 3.3 s, the venv is 384 MB in total, and the tests gave '2 failed, 339 passed, 3 skipped' (only the test_paths failures).
- **Recommendation:** Change the dependency to "PySide6-Essentials>=6.8" and keep the comment about setColorScheme. The bundle size barely changes, because PyInstaller hooks collect only the modules that are imported. The change saves about 885 MB per dev venv and CI cache.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** From RECORD sizes: pyside6_addons 885 MB, essentials 343 MB, pyside6 meta 5 MB. The code imports only QtCore (12), QtGui (9) and QtWidgets (9). My venv with only PySide6-Essentials is 378 MB and the tests give '2 failed, 339 passed, 3 skipped' (only the test_paths failures). My PyInstaller bundle from the Essentials venv is 86 MB, the same size as the auditor's bundle from full PySide6. So the change has no user-visible effect; it only saves disk in dev and CI. Minor.

### TOOLING-6: README and the dev workflow are Windows-only (.venv\Scripts, run.bat, 'Windows-only' claims, a missing 'Open folder' action)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: major); effort S; files: `README.md:7`, `README.md:9-14`, `README.md:18-20`, `README.md:30`, `README.md:34`, `README.md:42`, `README.md:46-51`, `.gitignore:9`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:172-199`
- **macOS behaviour:** The documented commands (`.venv\Scripts\pip`, `.venv\Scripts\python`) do not exist on macOS. The README says macOS 'Install for me is Windows-only' and 'the automatic theme following is untested there'. README:42 tells macOS users to 'use Open folder and install by hand', but the ⋯ menu has only 'Open settings folder' (app.py:197). The launcher (run.bat) is gitignored, so macOS has no equivalent.
- **Evidence:** README.md:11 is `.venv\Scripts\pip install -e .`, README.md:19 is `.venv\Scripts\python -m fontplayground`, and README.md:47-48 are `.venv\Scripts\pip install -e .[dev]` and `.venv\Scripts\python -m pytest -q`. .gitignore:9 is `run.bat`. app.py:174-198 builds a menu with Rescan fonts, Add folder…, Start over, Advanced…, Theme and Open settings folder, and no 'Open folder'. uv is at /opt/homebrew/bin/uv; `uv venv --python 3.12` plus `uv pip install` of the project took 3.3 s in my experiment.
- **Recommendation:** Standardise on uv for every OS. Commit `.python-version` (3.12) and `uv.lock`. Add `[dependency-groups] dev = ["pytest>=8", "pytest-qt>=4.4"]` and `build = ["pyinstaller>=6.10"]` (PEP 735), and keep `[project.optional-dependencies].dev` for plain pip users. The README then says `uv sync` / `uv run fontplayground` / `uv run pytest`, with a pip fallback that uses `python -m pip` and no Scripts or bin paths. Add a Makefile or justfile (targets: run, test, app, dmg, notarize, clean) that works on macOS and Linux, and keep run.bat or add `just run` for Windows. Rewrite the platform sections per OS: install location, theme follows System Settings > Appearance, and the Windows 'no admin rights' wording.
- **Verifier (partially):** Overstated in places. README.md:14 already says 'On macOS/Linux use `.venv/bin/pip`'. The summary claims the README 'still points at the gitignored run.bat', but `grep run.bat README.md` finds nothing; run.bat appears only in .gitignore:9. The claim that 'Open folder' is missing is confirmed: app.py:172-199 has no such action, and the closest is the action bar's 'Show file' (action_bar.py:310-313). The real macOS failures, which the auditor did not find, are in TOOLING-M1. `just` is not installed here (`which just` gives 'just not found'); /usr/bin/make is GNU Make 3.81.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Adopt uv as proposed, but use `uv run` plus small python scripts (packaging/build_macos.py and so on), or a Makefile, instead of a justfile. Those work on stock macOS and Windows without extra tools. Rename 'Open folder' in README:42 to the real 'Show file' button.

### TOOLING-8: Bundle metadata: PyInstaller defaults give version 0.0.0 and no LSMinimumSystemVersion, category or copyright; the version string is duplicated

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:7`, `fontplayground/__init__.py:1`, `tests/test_package.py:4-5`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:407-408`
- **macOS behaviour:** Info.plist has only 9 keys. CFBundleShortVersionString is '0.0.0', CFBundleVersion and LSMinimumSystemVersion are missing, and so are LSApplicationCategoryType and NSHumanReadableCopyright. The bundled Qt needs macOS 13 (minos 13.0) while the executable declares minos 11.0, so on macOS 11/12 the app would crash at launch instead of being refused by Finder. The app also never calls setOrganizationName, setApplicationDisplayName or setWindowIcon.
- **Evidence:** `plutil -p Info.plist` shows CFBundleDisplayName, CFBundleExecutable, CFBundleIconFile=icon-windowed.icns, CFBundleIdentifier, CFBundleName, CFBundlePackageType, CFBundleShortVersionString='0.0.0', NSHighResolutionCapable=true. `otool -l` gives: Frameworks/QtCore minos 13.0, MacOS/Font Playground minos 11.0, libpython3.12 minos 11.0. PyPI: pyside6-6.11.2-cp310-abi3-macosx_13_0_universal2.whl. Qt 6.11 docs (https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/macos.html): target platform 'macOS 13 or higher (including macOS 26)'.
- **Recommendation:** Single-source the version: `[project] dynamic = ["version"]` with `[tool.setuptools.dynamic] version = {attr = "fontplayground.__version__"}`. In the spec, set BUNDLE(version=__version__, info_plist={'CFBundleShortVersionString': v, 'CFBundleVersion': build_number, 'LSMinimumSystemVersion': '13.0', 'LSApplicationCategoryType': 'public.app-category.graphics-design', 'NSHumanReadableCopyright': '© 2026 kciceblue. MIT License.', 'NSHighResolutionCapable': True, 'CFBundleIconName': 'AppIcon', 'CFBundleDevelopmentRegion': 'en'}). Leave out NSRequiresAquaSystemAppearance so dark mode works. Optionally add CFBundleDocumentTypes for .ttf/.otf/.ttc as Viewer, together with QFileOpenEvent handling. In main(), call app.setOrganizationName('kciceblue') and app.setApplicationDisplayName('Font Playground').
- **Verifier (confirmed):** On my own build, `otool -l` shows QtCore minos 13.0 and the FPVerify executable minos 11.0. The auditor's Info.plist has CFBundleShortVersionString 0.0.0 and no LSMinimumSystemVersion. Qt 6.11 docs (https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/macos.html) say 'macOS 13 or higher (including macOS 26)'. The claim that it would 'crash at launch on 11/12' is plausible but I did not test it.

### TOOLING-9: No app icon; macOS 26+ needs an Icon Composer .icon compiled into Assets.car to draw it full size

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405-415`
- **macOS behaviour:** The bundle uses PyInstaller's generic icon-windowed.icns. Running unbundled, the Dock shows the python3.12 executable's generic icon and name. On macOS 26/27, apps that ship only a legacy .icns are drawn shrunken inside a system squircle plate.
- **Evidence:** Info.plist has CFBundleIconFile => icon-windowed.icns and there are no icon assets in `git ls-files`. `xcrun actool --version` gives short-bundle-version 27.0, and /usr/bin/iconutil is present. Refs: https://successfulsoftware.net/2025/09/26/updating-application-icons-for-macos-26-tahoe-and-liquid-glass/ and https://mjtsai.com/blog/2025/08/08/separate-icons-for-macos-tahoe-vs-earlier/: Tahoe reads CFBundleIconName (Assets.car) and older systems read CFBundleIconFile (.icns).
- **Recommendation:** Design the icon in Icon Composer (Xcode 26+) as packaging/macos/AppIcon.icon. Compile it with `xcrun actool AppIcon.icon --compile build/icons --platform macosx --minimum-deployment-target 13.0 --app-icon AppIcon --output-partial-info-plist build/icons/partial.plist` and copy Assets.car into Contents/Resources (via the BUNDLE datas or a post-build step). Set CFBundleIconName=AppIcon. Also produce AppIcon.icns with iconutil from an .iconset and pass it as BUNDLE(icon=...) so CFBundleIconFile covers macOS 13-15. Call app.setWindowIcon for unbundled runs.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** I made a minimal Icon Composer AppIcon.icon (icon.json plus one 1024 px PNG layer) and ran `xcrun actool AppIcon.icon --compile out --platform macosx --minimum-deployment-target 13.0 --app-icon AppIcon --output-partial-info-plist out/partial.plist` (actool 27.0). The one call produced AppIcon.icns (48,922 B), Assets.car (1,671,992 B) and a partial plist with CFBundleIconFile=AppIcon and CFBundleIconName=AppIcon. The auditor's separate iconutil/.iconset step is therefore unnecessary.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Run one actool call on packaging/macos/AppIcon.icon. Pass the generated AppIcon.icns to BUNDLE(icon=...) for macOS 13-15, add Assets.car as a data file that lands in Contents/Resources, and set CFBundleIconName='AppIcon' in info_plist. Keep app.setWindowIcon for unbundled runs.

### TOOLING-10: Architecture: builds are arm64-only by default; universal2 needs a universal2 Python and forced universal2 wheels

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:13-17`
- **macOS behaviour:** PyInstaller targets the running interpreter. uv's CPython 3.12 is arm64-only, so the .app is thin arm64 and does not run on Intel Macs. `--target-arch universal2` fails because uv/pip picked the arm64-only unicodedata2 wheel.
- **Evidence:** `file python3.12` gives 'Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64' (uv cpython-3.12.14-macos-aarch64). The bundle: `lipo -info` reports QtCore, the main executable, libpython3.12 and _pathops.abi3.so as 'Non-fat ... arm64'. The universal2 attempt raised 'IncompatibleBinaryArchError: .../unicodedata2.cpython-312-darwin.so is not a fat binary!', and its WHEEL tag is cp312-cp312-macosx_11_0_arm64. PyPI wheels: skia_pathops-0.9.2-cp310-abi3-macosx_10_9_universal2, pyside6/essentials/shiboken6 6.11.2 macosx_13_0_universal2, fonttools cp312 macosx_10_13_universal2 and x86_64, unicodedata2 cp312 universal2 and arm64, pyobjc 12.2.2 cp312 universal2. So every dependency has universal2 wheels.
- **Recommendation:** Ship arm64 by default: this Mac runs macOS 27, and Apple said macOS 26 is the last release for Intel Macs. If Intel users matter, either (a) build a second x86_64 DMG on an Intel CI runner, or (b) build universal2 with the python.org universal2 installer and install dependencies with `pip install --only-binary=:all: --platform macosx_11_0_universal2 --target ...`, or use Briefcase, which fetches universal2 wheels by default, then pass `--target-arch universal2`. Expect roughly 1.8x the bundle size for universal2.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** In my venv: python is 'Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64' (uv cpython-3.12.14-macos-aarch64). Wheel tags: unicodedata2 is cp312-cp312-macosx_11_0_arm64; skia_pathops, fonttools and pyside6_essentials/shiboken6 are universal2. `lipo -info` on the bundle's libpython3.12.dylib and QtCore reports 'Non-fat ... arm64'. unicodedata2 is pulled in only by the fonttools[unicode] extra. The app's script detection is fontTools.unicodedata.script, which uses fontTools' own Scripts data (fontTools/unicodedata/__init__.py:9-17 only tries unicodedata2 for its star import). textutil.py:18 uses stdlib unicodedata.category.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Ship arm64 only. If universal2 is ever needed, first consider dropping the [unicode] extra from `fonttools[unicode]`, after checking that the stdlib Unicode version is fine for textutil.is-control checks. That removes the only arch-specific wheel, so a python.org universal2 interpreter plus `--target-arch universal2` would build without forcing platform tags.

### TOOLING-11: LGPL/Qt compliance: the bundle ships no license texts or notices

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `README.md:55`, `LICENSE`
- **macOS behaviour:** The .app contains no LGPL/GPL text, no Qt third-party notices and no source offer. The PySide6 wheels do not ship license files either, so PyInstaller copies none.
- **Evidence:** `find 'Font Playground.app' -iname '*licen*' -o -iname '*copying*'` returns nothing. The pyside6_essentials dist-info contains only INSTALLER, METADATA, RECORD, REQUESTED, WHEEL, entry_points.txt and top_level.txt. METADATA says 'License: LGPL-3.0-only OR GPL-2.0-only OR GPL-3.0-only', and shiboken6 has the same. The bundle uses Qt as dynamic frameworks (Frameworks/PySide6/Qt/lib/QtCore.framework etc.), which keeps them replaceable as the LGPL requires. README.md:55 already asks for the Qt LGPL notices.
- **Recommendation:** Add packaging/licenses/ with LGPL-3.0, GPL-3.0 and the Qt/PySide6 attributions: Qt 6.11.2 third-party notices, fontTools MIT, skia-pathops BSD-3, unicodedata2 Apache-2.0, pyobjc MIT if used. Copy it to Contents/Resources/licenses via the spec datas. Add an About dialog, or an app-menu 'About Font Playground', that names the PySide6 and Qt versions, the LGPL, and a link to the Qt source (https://download.qt.io/official_releases/qt/). Keep onedir with dynamic frameworks, not static or onefile. Tell users that replacing a Qt framework breaks the code signature and that they can re-sign ad hoc with `codesign --force --deep -s -`.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** `find FPVerify.app -iname '*licen*' -o -iname '*copying*' -o -iname '*notice*'` finds nothing. The pyside6_essentials-6.11.2.dist-info folder holds only INSTALLER, METADATA, RECORD, REQUESTED, WHEEL, entry_points.txt and top_level.txt. METADATA says 'License: LGPL-3.0-only OR GPL-2.0-only OR GPL-3.0-only'. The bundle also ships libpython3.12.dylib, whose PSF licence the recommendation did not list.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same plan, plus the Python (PSF-2.0) licence for the bundled libpython, and the licences of whatever python-build-standalone statically links into it, in Contents/Resources/licenses.

### TOOLING-12: Packager choice: PyInstaller onedir recommended; onefile .app is on the way out; Briefcase, Nuitka and py2app compared

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:22-23`
- **macOS behaviour:** PyInstaller 6.22.3 plus pyinstaller-hooks-contrib 2026.7 bundled PySide6 6.11.2, fontTools, skia-pathops, unicodedata2 and pyobjc CoreText with no hidden imports or hooks. The code has no __file__, importlib.resources, multiprocessing or subprocess, so there are no freeze hazards. It still collects unused QtNetwork, QtDBus and QtSvg plus the tls/networkinformation plugins (about 3 MB).
- **Evidence:** Build: 8.9 s. .app 86 MB (88 MB with pyobjc). DMG (hdiutil ULFO) 36.7 MB, zip 35.1 MB. Largest pieces: libpython3.12.dylib 17 MB, QtWidgets.abi3.so 8.6 MB, QtGui.framework 8.0 MB. `grep -rn -E '__file__|importlib.resources|pkgutil|multiprocessing|subprocess|os.startfile|ctypes' fontplayground` (excluding install.py) finds nothing. PyInstaller changelog (https://pyinstaller.org/en/stable/CHANGES.html): onefile with macOS .app bundles 'will be blocked in v7.0'. Briefcase (https://briefcase.beeware.org/en/stable/reference/platforms/macOS/) makes DMG, zip or PKG, signs and notarizes by default, and uses universal2 wheels for universal builds. Nuitka supports --macos-create-app-bundle, --plugin-enable=pyside6, --macos-sign-identity and --macos-sign-notarization (https://github.com/Nuitka/Nuitka/issues/2232), compiles to C and builds slowly. py2app is setuptools-based and macOS-only, with weaker PySide6 support.
- **Recommendation:** Use PyInstaller onedir with a committed .spec, because it already works here and the same spec can serve Windows. Set excludes=['PySide6.QtNetwork', 'PySide6.QtDBus', 'PySide6.QtSvg'] and drop the tls/networkinformation plugins, then re-run the smoke test. Do signing, notarization and DMG steps in a script (TOOLING-1). Consider Briefcase only if you want a managed universal2, notarization and PKG pipeline and accept restructuring into its project layout. Skip py2app. Nuitka is an option later if startup or obfuscation matters; startup is already about 0.25 s to window.
- **Verifier (partially):** I verified that freezing works beyond the auditor's start-and-scan smoke test. My PyInstaller 6.22.3 onedir bundle ran a full forge headless: variable SFNS.ttf, CFF NotoSansJavanese.otf at weight 700 (synthetic bold through pathops), and a Hiragino Sans GB.ttc CJK subset. Output: 'FORGE ok frozen=True ... size=16774440 t=31.9s', against 32.7 s unfrozen. Correction: QtDBus is not unused. `otool -L QtGui.framework/Versions/A/QtGui` shows '@rpath/QtCore @rpath/QtDBus'. QtNetwork is linked by the qtuiotouch, networkinformation and tls plugins, and QtSvg by the qsvg and qsvgicon plugins. With --exclude-module for PySide6.QtNetwork, QtDBus and QtSvg, all three frameworks were still bundled, only the tls and networkinformation plugin dirs disappeared, the size went from 86 to 83 MB, and the app still scanned 795 faces. The auditor also missed the editable-install trap (TOOLING-M2).
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** PyInstaller onedir with a committed spec is right. Drop the claim that QtDBus is unused. The module excludes are optional and save about 3 MB. Always set pathex=[repo root] in the spec (or build from a wheel), and gate CI on the frozen self-test.

### TOOLING-13: Mac App Store is not realistic; distribute a Developer ID DMG and optionally a Homebrew cask, which must be notarized

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:22-23`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:299-347`
- **macOS behaviour:** Under App Sandbox the app could not scan ~/Library/Fonts, extra folders from settings.json, or arbitrary font files without a user-selected folder plus security-scoped bookmarks. Installing fonts outside the container means persistent CoreText registration with its own prompts and entitlement questions. Freezing Python and PySide6 under the sandbox and App Store review adds more friction. Homebrew now audits casks for signing and notarization and removes those that fail by September 2026.
- **Evidence:** Code: scanning walks absolute dirs (scanner.py:19-28), and extra_dirs are stored as plain strings in settings.json (app.py:339-347); a sandbox would need security-scoped bookmarks instead. Refs: Apple App Sandbox entitlement docs (https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Miscellaneous/Reference/EntitlementKeyReference/Chapters/EnablingAppSandbox.html). Homebrew: 'Casks without codesigning and notarization are now deprecated … removed … by September 2026' (https://github.com/orgs/Homebrew/discussions/7050, https://workbrew.com/blog/homebrew-5-0-0). Not tested empirically: the safety rules forbid persistent font registration.
- **Recommendation:** Distribute outside the App Store: a notarized DMG on GitHub Releases, and, once it is notarized, a Homebrew cask (own tap first, then homebrew/cask). Do not enable App Sandbox. Keep Hardened Runtime on, as notarization requires.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Homebrew 5.0.0 (https://brew.sh/2025/11/12/homebrew-5.0.0/) and https://github.com/orgs/Homebrew/discussions/7050 say casks failing Gatekeeper are deprecated and disabled by September 2026, and --no-quarantine is removed. That deadline has now passed (today is 2026-09-29); for example, the Flycast cask was disabled on 1 Sep 2026. The sandbox reasoning matches the code: extra_dirs are plain strings in settings.json (app.py:339-347), and scanning uses absolute rglob. I did not test the sandbox empirically.

### TOOLING-14: A bundled app has no diagnostics (stderr only, no excepthook), and closing can block the GUI for up to 180 s

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:282`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:329`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:38-39`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:380-402`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405-415`
- **macOS behaviour:** A .app launched from Finder or the Dock has no visible stderr, so logging.exception output (read-back failure, settings restore failure) and PySide6 slot tracebacks are lost. closeEvent calls model.cancel(wait_ms=COMBINE_WAIT_MS=180000) and scan_worker.wait(3000) on the GUI thread. On Cmd+Q during a build that shows the spinning cursor and can hold up logout or restart.
- **Evidence:** app.py:38 is `COMBINE_WAIT_MS = 180_000`, app.py:388 is `self.model.cancel(wait_ms=COMBINE_WAIT_MS)`, and app.py:282 and 329 are logging...exception(...). There is no logging.basicConfig or FileHandler anywhere (grep). I did not open a visible window to show the hang, per the safety rules.
- **Recommendation:** In main(), add a RotatingFileHandler at ~/Library/Logs/Font Playground/fontplayground.log (%LOCALAPPDATA% on Windows), `faulthandler.enable(file)`, and a sys.excepthook that logs and shows a QMessageBox. Add 'Open log folder' to the menu. On close, ask, hide the window, and quit when the worker's `cancelled` or `finished` signal arrives (QTimer poll or app.aboutToQuit), instead of blocking wait(). Or reduce COMBINE_WAIT_MS and let the daemon thread die with the process, since the result file is cleaned by clean_stale_results on the next start.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** From the code: app.py:38 sets COMBINE_WAIT_MS = 180_000, app.py:388 calls model.cancel(wait_ms=...) on the GUI thread, and model.cancel calls worker.wait(wait_ms) (model.py:648-650). No logging handler is configured anywhere. My forge of a CJK font took about 32 s in total, so one prepare stage can block ⌘Q for many seconds. This is not specific to macOS. I did not show the hang, because that needs a visible window.

### TOOLING-15: User-facing text and shortcuts assume Windows (Ctrl+Z, 'Windows already has…', 'no admin rights needed')

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/preview_pane.py:162`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:168`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:38`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:33`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:77-78`, `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:708-722`, `README.md:26`
- **macOS behaviour:** The Sample tooltip says 'Ctrl+Z brings yours back', but the macOS shortcut is ⌘Z (Qt maps the key but not the text). The block message names Windows. The idle hint 'no admin rights needed' is UAC wording. The picker handles Home/End but not ⌘↑/⌘↓. The short_path docstring uses a C:\ example, though the code itself uses os.sep and is fine.
- **Evidence:** preview_pane.py:162 is `setToolTip("Replace the text with a sample (Ctrl+Z brings yours back)")`. build.py:38 is `WINDOWS_HAS_TEXT = "Windows already has a font called …"`. action_bar.py:33 is `IDLE_TEXT = "Installs for your account only — no admin rights needed."`. Checked with QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen: `QKeySequence(QKeySequence.StandardKey.Undo).toString(NativeText)` returns '⌘Z'.
- **Recommendation:** Build shortcut text with QKeySequence(StandardKey.Undo).toString(QKeySequence.SequenceFormat.NativeText). Move the OS-specific strings into the installer backends, for example backend.system_name ('Windows' or 'macOS') and backend.idle_text ('Installs for your account only (~/Library/Fonts).' on macOS). In picker.eventFilter, map Ctrl+Up/Down (Qt reports ⌘ as ControlModifier on macOS) to Home/End.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** The strings are confirmed at preview_pane.py:162, build.py:38 and action_bar.py:33. Some citations are off: the picker key handling is at picker.py:730-746, not 708-722, and preview.py:168 is an unrelated setPlaceholderText. QKeySequence(StandardKey.Undo).toString(NativeText) returns '⌘Z' under both offscreen and the real cocoa QPA, which I checked without opening a window.

### TOOLING-16: Tests: install coverage is Windows-only, macOS behaviour is not asserted, and offscreen runs at 96 dpi so macOS rendering is never exercised

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `tests/test_install.py:12`, `tests/test_install.py:44-46`, `tests/test_install.py:80-88`, `tests/test_install.py:91-142`, `tests/conftest.py:7`, `tests/fakes.py:11-51`, `tests/test_model.py:57-58`, `tests/test_smart.py:26-33`, `tests/test_smart.py:129-149`, `tests/test_advanced.py:55-59`, `tests/test_languages.py:64`
- **macOS behaviour:** 3 tests are skipped ('per-user font install is Windows only'). test_user_fonts_dir_follows_localappdata asserts a Windows layout on every OS. test_not_supported_elsewhere monkeypatches sys.platform to 'linux' and will need updating once macOS is supported. Offscreen reports logicalDotsPerInch 96, but macOS Cocoa uses 72, so pt-based sizes are never tested as they render on macOS. The fake font names are all Windows ones (Segoe UI, Microsoft YaHei, JhengHei). The tests are hermetic, since fixtures are built by tests/fixtures.py, and timing is generous: waits of 1-60 s, 300 ms sleeps, and 3 full runs at 9.45-9.59 s each with no flakes.
- **Evidence:** `pytest -rs` gives 'SKIPPED [1] tests/test_install.py:91/122/139: per-user font install is Windows only'. Offscreen check: `QGuiApplication.primaryScreen().logicalDotsPerInch()` returns 96.0. Three consecutive runs gave '2 failed, 339 passed, 3 skipped in 9.52s / 9.59s / 9.45s'. FakeInstaller (fakes.py:11-51) already defines the installer contract: is_supported, user_fonts_dir, installed_path, is_forged, install_font_for_user, uninstall_font_for_user.
- **Recommendation:** Turn install into backends (install/windows.py, install/macos.py, install/unsupported.py) behind the FakeInstaller contract, and write contract tests parametrised over the fake and the current-OS backend. Make the macOS real-install test opt-in (for example FP_REAL_INSTALL=1), because it touches ~/Library/Fonts. By default, test the macOS backend with process-scope registration and a temporary target dir. Add fixtures with macOS naming (PingFang SC / 苹方-简, Hiragino Sans / ヒラギノ角ゴシック, Apple SD Gothic Neo) to the strip_vendor, default_family_name and picker search tests. Add a CI step that runs the frozen bundle with --self-test (the FP_SMOKE pattern). Update the Windows-specific comment at test_model.py:57-58.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** The skips and the 96 dpi reading are confirmed. Offscreen misrepresents the Mac more than the auditor says. With the same probe, offscreen reports appfont 'Sans Serif' 9pt (12 px, font-alias fallback) and dpr 1.0. Cocoa, run with QT_MAC_DISABLE_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION_TRANSFORM=1 so no Dock icon or window appears, reports '.AppleSystemUIFont' 13pt, logical dpi 72 and dpr 2.0. Offscreen screenshots taken on a Mac therefore show neither the real typeface nor the real sizes.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Also add an opt-in metrics test that runs under QT_QPA_PLATFORM=cocoa with QT_MAC_DISABLE_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION_TRANSFORM=1, creating no windows and only checking QFontInfo and QScreen values, so macOS sizing regressions show up locally. Keep offscreen for CI.

### TOOLING-17: pyproject hygiene: license table deprecated (hard deadline 2027-02-18), no platform markers, no dependency groups, console-script entry for a GUI

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:10`, `pyproject.toml:13-17`, `pyproject.toml:19-23`, `pyproject.toml:28-30`
- **macOS behaviour:** Building warns: 'SetuptoolsDeprecationWarning: `project.license` as a TOML table is deprecated … By 2027-Feb-18, you need to update your project'. There is nowhere to declare macOS-only dependencies such as pyobjc-framework-CoreText, if the macOS installer uses it.
- **Evidence:** `uv build --wheel` on my copy printed the warning twice. The pyobjc probe inside the PyInstaller bundle under Hardened Runtime worked. `import CoreText` takes about 0.12 s once cached, and the first import compiled for about 6 s (shown=6.56 s on the first unbundled run versus 0.38 s in the bundle).
- **Recommendation:** `license = "MIT"` plus `license-files = ["LICENSE"]`, with build requires setuptools>=77. If the CoreText route is chosen, add `"pyobjc-framework-CoreText>=11; sys_platform == 'darwin'"` (and Cocoa if NSWorkspace or NSURL is needed). Add classifiers for MacOS, Microsoft Windows, X11 Applications and Qt. Move the GUI entry to `[project.gui-scripts] fontplayground = ...`. Add [dependency-groups] dev and build (TOOLING-6). Under [tool.pytest.ini_options], add `addopts = "-ra"` and a `markers` entry for real_install.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** `uv build --wheel` reproduces 'SetuptoolsDeprecationWarning: `project.license` as a TOML table is deprecated ... By 2027-Feb-18'. This applies to every platform and is not a macOS gap.

### TOOLING-18: Scanner and catalog assumptions: case-folded dedup, an Apple bitmap font reported as unreadable, and absolute paths as keys for MobileAsset fonts whose paths change

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:27-28`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:170-197`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:102-103`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:73`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:84-91`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:681`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:95`
- **macOS behaviour:** (a) The dedup key is str(p.resolve()).lower(), so on a case-sensitive APFS volume two different files that differ only in case collapse into one. (b) Every stock Mac shows '1 file couldn't be read': /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf is an Apple bitmap font with 'bhed' instead of 'head', and face._face does font['head']. (c) Recipe keys and cache keys are absolute paths. PingFang and other downloadable fonts live under /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/<sha1>.asset/AssetData/, and that hash directory changes when Apple updates the asset, so forge_last.json silently drops those materials. (d) catalog.json is regenerable data stored in Application Support; Apple guidance puts caches in ~/Library/Caches. (e) _name_rank prefers Windows-platform name records ('what Windows itself shows'), which is fine for most fonts.
- **Evidence:** `scan(default_font_dirs())` gives 379 files, 795 faces, failed=[('/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf', "KeyError: 'head'")]. Cold scan: about 2.5 s unbundled and bundled (SMOKE shown=0.23s, scan_done=2.74-2.78s). `find /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8 -name 'PingFang*'` gives .../86ba2c91f017a3749571a82f2c6d890ac7ffb2fb.asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc. Point (a) comes from reading the code; this Mac has no case-sensitive volume, so it is untested.
- **Recommendation:** (a) Dedup on (st_dev, st_ino) from stat() instead of a lowercased string. (b) In _face, treat a missing 'head' ('bhed' or 'bdat' present) as outline='none' (unsupported, bitmap-only) instead of an error, or skip such files quietly. (c) Also store the PostScript name (name ID 6) and full name in to_settings(); on restore, fall back to matching by PostScript name and index when the path is gone. Coordinate with the fonts dimension, which adds AssetsV2 to the scan dirs. (d) On macOS put catalog.json in ~/Library/Caches/FontPlayground, and add paths.cache_dir().
- **Verifier (confirmed):** read_faces('/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/NISC18030.ttf') raises KeyError 'head'. The file's tables are GlyphOrder, OS/2, bdat, bhed, bloc, cmap, maxp, meta, name and post. The frozen scan also reports that file as its only failure. PingFang is at /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8/86ba2c91f0....asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc. The catalog.json the app writes on this Mac is 5.4 MB, which supports moving it to ~/Library/Caches. Point (a) about case-sensitive volumes is untestable here.

### TOOLING-19: Point-based font sizes render about 25% smaller relative to px-based stylesheet chrome on macOS (72 vs 96 logical dpi)

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/picker.py:44-46`, `fontplayground/ui/recipe.py:51`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:28`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:36`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:409-410`
- **macOS behaviour:** Stylesheets use px (font-size: 14px, 18px), while the delegate, cards and preview use pt (NAME_PT 13, SAMPLE_PT 18, preview 30 pt). On Cocoa, 1 pt = 1 logical px; on Windows, 1 pt = 1.33 px. The balance tuned on Windows shifts, and the preview default looks smaller. The Segoe UI 10 pt app font is set only on Windows, so macOS uses the system font, as it should.
- **Evidence:** Code: picker.py:44 `NAME_PT, NATIVE_PT, SAMPLE_PT = 13, 11, 18`; recipe.py:51 `NAME_PT, NATIVE_PT = 17, 12`; picker.py STYLE `QLabel#h1 { font-size: 18px; ...}`. Offscreen reports 96 dpi, so tests cannot detect this. I could not check it visually without opening a Cocoa window, which the safety rules forbid.
- **Recommendation:** Hand this to the UI dimension. Either express these sizes in px (QFont.setPixelSize) to match the stylesheets, or scale pt by QGuiApplication.primaryScreen().logicalDotsPerInch()/96. Review the preview default size on macOS. Add a manual screenshot checklist, because offscreen CI cannot catch it.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Now verified empirically without opening a window. The real cocoa QPA gives logicalDotsPerInch=72.0, 13pt→13 px, and 18 px→18.0 pt. Offscreen gives 96.0, 13pt→17 px, and 18 px→13.5 pt. So pt-sized picker, recipe and preview text (NAME_PT 13 and so on) is drawn at about 0.75× relative to the px-sized stylesheet chrome, compared with Windows. verified_empirically should be true.

### TOOLING-M3: TCC privacy prompts: the bundled app rescans user-added folders at every launch and defaults saves to ~/Documents; no purpose strings, and ad-hoc rebuilds reset the grants

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:299-300`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:169`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:339-347`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:122-125`
- **macOS behaviour:** Once it runs as its own .app, a start-up scan of an 'Add folder…' directory under ~/Desktop, ~/Documents or ~/Downloads, or on a removable or network volume, is subject to TCC. macOS asks 'Font Playground would like to access files in your Downloads folder' with no explanation, because the Info.plist has no NS*FolderUsageDescription. Ad-hoc-signed builds are identified by cdhash, so every rebuild looks like a new app and asks again. The NSSavePanel save to ~/Documents is fine, because it is user-selected. Run from Terminal, TCC charges Terminal instead, so the problem never shows up during development.
- **Evidence:** From the code: MainWindow.__init__ calls start_scan(use_cache=True) (app.py:169), which scans self.font_dirs plus settings['extra_dirs'] (app.py:299-300). add_folder stores arbitrary folders from QFileDialog.getExistingDirectory (app.py:339-347). Apple docs: NSDesktopFolderUsageDescription, NSDocumentsFolderUsageDescription, NSDownloadsFolderUsageDescription and NSRemovableVolumesUsageDescription (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/information-property-list/nsdocumentsfolderusagedescription). I did not trigger a TCC prompt, since that needs a Finder-launched app with a visible UI.
- **Recommendation:** Add purpose strings to BUNDLE info_plist, for example NSDocumentsFolderUsageDescription 'Font Playground reads fonts in folders you add and saves fonts you forge.', plus the Desktop, Downloads and RemovableVolumes variants. Sign local dev builds with the existing Apple Development identity rather than ad hoc, so grants persist across rebuilds. When a rescan of an extra folder fails with PermissionError, report it as 'no access to <folder>' in the scan result instead of silently dropping it.

### TOOLING-20: Missing macOS shell conventions: 'Show file' opens the folder instead of revealing the file, and there is no native menu bar, Settings…, or About

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:86-88`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:310-313`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:172-199`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:207-208`
- **macOS behaviour:** open_folder(parent) opens the parent in Finder without selecting the file. All commands live in a ⋯ popup. A macOS user expects an app menu with About, Settings… (⌘,) and Quit, plus File and View menus. Run unbundled, the menu bar and Dock say 'python3.12'.
- **Evidence:** action_bar.py:313 is `open_folder(str(Path(shown).parent))`. app.py:173-198 creates one QMenu attached to the preview's ⋯ button, and there is no QMenuBar anywhere (grep).
- **Recommendation:** Hand this to the UI dimension. On macOS, reveal with NSWorkspace.sharedWorkspace().activateFileViewerSelectingURLs_([url]), or run `open -R <file>` via QProcess.startDetached; on Windows use `explorer /select,<file>`. Add a QMenuBar with QAction.MenuRole (AboutRole, PreferencesRole for Theme, QuitRole); Qt moves these into the app menu on macOS.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** From the code: action_bar.py:310-313 calls open_folder(parent) through QDesktopServices, and there is no QMenuBar anywhere.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** `QProcess.startDetached('open', ['-R', path])` needs no PyObjC dependency and is enough; use NSWorkspace only if PyObjC is already a dependency.

### TOOLING-21: Git migration: clone into the empty target dir, keep one cross-platform codebase, delete the stale branch, add .gitattributes and .gitignore entries

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `.gitignore:1-18`, `(missing) .gitattributes`, `(missing) .python-version`, `(missing) uv.lock`
- **macOS behaviour:** ~/HF/fontplayground exists and is empty, so a clone works directly. The macOS clone has core.ignorecase=true and core.precomposeunicode=true. All tracked filenames are ASCII, so there are no NFC/NFD issues. The index is all LF (67 i/lf, 4 empty __init__.py files). origin/mixer-ui has a tree identical to master (pre-rebase hashes). The .gitignore already has .DS_Store, dist/, build/ and .venv/, but not *.dmg, .ruff_cache/ or .mypy_cache/.
- **Evidence:** `ls -la ~/HF/fontplayground` shows only . and .. `git diff --quiet master origin/mixer-ui` confirms 'mixer-ui tree identical to master'. `git ls-files --eol` gives 67 'i/lf w/lf' and 4 'i/none'. A non-ASCII filename grep found nothing. `git ls-files -s` shows 71 files, all mode 100644.
- **Recommendation:** `git clone https://github.com/kciceblue/fontplayground.git ~/HF/fontplayground`. Work on a branch such as `macos-native` and merge it back into master. Keep one codebase with platform backends, not a macOS fork, and keep Windows in CI. Delete origin/mixer-ui after confirming it is identical: `git push origin --delete mixer-ui`, which is the user's action. Add .gitattributes with `* text=auto eol=lf` and `*.ttf *.otf *.ttc *.otc *.icns *.car *.png binary`. Add *.dmg, *.zip, .ruff_cache/ and .mypy_cache/ to .gitignore, and keep committed: *.spec, .python-version, uv.lock and packaging/. .gitignore already ignores .claude/, so project-level Claude settings will not be committed. Change that if you want shared CLAUDE.md settings in the repo.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** `git rev-parse master^{tree} origin/mixer-ui^{tree}` gives 46e56116… for both, so the trees are identical. ~/HF/fontplayground is empty. core.ignorecase and core.precomposeunicode are true. `git ls-files --eol` gives 67 i/lf and 4 i/none. All filenames are ASCII.

### TOOLING-22: Naming heuristics tuned for Windows vendors and filenames

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:19`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:27`, `fontplayground/ui/smart.py:122-125`
- **macOS behaviour:** VENDOR_WORDS = {microsoft, ms, adobe, google} has no 'apple', so 'Apple SD Gothic Neo' plus 'Helvetica' gives 'Helvetica Apple SD Gothic Neo' (longer than 31 characters, falling back to '<Main> Forged'). The filename sanitiser strips the Windows-illegal set <>:"/\|?* on every OS, which is fine on macOS but allows a leading '.' (a hidden file). The ~/Documents default output is fine on macOS: the save goes through the native NSSavePanel via QFileDialog, which grants TCC access.
- **Evidence:** smart.py:19 is `VENDOR_WORDS = frozenset({"microsoft", "ms", "adobe", "google"})`, smart.py:27 is `_UNSAFE_FILENAME = re.compile(r'[<>:"/\\|?*\x00-\x1f]+')`, and smart.py:125 is `Path.home() / "Documents" / f"{stem}.ttf"`.
- **Recommendation:** Consider adding 'apple' to VENDOR_WORDS, with a test, since stripping it from 'Apple Color Emoji' is harmless. Also strip a leading '.' in default_output_path. No change is needed for the Documents default.
- **Verifier (partially):** The example is wrong. smart.default_family_name([Helvetica, Apple SD Gothic Neo]) returns 'Helvetica Apple SD Gothic Neo', which is 29 characters, within MAX_FAMILY_NAME=31, so there is no fallback. The fallback happens for 'Helvetica Neue' plus 'Apple SD Gothic Neo', which gives 'Helvetica Neue Forged'. The leading-dot point is verified: default_output_path('.hidden', 'Regular') returns ~/Documents/.hidden-Regular.ttf.

### TOOLING-23: Sweep: remaining Windows and platform touchpoints that are benign on macOS (inventory for completeness)

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:16-21`, `fontplayground/paths.py:31-32`, `fontplayground/paths.py:35`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:71`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:19-24`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:36-39`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:32`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:66-76`, `fontplayground/engine/forge.py:41`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:69-71`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:15-77`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:409-410`, `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:163-182`, `tests/test_picker.py:281-288`, `tests/test_advanced.py:146`, `tests/test_widgets.py:9`, `tests/test_action_bar.py:284-287`
- **macOS behaviour:** Inert or correct on macOS. WINDIR and LOCALAPPDATA env keys and the r'C:\Windows' default are used only on Windows paths. The ValueError 'different drives' branch in _is_under never fires on POSIX. RESULT_DIR = tempfile.gettempdir()/fontplayground resolves to the per-user /var/folders/…/T/fontplayground on macOS, which is fine; on Linux it is /tmp/fontplayground and would collide between users. forge.py uses TemporaryDirectory with unique names. merge.py writes Mac Roman (1,0,0) name records only when safe, which is correct. There is no multiprocessing and no subprocess; the only threads are QThreads doing pure fontTools and pathops work. The Segoe UI font (app.py:409) and theme following are known items in other dimensions. Test strings like 'C:/Fonts/bad.ttf', 'C:/out/Fixture.ttf', '~\\Documents' and 'Z:/elsewhere' are plain data and pass on POSIX.
- **Evidence:** `tempfile.gettempdir()` gives /var/folders/bz/kcz1wjc57xb4dw73ppdtnv1h0000gn/T. The full suite passes apart from test_paths (339 passed). The freeze-hazard grep came back clean (see TOOLING-12).
- **Recommendation:** No action for macOS. For Linux later, use paths.config_dir honouring XDG_CONFIG_HOME and a per-user RESULT_DIR (tempfile.mkdtemp or XDG_RUNTIME_DIR). Keep install.py's winreg, gdi32 and WM_FONTCHANGE code as the Windows backend (TOOLING-16).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** The inventory matches my reading. There is no os.replace, subprocess or multiprocessing in the package, and the tempfile paths are per-user on macOS.

### TOOLING-M4: On macOS, QFontDatabase.addApplicationFont rejects relative paths (returns -1); FontLoader passes paths through unresolved

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:24-28`
- **macOS behaviour:** Qt passes native paths to the CoreText font DB as a file URL (CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL). A relative path yields no descriptors, so family_for() returns None and the font draws in a fallback face. Absolute paths work. Every current caller passes absolute paths (scanned dirs, QFileDialog results, RESULT_DIR), so this is latent. It would surface with a hand-edited relative extra_dirs entry in settings.json, or a future CLI/open-file argument.
- **Evidence:** Offscreen, which uses CoreText on macOS: after copying Georgia.ttf to ./probe.ttf, `QFontDatabase.addApplicationFont('probe.ttf')` returns -1, while `addApplicationFont('/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Georgia.ttf')` returns 2 with ['Georgia']. The same happens for Arial and Arial Unicode.
- **Recommendation:** In FontLoader.family_for, load `str(Path(path).resolve())`, keeping the caller's key for the cache dicts. Also resolve extra_dirs when reading settings (app.py:300).

### TOOLING-M5: Settings, forge_last.json and the 5.4 MB catalog.json are written non-atomically

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:56-58`, `fontplayground/catalog/cache.py:61-63`
- **macOS behaviour:** path.write_text truncates the file, then writes it. A Force Quit, crash, power loss or full disk mid-write leaves a truncated JSON file. The readers then fall back to defaults (_read_json catches ValueError), so the user silently loses the last recipe or theme, or the whole catalog cache, which forces a roughly 2.7 s rescan. Force Quit or a logout while the app hangs in closeEvent (TOOLING-14) makes this more likely on macOS.
- **Evidence:** From the code: _write_json is `path.write_text(json.dumps(data, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')` (app.py:56-58), and CatalogCache.save does the same (cache.py:61-63). On this Mac the catalog.json the app wrote was 5,418,060 bytes. I did not reproduce a torn write.
- **Recommendation:** Route these writes through the same atomic helper proposed in TOOLING-2: a temp file in the same dir, then flush/fsync, then os.replace. The change is small and helps on every platform.

#### Data points

- Baseline: 339 passed, 3 skipped (Windows-only install tests), 2 failed (test_paths) in 9.45-9.59 s over 3 runs on Apple Silicon, macOS 27.0 (26A428); no flakes
- test_paths fix with PureWindowsPath: 4 passed in 0.02 s (private copy)
- PySide6 6.11.2 on-disk size: Essentials 343 MB, Addons 885 MB (unused), pyside6 meta 5 MB, shiboken6 1 MB; private venv with Essentials only: 384 MB total; test suite passes
- PyInstaller 6.22.3 onedir build: 8.9 s; .app 86 MB (88 MB with pyobjc CoreText); thin arm64; ad-hoc signed
- DMG (hdiutil ULFO): 36.7 MB; ditto zip: 35.1 MB
- Largest bundle items: libpython3.12.dylib 17 MB, QtWidgets.abi3.so 8.6 MB, QtGui.framework 8.0 MB, QtCore.framework 5.8 MB, QtWidgets.framework 5.8 MB, _pathops 3.2 MB
- Frozen app headless smoke (offscreen, empty cache): Python code start to window shown about 0.23 s; font scan done at about 2.74-2.78 s; wall clock about 3.0-3.3 s per run
- Scan of default macOS dirs: 379 files (213 ttf, 128 ttc, 38 otf), 795 faces, 1 failure (NISC18030.ttf KeyError 'head'); 2 extensionless files (HelveLTMM, TimesLTMM) ignored
- Minimum OS: QtCore.framework minos 13.0, PySide6 wheel tag macosx_13_0_universal2; the PyInstaller bootloader and libpython declare minos 11.0
- Hardened runtime plus ad-hoc: fails to load libpython (different Team IDs); plus disable-library-validation only: runs, and a PyObjC CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs block callback works, so no JIT or unsigned-memory entitlements are needed
- Gatekeeper: `spctl -a -vv -t exec` on the ad-hoc bundle gives rejected; keychain has only 'Apple Development: <redacted>', no Developer ID
- In-place overwrite demo: shutil.copyfile over an mmapped file keeps the inode and the reader dies with -10 (SIGBUS); os.replace gives a new inode
- The universal2 attempt failed: unicodedata2 installed as the arm64-only cp312 wheel; uv CPython 3.12.14 is arm64-only
- All runtime dependencies have universal2 wheels on PyPI (PySide6/Essentials/shiboken6 6.11.2, skia-pathops 0.9.2 abi3, fontTools 4.66.0 cp312, unicodedata2 18.0.0, pyobjc 12.2.2)
- Git: 40 commits, 71 tracked files all LF/ASCII, origin/mixer-ui tree identical to master, no .github, no .gitattributes; target ~/HF/fontplayground is empty
- setuptools build warns: project.license TOML table deprecated, must change by 2027-02-18
- tempfile.gettempdir() on macOS: /var/folders/bz/kcz1wjc57xb4dw73ppdtnv1h0000gn/T (per-user)
- Offscreen QPA logical DPI = 96 (Cocoa uses 72), so tests cannot see macOS pt-size rendering
- QKeySequence Undo NativeText on macOS: ⌘Z
- Experiment artifacts: <audit-scratch>/tooling/ (repo copy, venv, pyi/launch.py, pyi/dist/Font Playground.app, FontPlayground-arm64.dmg, mmapdemo/)

## Architecture options: cross-platform Qt vs fully native Swift (`NATIVE-*`)

The codebase has a clean, Qt-free fontTools engine (engine/ 812 lines + catalog/ 354 + paths 39 = 1,205 lines). It ran unmodified on this Mac: Georgia + PingFang HK built in 23 s, Georgia + Hiragino Sans GB in 28 s at a 704 MB peak. The layer above it is not portable as-is. ForgeModel (868 lines) imports QObject/QTimer/Signal, QFontDatabase (via ui.fonts) and QThread (via ui.workers), and 3,418 lines are Qt views styled with hard-coded custom stylesheets, so 72% of the source is Qt. I checked the hybrid design end to end: a compiled Swift CLI started a Python engine helper, streamed its JSON-lines progress, decoded the report, and drew the forged TTF with CoreText (process scope, no fallback). A full Swift rewrite is realistic for everything except merging. HarfBuzz hb-subset reproduced the fontTools subset closure exactly (41,005 glyphs) 57x faster, and CGPath stroke+union replaces skia-pathops. But no Swift, C or Rust library replaces fontTools.merge, and every engine option (fontTools, hb-subset) drops Apple AAT `morx` shaping, which on this Mac affects 189 of 921 installed faces. Recommendation, in phases: (1) ship a signed, notarized PySide6 .app with a small macOS platform layer (3.5–5 person-weeks). (2) Then draw a process boundary: a Qt-free model plus a JSON engine helper. (3) Build a SwiftUI/AppKit + CoreText shell on that helper (10–14 person-weeks). Rewrite the engine in Swift or Rust only if App Store or iPad distribution becomes a goal.

| ID | Verified severity | Verdict | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| NATIVE-6 | major | confirmed | AAT fonts: the preview shapes Apple morx fonts correctly but the forged result drops morx, and no engine option keeps it (189/921 faces here) |
| NATIVE-9 | major | confirmed | On macOS the catalog should come from CoreText in every option: the file scanner misses AssetsV2 fonts, lists hidden faces and picks non-English names for Apple fonts |
| NATIVE-M1 | major | (verifier-found) | Qt's preview shapes with its bundled HarfBuzz, not CoreText, so even the 'real built font' preview can differ from what every Mac app draws |
| NATIVE-1 | minor | confirmed | Layer map: the engine and catalog are Qt-free and portable; ForgeModel is logic but tied to Qt, so it is the seam to cut |
| NATIVE-2 | minor | partially | Option 1 (Qt-native-ish): keep PySide6 + fontTools, add a macOS platform layer, ship a signed/notarized .app |
| NATIVE-3 | minor | confirmed | Option 2 (hybrid): SwiftUI/AppKit + CoreText shell, fontTools engine kept as a bundled Python helper over JSON-lines |
| NATIVE-4 | minor | partially | Option 3 (full Swift rewrite incl. engine): subset/instancing/bold/reading are coverable; merge, UPEM scaling, CFF→glyf and GPOS building are not |
| NATIVE-5 | minor | unverifiable | Option 4 (Rust core + native shells): fontations covers read/write and repacking but not merging; the best choice if Windows, macOS and iPad must share one engine |
| NATIVE-7 | minor | confirmed | Run the engine out of process in every option: cancelling currently waits for the next stage (observed 17.7 s), and a build peaks at 700 MB inside the UI process |
| NATIVE-8 | minor | confirmed | Distribution constraints per option: App Sandbox cannot write ~/Library/Fonts, Python needs App Store patches, Qt is LGPL, so ship Developer ID + notarization first |
| NATIVE-11 | minor | partially | Recommendation: a phased path. Qt-native release first, then a process boundary, then a SwiftUI/CoreText shell; rewrite the engine only if needed |
| NATIVE-M2 | minor | (verifier-found) | CoreText descriptors cannot be mapped to the engine's (path, index) face key by position: no public TTC index, and variable TTCs expand into named instances |
| NATIVE-M3 | minor | (verifier-found) | CoreText applies optical size automatically; the engine pins opsz at the axis default, so a CoreText (or Qt-on-Mac) preview of an opsz font drifts from the result |
| NATIVE-M4 | minor | (verifier-found) | The recommended engine subprocess needs a frozen-app entry point: `python -m fontplayground.engine.cli` does not exist inside a packaged .app |
| NATIVE-M5 | minor | (verifier-found) | pyproject pulls the full PySide6 meta-package (885 MB of unused Addons) although only QtCore/QtGui/QtWidgets are used; the Qt 6.11 wheels require macOS 13+ |
| NATIVE-M6 | minor | (verifier-found) | iPadOS (and probably sandboxed App Store builds) cannot install a font generated at runtime: CTFontManager installs only fonts shipped in the bundle or on-demand resources |
| NATIVE-10 | polish | confirmed | Cheap speed-up that needs no rewrite: HarfBuzz subsetting inside the Python helper matches the fontTools closure 57x faster |
| NATIVE-M7 | polish | (verifier-found) | Native-only opportunity: session-scope registration lets users try the forged font in other Mac apps without installing it |

### NATIVE-6: AAT fonts: the preview shapes Apple morx fonts correctly but the forged result drops morx, and no engine option keeps it (189/921 faces here)

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:70`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:61`
- **macOS behaviour:** Many Apple system fonts shape only through AAT `morx` and have no GSUB: Al Bayan, Baghdad, Beirut, Al Nile, American Typewriter, Baskerville, Apple Chancery… Qt's preview and CoreText both shape them correctly. The engine's subsetter drops morx ('morx NOT subset; don't know how to subset; dropped'), and KEEP_TABLES has no AAT tables. For Arabic, the forged font therefore draws unjoined isolated letters while the preview showed joined text. Preview ≠ result, the exact promise the app makes.
- **Evidence:** - Scan of the unique CoreText font files (fontTools lazy table directory): faces 921; morx without GSUB: 189; morx+GSUB: 74.
- Forging Georgia + Baghdad.ttc: log 'morx NOT subset; don't know how to subset; dropped'. Source tables include 'morx','feat','just','kern'; result tables are ['GDEF','GPOS','GSUB','OS/2','cmap','glyf',…] with 88 Arabic chars.
- CoreText check of 'بغداد': Baghdad source 'contextual forms used: True' (shaped [4,71,98,199,207] vs nominal [199,200,207,207,218]); forged font 'contextual forms used: False' ([1272,1273,1280,1280,1291] both).
- Qt (offscreen, NoFontMerging) shapes the source the same as CoreText: `contextual forms used: True`, so today's preview misleads.
- hb-subset on Baghdad also outputs no morx (and no kern): ['GlyphOrder','OS/2','cmap','cvt ','fpgm','glyf','hdmx','head',…].
- **Recommendation:** Engine-level. It must be fixed whichever architecture is chosen, before a Mac release.

Short term (S):
- In catalog/face.py, record `aat_only = 'morx' in font and 'GSUB' not in font`.
- Treat such a face as unsupported for scripts that need shaping (arabic, indic, southeast_asian, hebrew marks), or unsupported entirely. Otherwise keep it, add a report warning ('ligatures and contextual forms are lost'), and have the preview draw that face with shaping disabled so it matches the result.

Long term (L): converting morx state tables to GSUB has no library in any language, so treat it as out of scope. A CoreText-native preview makes the mismatch more visible, so the guard is a prerequisite for Option 2.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Reproduced over the 411 CoreText font files: 921 faces, 189 morx without GSUB, 74 with both, 2 with kerx and no GPOS/kern.

Classified by script (supported faces):
- 34 carry Arabic (e.g. Geeza Pro, Al Nile, Al Tarikh), 10 Hebrew, 9 Indic (Devanagari MT, Gujarati MT, Malayalam MN) and 4 Thai (Thonburi, Sathu).
- The rest are Latin/CJK faces, where the loss is ligatures and alternates.

CoreText on the forged Georgia+Baghdad for 'بغداد': logical glyphs [1273,1291,1280,1272,1280], identical to the nominal cmap glyphs, so no joining. The source shapes to contextual forms. The forged GSUB has only script 'latn' and feature 'aalt'. Qt shapes the source correctly: [4,71,98,199,207] vs nominal [200,218,207,199,207].

New twist: Qt also 'joins' the forged font ([1108,1171,1198,…] = u0628.behNtl/u063A.ghainMdl/u062F.dalFnl). Its HarfBuzz falls back to the Arabic Presentation Forms the result still maps in cmap (89 in FExx, 57 in FBxx). So the Qt app shows joined text before and after building, while TextEdit/Pages show it unjoined (NATIVE-M1).
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Drop 'draw that face with shaping disabled'. Qt has no public per-font shaping switch, and even the built font is joined by Qt's HarfBuzz, so it still would not match CoreText.

Simplest sound fix:
1. Add FontFace.aat_only = 'morx' in font and 'GSUB' not in font (catalog/face.py; bump the cache schema).
2. In smart/planner, never let such a face supply shaping-dependent groups (arabic, hebrew, indic, southeast_asian). Its characters go to the next font, or the build is refused with a clear message.
3. For other groups, keep it with a report warning that morx ligatures and alternates are lost.

This fixes Windows and Mac identically and needs no preview trickery.

### NATIVE-9: On macOS the catalog should come from CoreText in every option: the file scanner misses AssetsV2 fonts, lists hidden faces and picks non-English names for Apple fonts

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort S; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:22`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:100`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:182`
- **macOS behaviour:** What the picker gets wrong today:
- It never offers PingFang or the other downloaded AssetsV2 fonts.
- It lists 74 '.'-hidden system faces (e.g. '.Al Bayan PUA', '.Geeza Pro Interface') and families that macOS hides from font menus (Noto Sans Adlam…).
- It names Apple Arabic fonts by their Arabic name: Al Bayan -> 'البيان', Geeza Pro -> 'گیزا پرو'. _best_name ranks Windows-platform records first, and these fonts keep English only in Mac-platform records.

CoreText returns the names every other Mac app shows, and enumerates fonts in about 0.01 s.
- **Evidence:** pyobjc: CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs -> 411 unique files. The repo scanner (default_font_dirs('darwin')) found 379; CoreText has 32 it misses ('/System/Library/AssetsV2' 29, PrivateFrameworks 1, 2 extensionless). The scanner found none that CoreText lacks.

Scanner with AssetsV2 added: faces=888, families 422, cold 4.9 s, warm 0.44 s, catalog.json 10.1 MB, 1 failure (NISC18030.ttf KeyError 'head').

Versus CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames (221):
- 223 families only in the scanner, 74 of them '.'-prefixed.
- 22 only in CoreText, e.g. 'Al Bayan', 'Geeza Pro', 'Baghdad'.
- `read_faces(AlBayan.ttc)` -> [('البيان','Plain'), ('.Al Bayan PUA','Plain'), …].

Also: Apple's downloadable catalog (kCTFontDownloadableAttribute) lists 1,170 descriptors / 332 families (including some already installed). The query took 126 s, so it must run async.
- **Recommendation:** Option 1: add `macos.font_files()` using CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs.
- Skip faces whose CoreText family starts with '.'.
- Fill FontFace.family/style from CTFontCopyFamilyName/kCTFontStyleNameAttribute. Or fix _best_name to prefer English (langID 0x409 or Mac langID 0) across both platforms before other languages.
- Keep fontTools for cmap/fvar/OS/2 so FontFace stays identical.

Option 2/3: the Swift shell enumerates with CoreText and asks the helper only for per-face details it cannot get natively (local_names ranking, group_counts), cached by path+mtime as today.

Later, optionally: offer Apple's downloadable fonts via CTFontDescriptorMatchFontDescriptorsWithProgressHandler.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** pyobjc CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs returns 411 unique files in 0.045 s; the repo scanner finds 379.
- CoreText-only: 32 files (29 in AssetsV2 incl. PingFang.ttc, 1 PingFangUI in PrivateFrameworks, 2 extensionless: TimesLTMM, HelveLTMM).
- Scanner-only: 0.
- CoreText families: 221, none '.'-prefixed.

read_faces names the Arabic fonts in their native script:
- AlBayan.ttc → ('البيان','Plain'), ('.Al Bayan PUA','Plain').
- GeezaPro.ttc → 'گیزا پرو' plus '.Geeza Pro Interface'/'.Geeza Pro PUA'.
- The CoreText descriptor family attribute gives 'Al Bayan' / 'Geeza Pro' for the same files.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed, and match CoreText descriptors to fontTools faces by PostScript name (nameID 6), not by position. CoreText exposes no TTC index, and variable TTCs expand into named instances (NATIVE-M2). If a pyobjc dependency is unwanted, the minimum fix is:
- fix _best_name to prefer English records from both platforms (Win 0x409 or Mac lang 0);
- skip '.'-prefixed families;
- use CoreText only for the file list, because the AssetsV2/Font8 path is an undocumented implementation detail.

### NATIVE-M1: Qt's preview shapes with its bundled HarfBuzz, not CoreText, so even the 'real built font' preview can differ from what every Mac app draws

- **Severity:** major (auditor: major); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:55`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:23`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:19`, `README.md:24`
- **macOS behaviour:** The README promises a preview drawn exactly as the result will draw. For the forged Georgia+Baghdad font:
- Qt draws 'بغداد' joined: glyphs [1108,1171,1198,1272,1280] = u0628.behNtl, u063A.ghainMdl, u062F.dalFnl.
