- CoreText, and therefore TextEdit, Pages and Safari, draws it unjoined: logical glyphs identical to the nominal cmap [1273,1291,1280,1272,1280].

Why: the result has no Arabic GSUB (scripts ['latn'], features ['aalt']). Qt's HarfBuzz synthesizes joining from the Arabic Presentation Forms still in cmap (89 in U+FE70–FEFF, 57 in U+FB50–FDFF); CoreText does not. The source Baghdad is shaped correctly by both (Qt via HarfBuzz's AAT support; the QtGui binary contains 'start table morx').

So in the Qt app the user sees correct Arabic before and after building and never learns the installed font is broken in native apps.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-native/qt_shape.py (offscreen, NoFontMerging, absolute path):
- Baghdad: [('Baghdad', [4, 71, 98, 199, 207])], nominal [200, 218, 207, 199, 207]
- Forged GB: [('GB', [1108, 1171, 1198, 1272, 1280])], nominal [1273, 1291, 1280, 1272, 1280]

work/verify-native/aat_shape.py (CoreText):
- Baghdad.ttc shaped [207,199,98,71,4], visual order = contextual forms.
- Forged: shaped [1280,1272,1280,1291,1273], visual order = nominal reversed, so no joining.

fontTools on the forged file:
- GSUB scripts ['latn'], features ['aalt'].
- Glyphs 1108/1171/1198 map from cmap 0xfe91/0xfeaa/0xfed0.
- **Recommendation:** (1) Fix the cause in the engine with the NATIVE-6 aat_only guard, so no result depends on shaper fallbacks.

(2) Option 1: add a CoreText 'as macOS apps draw it' check for the built font through pyobjc.
- Draw a CTLine into a CGBitmapContext and wrap it as a QImage, or compare CTRun glyph IDs with Qt's QTextLayout glyph runs.
- Warn when they differ.

(3) Option 2: a CoreText preview gets this by construction, which is a concrete argument for the native shell rather than just a look-and-feel one.

### NATIVE-1: Layer map: the engine and catalog are Qt-free and portable; ForgeModel is logic but tied to Qt, so it is the seam to cut

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/model.py:15`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:25`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:27`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:173`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:205`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:630`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:669`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:19`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:7`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:9`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:6`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:10`
- **macOS behaviour:** The engine and catalog import only fontTools, skia-pathops and the stdlib. A headless run on macOS system fonts worked with no changes and loaded no PySide6 module. The Qt dependency starts at ui/model.py, which the planner, preview and build controller all go through.
- **Evidence:** wc -l / grep PySide6 per file (repo clone):
- Qt-free, runs anywhere: engine 812 (forge 81, prepare 183, merge 142, kern 136, spec 129, scripts 68, synth_bold 46, planner 27), catalog 354 (face 214, cache 83, scanner 57), paths 39.
- Qt-free UI logic: languages 196, smart 193, mix 43, textutil 23, requests 12 (467 total). Windows install.py: 227.
- Qt-coupled logic: model 868, build 353, fonts 113, workers 77 (1,411 total).
- Qt views: picker 757, recipe 676, app 415, advanced 333, action_bar 323, preview 322, preview_pane 263, theme 232, widgets 97 (3,418 total).
- Totals: 1,911 Qt-free lines vs 4,829 Qt-importing lines.
- Tests: 85 pure test functions (954 lines) vs 213 Qt test functions (3,616 lines); 4,802 lines in all.

Where model.py couples to Qt:
- model.py:15 `from PySide6.QtCore import QObject, QTimer, Signal`
- :25 `font_loader` (QFontDatabase, used at :669 to unload the result)
- :27 `CombineWorker` (QThread, :630)
- :205 QTimer debounce

Other dependency notes:
- build.py:19 uses only QObject/Signal.
- engine/spec.py:6 imports catalog.face.FontFace, and catalog/face.py:10 imports engine.scripts. The two packages depend on each other, but there is no module-level cycle.

Run: `forge_mac.py` (scratch) printed `import engine+catalog+smart (no Qt): 0.07s`, `Qt loaded? False`, `forge 23.1s`, Characters 33793, Glyphs 45771.
- **Recommendation:** Do this seam refactor whichever option wins (about 1 person-week).

1. Make ForgeModel/BuildController plain Python:
   - Replace Signals with a tiny observer (callbacks or blinker-style).
   - Replace QTimer with an injected `schedule(delay, fn)`.
   - Replace CombineWorker with an injected `EngineRunner` (thread, QProcess or subprocess).
   - Replace font_loader().unload with an injected `on_result_discarded` hook.
2. Move the Qt adapters into ui/qt_bridge.py.
3. Define the engine boundary as data: ForgeSpec.to_dict() in; ForgeReport/ForgeError and cache.face_to_dict() FontFace records out.

The current Qt app keeps working, and the ~37 model tests can run without qtbot. Any Swift/Rust shell then ports a UI-free model whose behaviour the tests already specify.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** Line counts match exactly (wc -l total 6,740). Import grep confirms model.py:15 (QObject/QTimer/Signal), :25 (font_loader, QFontDatabase), :27 (CombineWorker QThread), :205 QTimer, :630 worker, :669 unload. build.py:19 only QObject/Signal; build.py:21 also imports ui.install (Windows installer), which is part of the seam too. Run: importing engine.forge + catalog.scanner + ui.smart/languages/mix took 0.047 s with PySide6 loaded: False; importing ui.model then loaded PySide6. Test split re-counted: 298 test functions, 213 touch Qt, 85 are pure, 4,802 lines.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same as proposed. Also move the installer behind the same injected interface: build.py imports ui.install directly (build.py:21), so BuildController cannot be ported without it.

### NATIVE-2: Option 1 (Qt-native-ish): keep PySide6 + fontTools, add a macOS platform layer, ship a signed/notarized .app

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:12`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:1`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:60`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:164`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:172`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:409`, `fontplayground/ui/theme.py:99`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:100`, `tests/test_paths.py`
- **macOS behaviour:** It runs today, but it does not feel like a Mac app:
- The only menu is a ⋯ QMenu (app.py:172). There is no menu bar, no About/Settings/Quit roles and no Window menu.
- Theme tokens are hard-coded (accent #1a6bd8, theme.py:99-110), so the system accent colour is ignored. 35 custom border-radius stylesheet rules draw a Fluent-like look.
- Install is Windows-only (install.is_supported() returns False), so the bar offers Save only.
- Conflict detection checks %WINDIR%\Fonts (build.py:60-62).

The font stack is already native: Qt's font database on macOS is CoreText-backed (222 families, PingFang SC/Songti SC/Hiragino Sans GB known by name). Qt also shapes AAT morx correctly for source fonts.
- **Evidence:** - `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen python`: `Qt 6.11.2 platform offscreen families 222; PingFang SC known: True`. addApplicationFont(forged ttf) -> 0 ['Georgia Hiragino'].
- Qt payload actually needed (QtCore/Gui/Widgets/DBus frameworks + libqcocoa + qmacstyle + 3 bindings + libpyside6 + shiboken6): `du -ch` = 88M, universal2 (`lipo -archs` QtCore -> x86_64 arm64).
- Full PySide6 wheel set is 1.2G on disk; it must be pruned (243 Mach-O files in PySide6 alone).
- Standalone Python 3.12.14 (uv/python-build-standalone): 71M on disk, of which libpython3.12.dylib is 17M and the stdlib 27M.
- fontTools 17M, pathops 7.1M (universal2), pyobjc objc 10M + CoreText 248K + Foundation 1.1M.
- Qt frameworks and extension modules are ad-hoc/unsigned (`codesign -dv` QtCore.framework: 'code object is not signed at all'), so every Mach-O must be re-signed for notarization. `xcrun --find notarytool` -> present.
- Startup: `import PySide6.QtWidgets + fontplayground.ui.app` 0.13 s warm.
- Docs: https://docs.python.org/3/using/mac.html lists py2app, Briefcase ('managing signing and notarization') and PyInstaller.
- **Recommendation:** Do this first. Estimate 3.5–5 person-weeks.

(a) Platform layer, ~1–1.5 weeks. Add a fontplayground/platform package with a `FontPlatform` protocol: font_files(), system_font_roots(), user_fonts_dir(), install/uninstall/installed_path/is_supported, reveal_label ('Show in Finder'), ui_font().
- Enumerate fonts with CoreText (pyobjc-framework-CoreText, `CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs`). This picks up AssetsV2 (PingFang) and filters '.'-hidden faces.
- Take display names from CoreText or fix _best_name (see NATIVE-9).
- Mac installer: copy into ~/Library/Fonts (Font Book behaviour) and/or CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs(.persistent). Uninstall = unregister + delete.
- Conflict roots: /System/Library/Fonts, /Library/Fonts, /System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font8.
- Replace the 'Windows already has…' text.

(b) Native feel, ~1–1.5 weeks.
- A parentless QMenuBar gives the global menu bar. Use QAction.MenuRole About/Preferences/Quit, QKeySequence.StandardKey shortcuts (Cmd), and a Window menu.
- Drop the Segoe branch (app.py:409).
- Derive accent from QPalette.Accent (it follows System Settings) instead of #1a6bd8. Tone down radii and fills so controls read as native.
- Test with QT_QPA_PLATFORM=cocoa on a real session.

(c) Tests, ~0.5 week. Use PureWindowsPath in test_paths; add Mac installer tests on a temp dir.

(d) Packaging, ~1–1.5 weeks.
- Use Briefcase (does signing + notarization) or pyside6-deploy/PyInstaller with the unused Qt modules excluded.
- Info.plist: bundle id, LSMinimumSystemVersion, .icns.
- Sign inside-out with `codesign --options runtime --timestamp` (no --deep), then `notarytool submit --wait` and `stapler staple`, then a DMG.
- Distribute with Developer ID, outside the App Store: Qt is LGPL and Python needs --with-app-store-compliance for the store.

Expected size: ~150 MB universal2 or ~85–95 MB arm64-thin, roughly 50–60 MB as a DMG.

What the user gets: a working Mac release fast, with Windows kept from one codebase and 100% of the tests reused. What they do not get: Mac-native controls, text system or preview typography.
- **Verifier (partially):** Confirmed:
- The only menu is the ⋯ QMenu (app.py:172).
- The Segoe branch is at app.py:409.
- There are exactly 35 border-radius rules (action_bar 5, advanced 6, preview 1, preview_pane 9, recipe 9, picker 5).
- install.is_supported() returns False; system_fonts_dir() returns 'C:\Windows/Fonts' on this Mac.
- The needed Qt payload measures 88M; QtCore is x86_64+arm64 and 'not signed at all'.

Corrections:
(a) Accent colour. Under QT_QPA_PLATFORM=cocoa (Dock transform disabled, no window), the platform palette exposes a system Accent (#0a60ff). After ThemeManager runs, QGuiApplication.palette() returns the app's #4c8ef5, because theme.py:220 replaces the whole palette. So 'derive accent from QPalette.Accent' only works if the platform accent is captured before the first setPalette (and re-read on scheme change), or read from NSColor.controlAccentColor.
(b) 'Not Mac-native preview typography' is imprecise. Qt on macOS rasterizes with CoreText but shapes with its bundled HarfBuzz, and that visibly differs from CoreText (see NATIVE-M1).
(c) pyproject.toml depends on the full 'PySide6' meta-package: Addons are 885 MB by RECORD and unused. Only QtCore/QtGui/QtWidgets are imported, so depend on PySide6-Essentials.
(d) The Qt 6.11 wheels are tagged macosx_13_0_universal2 (otool minos 13.0), so LSMinimumSystemVersion must be ≥13.0.
(e) The app defines no keyboard shortcuts at all (grep …
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Keep the plan, with these changes:
- Switch the dependency to PySide6-Essentials>=6.8 before packaging.
- Take the accent from the platform palette captured before ThemeManager._push overrides it (or from pyobjc NSColor.controlAccentColor), refreshed on colorSchemeChanged/ApplicationPaletteChange.
- Use QMainWindow.menuBar() or a parentless QMenuBar with MenuRole About/Preferences/Quit; Qt already maps Ctrl to Cmd.
- Render shortcut hints with QKeySequence(...).toString(NativeText) so the tooltips say ⌘Z.
- Add a CoreText check of the built font (NATIVE-M1), because Qt's HarfBuzz shaping is not what Mac apps draw.

### NATIVE-3: Option 2 (hybrid): SwiftUI/AppKit + CoreText shell, fontTools engine kept as a bundled Python helper over JSON-lines

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort L; files: `fontplayground/engine/forge.py:32`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:71`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:84`, `fontplayground/catalog/cache.py:27`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:39`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:622`, `fontplayground/ui/preview.py:44`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:62`
- **macOS behaviour:** Verified with prototypes in the scratch dir. forge_helper.py (~50 lines) reads ForgeSpec.to_dict()+output on stdin and writes progress/done/error JSON lines to stdout. shell.swift (swiftc -O, 1.8 s build) starts it with Process+Pipe, decodes the events with Codable, and registers the result in process scope. CoreText then drew the forged font by itself: all glyphs found, every CTRun used GeorgiaHiragino-Regular. The CoreText preview can be made as honest as Qt's NoFontMerging: a cascade list of only LastResort makes missing glyphs show as LastResort instead of being borrowed from STSongti.
- **Evidence:** `./swift/shell python forge_helper.py req.json`:
```
[  0.1s] plan 0% ... [  0.2s] prepare:Hiragino Sans GB W3 35%
[ 23.3s] merge 70% ... [ 28.9s] done 100%
report: 30104 chars, 30179 glyphs
register process scope: true
CoreText family=Georgia Hiragino allGlyphsFound=true missing=0
  run font: GeorgiaHiragino-Regular glyphs: 8 / 3 / 2
```

`./swift/nofallback`:
- default cascade: [Georgia x8, STSongti-SC-Regular x2]
- cascade=LastResort: [Georgia x8, LastResort x2]
- unregistered file via CTFontManagerCreateFontDescriptorsFromURL: Hiragino Sans GB draws without registration.

Helper cost:
- `python -c 'import fontplayground.engine.forge, fontplayground.catalog.face'` 0.05–0.06 s warm.
- `/usr/bin/time -l` forge: 27.72 s real, 703,840,256 bytes max RSS.

Bundle inputs:
- python-build-standalone 3.12.14: libpython 17M plus stdlib 27M (droppable: idlelib 1.3M, ensurepip 1.8M, tkinter + tcl/tk ~6M). Only 10 Mach-O files, `Signature=adhoc`.
- fontTools: 6 .so files; pathops: 1 universal2 .so; unicodedata2: 1 .so. About 18 Mach-O files to sign.

Logic that moves to Swift: model 868 + smart 193 + languages 196 + mix 43 + textutil 23 + build 353 + planner.source_of + scripts.group_of (≈1,750 Python lines).
- **Recommendation:** This is the target native app. Estimate 10–14 person-weeks after NATIVE-1.

Helper design:
- Executable `Contents/Helpers/fp-engine`: a small launcher running a trimmed python-build-standalone with `-I` and an explicit sys.path, plus precompiled .pyc. Never write __pycache__ into the signed bundle; set PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1.
- `scan`: stream FontFace records in cache.face_to_dict format, one JSON line each, so Swift keeps exact parity for local_names, group_counts and supported. Alternatively Swift enumerates via CoreText and asks the helper only for faces it has not cached.
- `forge`: stdin = ForgeSpec.to_dict() + output. stdout = {progress, stage, fraction} ... {done, report = asdict(ForgeReport)} or {error, stage, material, message}.
- Cancel: SIGTERM (stops at the next stage boundary), then SIGKILL after about 2 s.
- Point TMPDIR at an app-owned directory so a killed run's TemporaryDirectory is swept at launch.
- Keep the helper process-per-forge. Peak memory (700 MB) then leaves with it; PythonKit would keep it in the UI process.
- An XPC service is only worth it under App Sandbox. There the helper also needs com.apple.security.app-sandbox + com.apple.security.inherit.

Swift side:
- An @Observable ForgeModel ported from model.py, using its tests as the spec.
- Picker: NSTableView or a SwiftUI List with CTFontDescriptors loaded from URL without registering.
- Preview: NSTextView with per-character font attributes from source_of (the NSTextStorage equivalent of MixHighlighter), cascade list forced to LastResort. Verify honesty by reading CTRun fonts.
- Install via CTFontManager, Add folder via NSOpenPanel + security-scoped bookmarks, a Settings scene, automatic appearance and accent.
- Sign every Mach-O (hardened runtime + timestamp), notarize, staple.
- Size ≈ 60 MB (Python ~30 + fontTools 17 + pathops 7 + unicodedata2 1 + app ~5).

What the user gets: a real Mac app. CoreText rendering matches TextEdit/Pages, with native text input, IME and menus, and the proven fontTools engine unchanged.

Costs: two languages, and the Qt UI dropped or kept separately for Windows.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** I re-ran the auditor's compiled shell with the Python helper on a new request (Georgia + Songti SC):
- Progress streamed from plan to done in 3.2 s.
- Report decoded: 8,886 chars / 8,881 glyphs. Process-scope registration returned true.
- CoreText drew every run from VerifyShell-Regular.

I re-ran nofallback:
- Default cascade: [Georgia x8, STSongti-SC-Regular x2].
- Cascade=LastResort: [Georgia x8, LastResort x2]. Confirmed.
- Its output also shows that an unregistered URL descriptor still falls back (Hiragino Sans GB: 'ä' drawn by Helvetica), so the LastResort cascade must be applied to every URL-created descriptor too.

My own in-process forge of Georgia + PingFang HK peaked at 923 MB RSS, higher than the 704 MB quoted, which strengthens the process-per-forge advice.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Add three requirements to the Swift shell design:
(1) Key catalog rows by (path, PostScript name), not descriptor position or TTC index (NATIVE-M2).
(2) Pin optical size in the CoreText preview (kCTFontOpticalSizeAttribute='none' or an explicit opsz variation) to match what the engine instantiates (NATIVE-M3).
(3) Apply the LastResort cascade to URL-created descriptors as well as registered ones.

### NATIVE-4: Option 3 (full Swift rewrite incl. engine): subset/instancing/bold/reading are coverable; merge, UPEM scaling, CFF→glyf and GPOS building are not

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort L; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:8`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:21`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:10`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:12`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:13`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:78`, `fontplayground/engine/kern.py:8`, `fontplayground/engine/synth_bold.py:4`, `fontplayground/engine/scripts.py:7`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:121`
- **macOS behaviour:** There is no Apple API that writes, subsets or merges fonts. CoreText only reads and renders. The Swift toolchain is present (Apple Swift 6.4, Xcode 27).
- **Evidence:** fontTools features the engine actually uses (174 fontTools modules loaded after a 3-font forge with a variable font and synthetic bold):
- ttLib TTFont/TTCollection/newTable
- subset.Subsetter/Options (prepare.py:44-75, merge.py:25-29)
- merge.Merger (merge.py:21-22)
- varLib.instancer.instantiateVariableFont (prepare.py:35-41)
- ttLib.scaleUpem.scale_upem (prepare.py:123-128)
- pens Cu2QuPen + TTGlyphPen (CFF→glyf, prepare.py:78-120)
- otlLib.builder buildLookup/buildPairPosGlyphsSubtable/buildValue + otTables (kern→GPOS, kern.py)
- otlLib.maxContextCalc (merge.py:123)
- OS/2 recalcUnicodeRanges/recalcCodePageRanges (merge.py:121-122)
- unicodedata.script + unicodedata2 (scripts.py:7)
- skia-pathops stroke+union (synth_bold.py:33-37)
feaLib, designspaceLib and varLib.merger load only transitively.

Measured substitutes:
1) HarfBuzz hb-subset (uharfbuzz 0.56.2 / HB 14.5.0, private venv) on PingFang SC, 28,258 cps: 0.11 s, 41,005 glyphs. The engine's fontTools subset_font on the same input: 6.29 s, glyphs=41005 (same closure, 57x faster). SFNS.ttf pinned wght=600 + Latin subset: <0.01 s, fvar=False, glyf. hb-subset kept CFF, though; it has no CFF→glyf conversion.
2) CGPath.copy(strokingWithWidth:) + union(_:using:) (macOS 13+) emboldens Georgia 'a' in 0.34 ms, but outputs only cubics: `source (quad: 48, cubic: 0) -> bold (quad: 0, cubic: 60)`. A cu2qu port is therefore needed to …
- **Recommendation:** Not recommended now. Estimate 22–33 person-weeks (engine 14–22 + app 8–11), with high risk.

Engine work breakdown:
- Metadata via CoreText + a name/OS/2/fvar table parser: 1 week.
- Unicode Script table generated from Scripts.txt (Swift has no Unicode.Scalar script property): 0.5 week.
- hb-subset vendored as a SwiftPM C target (harfbuzz amalgamation, MIT) for subset + instancing: 1–2 weeks. This includes option parity: DESUBROUTINIZE, NO_HINTING, GLYPH_NAMES, name IDs/langs, dropping locl.
- UPEM scaler over glyf/hmtx/hhea/OS/2/GPOS ValueRecords/Anchors/GDEF: 2–3 weeks.
- cu2qu port + TrueType writer for glyf/loca/maxp/post/hmtx: 2–3 weeks.
- CGPath bold + cu2qu: 1 week.
- OpenType Layout object model (read/write all GSUB 1–8 and GPOS 1–9 lookup types, extension lookups, GDEF): 4–6 weeks.
- Merger (glyph renumbering, .notdef dedupe, cmap/hmtx/glyf concat, Script/Feature/Lookup list union, offset-overflow repacking for large CJK GPOS): 3–5 weeks.
- Finish/verify: 1 week.

The main risk is silently wrong GSUB/GPOS on real CJK and Indic fonts. Mitigate with golden tests: build the same ForgeSpecs with both engines and diff with ttx/hb-shape.

What the user gets: a single-binary app of ~10–15 MB, forges probably around 1–3 s, and a path to the App Store and iPad. What it costs: all engine code and pure tests are reused only as a specification.
- **Verifier (partially):** Re-verified:
- hb-subset on PingFang SC (face 3, 28,258 cps, all features minus locl) gives the identical glyph-name set as fontTools Subsetter: 41,005 glyphs, only-ft 0, only-hb 0. Time 0.105 s vs 5.46 s (subset only, without save), about 52x.
- hb output keeps CFF.
- CGPath.union(_:using:) is @available(macOS 13.0) in the SDK swiftinterface.

Corrections:
(a) 'Swift has no script property, generate Scripts.txt' is unnecessary. uchar.h is in the macOS SDK. A swiftc test calling u_getIntPropertyValue(cp, UCHAR_SCRIPT) linked with -licucore returned Arabic=2, Han=17, Devanagari=10, Latin=25, Hiragana=20. The ICU Unicode version may lag unicodedata2 18.0.
(b) 'No merge library in any language' needs a caveat. The Rust crate fontmerge 0.2.0 (Simon Cozens, babelfont-rs, Sept 2026, https://docs.rs/fontmerge) merges glyphs plus OpenType layout between font *sources*, not compiled TTFs. So it does not replace fontTools.merge, but a TTF→babelfont→fontmerge→fontc route exists and is immature.

The effort figures are judgment and cannot be verified.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Keep 'not recommended now'. In the estimate:
- Replace the Scripts.txt generator with ICU u_getIntPropertyValue(UCHAR_SCRIPT) from libicucore (about 0.5 week less).
- Note fontmerge/babelfont-rs as a source-level merge path to re-evaluate before committing to a hand-written merger.

### NATIVE-5: Option 4 (Rust core + native shells): fontations covers read/write and repacking but not merging; the best choice if Windows, macOS and iPad must share one engine

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort L; files: `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:21`, `fontplayground/engine/kern.py:66`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:137`
- **macOS behaviour:** Not prototyped: rustc/cargo are not installed on this Mac (`which cargo` -> not found), and brew installs are out of scope. A Swift shell would link the core via UniFFI or swift-bridge as an XCFramework.
- **Evidence:** Ecosystem, from docs/search:
- fontations: read-fonts (parse), write-fonts (typed table builders incl. GSUB/GPOS, compile with offset-overflow packing), skrifa (outlines).
- klippa: the Rust port of hb-subset.
- harfbuzz-sys is available for instancing where klippa lacks it.
- kurbo provides cubic→quadratic conversion.
- No merger exists (https://crates.io/crates/fontations, https://github.com/googlefonts/fontations, https://github.com/googlefonts/oxidize).

The engine surface that would move: merge.Merger (merge.py:21), kern_to_gpos (kern.py:66-136), prepare pipeline (prepare.py:137-183), finish (merge.py:112-126).
- **Recommendation:** Keep this as the long-term option if an iPad build (the user already ships legado-ios-ipados) or App Store distribution becomes a goal. Estimate 17–25 person-weeks.

- Rust engine: 8–12 weeks. Merging is still hand-written, but on write-fonts' typed OTL model and repacker, which removes the hardest parts of the Swift-only path.
- UniFFI bindings + XCFramework build: 1 week.
- Swift shell: the same UI work as Option 2, 7–10 weeks, minus the Python bundling.

Until then, the Option 2 helper protocol (JSON in, events out) is exactly the interface a Rust core would later implement, so the shell survives the engine swap.
- **Verifier (unverifiable):** `which cargo rustc` returns not found, so nothing could be built. The ecosystem claims (read-fonts/write-fonts/skrifa/klippa/kurbo) match the docs.

The iPad justification is weak:
- CTFontManagerErrors.h documents kCTFontManagerErrorInvalidFilePath (306) as: the file must be in the application's bundle or an on-demand resource.
- WWDC19 session 227 says font-provider apps (Fonts capability, user consent) may install only fonts shipped in the bundle or an asset catalog, never ones produced at runtime.
- So an iPad build could not install a forged font system-wide through CTFontManager; see NATIVE-M6.

Also, Python 3.13+ officially supports embedded, in-process iOS (https://docs.python.org/3/using/ios.html), so 'iPad needs a non-Python engine' is not absolute. Only the subprocess helper is impossible there, and binary modules (skia-pathops, fontTools .so) must be repackaged as frameworks.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Keep Rust as the long-term candidate, but do not justify it with iPad install:
- On iPadOS the forged font can only be exported/shared or delivered as a configuration profile with a Fonts payload.
- If iPad matters, compare a Rust core with embedded CPython (PEP 730) running in-process.
- Re-check the fontmerge/babelfont-rs source-level merger before scoping a hand-written merger.

### NATIVE-7: Run the engine out of process in every option: cancelling currently waits for the next stage (observed 17.7 s), and a build peaks at 700 MB inside the UI process

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:50`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:57`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:38`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:388`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:640`
- **macOS behaviour:** CombineWorker can only stop by raising in the progress callback (workers.py:57-60). The PingFang HK run emitted 'prepare:PingFang HK Regular' at 0.55 s and the next event 'merge' at 18.26 s, so Cancel or Quit can hang for about 18 s. closeEvent waits up to COMBINE_WAIT_MS = 180,000 ms (app.py:38, :388). The Python heap from a 700 MB build stays in the GUI process afterwards.
- **Evidence:** - forge_mac.py stage timestamps: (0.55,'prepare:PingFang HK Regular',0.35) -> (18.26,'merge',0.7).
- `/usr/bin/time -l` helper forge: 27.72 real, 703,840,256 maximum resident set size.
- The prototype helper (forge_helper.py) handles SIGTERM at stage boundaries, and the parent can SIGKILL immediately. Helper import cost is 0.05–0.06 s warm.
- **Recommendation:** Implement NATIVE-1's EngineRunner as a subprocess. In Option 1 that is QProcess running `python -m fontplayground.engine.cli`; in Option 2 it is Swift's Process with the same protocol.
- Cancel = SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after about 2 s. Quitting becomes instant.
- Crashes in native code (skia-pathops, fontTools cython) can no longer take the UI down.
- Memory returns to the OS after each build.
- Keep ForgeError semantics by sending {stage, material, message} over the wire.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** My own run (Georgia + PingFang HK Regular, CFF, 49,533 glyphs):
- Gaps before each event: prepare:PingFang 0.06 s → merge 17.44 s → finish 0.37 s → verify 4.84 s. Total 22.8 s.
- ru_maxrss 923 MB in-process.

workers.py:57-60 cancels only by raising in the progress callback. closeEvent waits COMBINE_WAIT_MS=180,000 (app.py:38, :388).

Caveat on the recommendation:
- `python -m fontplayground.engine.cli` does not exist inside a PyInstaller/Briefcase .app, where sys.executable is the bundle launcher.
- On macOS, multiprocessing.get_start_method() returns 'spawn' (verified), so a multiprocessing helper needs freeze_support() and a guarded entry point.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Two steps:
(1) Cheap, right away: pass a should_stop callback into prepare() and check it between subset / cff_to_glyf / embolden / scale / save, and every N glyphs inside cff_to_glyf. Cancel latency drops from about 17 s to under 1 s with no IPC.
(2) For isolation: have __main__ dispatch on an argv flag (e.g. `FontPlayground --engine-helper`), and launch sys.executable with that flag via QProcess. Or use a multiprocessing 'spawn' context with multiprocessing.freeze_support() in main. Cancel with SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, and sweep the temp dir at launch.

### NATIVE-8: Distribution constraints per option: App Sandbox cannot write ~/Library/Fonts, Python needs App Store patches, Qt is LGPL, so ship Developer ID + notarization first

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:85`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:339`, `README.md`
- **macOS behaviour:** The sandbox profile allows reading all of /System (including AssetsV2 fonts), /Library and ~/Library/Fonts, but gives no write rule for ~/Library/Fonts. A sandboxed build therefore cannot install by copying (the Windows approach, install.py:160). It would have to keep the TTF in its container and use CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs(.persistent); the header warns: 'Once fonts have been registered from a file, it shouldn't be moved or renamed.'

'Add folder…' (app.py:339) would need NSOpenPanel + security-scoped bookmarks under the sandbox. Every bundled binary is currently ad-hoc signed or unsigned.
- **Evidence:** - /System/Library/Sandbox/Profiles/application.sb:
  - line 376 `(read-only-and-issue-extensions (subpath "/System"))`
  - line 80 `(allow file-read* process-exec (subpath "/Library"))`
  - line 391 `(home-subpath "/Library/Fonts")`, inside an `allow file-read*` block only
  - lines 78-79 deny read/write extensions on home ~/Library.
- CTFontManager.h (SDK): kCTFontManagerScopePersistent = 'available to all processes for the current user session and will be available in subsequent sessions unless unregistered'. kCTFontManagerScopeUser == 2 (the same value as Persistent).
- CTFontManagerErrors.h: kCTFontManagerErrorMissingEntitlement = 302, kCTFontManagerErrorDuplicatedName = 305 (maps to build.py 'block'), kCTFontManagerErrorInvalidFilePath = 306 ('must be either in the application's bundle or an on-demand resource').
- Python docs (https://docs.python.org/3/using/mac.html): the stdlib must be patched via `--with-app-store-compliance` only for Mac App Store distribution.
- `codesign -dv` python3.12: `Signature=adhoc`; QtCore.framework: 'not signed at all'. `xcrun --find notarytool` present.
- **Recommendation:** v1 in any option: Developer ID signing, hardened runtime, notarized DMG, no sandbox. Install then copies into ~/Library/Fonts like Font Book.

App Store later: Option 2 with an XPC or inheriting-sandbox helper and persistent CoreText registration, or Option 3/4 without Python. Qt/LGPL plus the App Store is a legal grey area, so don't plan Option 1 for the store.

Before promising a store build, verify persistent registration from a sandboxed test app in a throwaway VM or user account. It was not exercised here: persistent/user-scope registration is off-limits for this audit.
- **Verifier (confirmed):** application.sb:
- Line 376 read-only (subpath "/System").
- Line 80 allow file-read* on /Library.
- Lines 78-79 deny extensions on home ~/Library.
- Line 391 home-subpath /Library/Fonts sits inside an 'allow file-read*' block only.
- A grep finds no write rule for Fonts anywhere.

CTFontManager.h: Persistent=2 and User=2 (same value); Session=3 is macOS-only. The 'shouldn't be moved or renamed' text is at lines 160/242/288.

CTFontManagerErrors.h: 302 MissingEntitlement, 305 DuplicatedName, and 306 InvalidFilePath with the text 'must be either in the application's bundle or an on-demand resource'.

The Python doc quote on --with-app-store-compliance being needed only for Mac App Store distribution is verified. The doc also says Briefcase manages signing and notarization.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Same plan (Developer ID + hardened runtime + notarized DMG, install by copying into ~/Library/Fonts). Put the App Store install path down as doubtful rather than just untested. Error 306's wording implies a font generated at runtime into the container may be refused persistent registration. Prototype that in a throwaway account before promising a store build.

### NATIVE-11: Recommendation: a phased path. Qt-native release first, then a process boundary, then a SwiftUI/CoreText shell; rewrite the engine only if needed

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort L; files: `fontplayground/ui/model.py:173`, `fontplayground/ui/install.py:1`, `fontplayground/paths.py:22`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405`, `README.md`
- **macOS behaviour:** Summary of the evidence:
- The engine works on macOS today.
- The UI works but is non-native: a ⋯ menu instead of a menu bar, custom stylesheet look, Windows-only install and conflict logic.
- The catalog misses Mac fonts and misnames some.
- AAT fonts break the preview=result promise.
- **Evidence:** Consolidated from NATIVE-1…10: engine runs unmodified (23.1 s / 27.7 s forges); the Swift↔helper prototype works end to end; CoreText honesty via cascade=LastResort; hb-subset parity; 189 morx-only faces; 32 missed files; sandbox profile rules; bundle sizes (Qt 88 MB, Python ~30–45 MB, fontTools 17 MB).
- **Recommendation:** Phase 0 (week 1): correctness on Mac.
- CoreText-based font list and English names (NATIVE-9).
- AAT guard (NATIVE-6).
- Fix the two Windows-path tests.
- Mac installer + conflict roots; platform-neutral wording.

Phase 1 (weeks 2–5): native-feeling Qt build, as in NATIVE-2.
- Menu bar with About/Settings/Quit roles, Cmd shortcuts, system accent and appearance, Show in Finder.
- Briefcase or PyInstaller .app, Developer ID signing, notarization, DMG.
- Result: a shippable Mac app in about 4–5 person-weeks.

Phase 2 (about 1–2 weeks, can overlap): the seam (NATIVE-1 + NATIVE-7).
- Qt-free ForgeModel/BuildController.
- `fontplayground.engine.cli` with scan/forge JSON-lines, used by the Qt app via QProcess (instant cancel, isolated memory).
- Freeze the protocol and write golden tests: ForgeSpec JSON -> ttx/cmap/glyph count snapshots.

Phase 3 (10–14 weeks, when wanted): the native shell.
- SwiftUI app plus AppKit where needed. Ship the helper in Contents/Helpers with a trimmed python-build-standalone.
- Port the model from Python with its tests as the spec.
- NSTextView/CoreText preview with the LastResort cascade.
- NSTableView picker drawing each family via CTFontDescriptor.
- CTFontManager install, NSOpenPanel bookmarks, Settings scene.
- Optionally uharfbuzz in the helper (NATIVE-10).

Phase 4 (only if App Store, iPad or a no-Python binary becomes a goal): replace the helper with a Rust core, fontations + hb-subset + a hand-written merger (NATIVE-5), keeping the Phase-2 protocol. A pure-Swift engine (NATIVE-4) is the riskier variant, because GSUB/GPOS merging and offset-overflow repacking would be written from scratch.
- **Verifier (partially):** The consolidated evidence holds up on re-run: the engine runs unmodified, the Swift↔helper prototype works, the LastResort cascade works, hb-subset parity holds, 189 faces are morx-only, the scanner misses 32 files, the sandbox rules are as quoted, and the Qt payload is 88 MB.

The phasing has three problems:
(1) Phase 1 spends 3.5–5 weeks on Qt-specific polish (menu roles, accent, packaging and signing about 243 Qt Mach-O files). That work is largely thrown away if Phase 3 happens, and the user explicitly asked for a 'native adoption' and already ships Swift apps.
(2) Qt on the Mac cannot keep the app's core promise, preview = what Mac apps draw. Its bundled HarfBuzz joins Arabic in the forged font where CoreText does not (NATIVE-M1), so Option 1 is not just 'less native looking' but less honest.
(3) The Phase 4 iPad rationale is weakened: iPadOS cannot install runtime-generated fonts system-wide (NATIVE-M6).

The effort figures are judgment and cannot be verified.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** Reorder:
- Phase 0 (about 1–1.5 weeks): engine and catalog correctness (AAT guard, CoreText enumeration + English names, test_paths fix) and the Qt-free seam + engine helper with self-dispatch entry point and golden tests. Keep the Qt app runnable from source on both OSes, with no Mac Qt packaging.
- Phase 1: go straight to the SwiftUI/AppKit + CoreText shell on the helper, Developer ID notarized. Windows keeps the Qt UI on the same helper protocol.
- Package the Qt app for the Mac only if a Mac release is needed before the Swift shell is ready.
- Treat an engine rewrite (Rust vs embedded CPython in-process) as a separate decision tied to App Store/iPad goals, given the iPad install limits.

### NATIVE-M2: CoreText descriptors cannot be mapped to the engine's (path, index) face key by position: no public TTC index, and variable TTCs expand into named instances

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:41`, `fontplayground/catalog/face.py:199`, `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:71`
- **macOS behaviour:** A CoreText-driven catalog (the Option 1 enumeration in NATIVE-9, or the Swift picker in NATIVE-3) produces descriptors that have to be tied back to FontFace.key = (path, index).
- Descriptor attributes expose only NSFontNameAttribute/NSFontSizeAttribute; there is no face index.
- Of 145 TTC/OTC files, 140 list descriptors in the same order as fontTools.
- 5 do not, because variable collections expand into named instances: ADTNumeric 1,053 descriptors vs 12 faces, PingFangUI 268 vs 32, SFIndia 81 vs 9, CJKSymbolsFallback 70 vs 10, ThonburiUI 6 vs 2.

Indexing by position therefore silently picks the wrong face.
- **Evidence:** work/verify-native/ttc_index.py: 'ttc files 145 same order 140 different order 0 count mismatch 5'. Examples: ('ADTNumeric.ttc', 1053, 12), ('PingFangUI.ttc', 268, 32). 'public attribute keys on a TTC face: [NSFontNameAttribute, NSFontSizeAttribute]'.
- **Recommendation:** Key the bridge by (realpath, PostScript name).
- The helper, or fontTools in Option 1, resolves the TTC index by matching nameID 6.
- Collapse named-instance descriptors of a variable face into one FontFace, exposing the instance as a MaterialSpec weight (plus other axes if supported later), not as separate faces.
- Keep path+mtime cache invalidation as today.

### NATIVE-M3: CoreText applies optical size automatically; the engine pins opsz at the axis default, so a CoreText (or Qt-on-Mac) preview of an opsz font drifts from the result

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:35`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:55`
- **macOS behaviour:** CoreText sets opsz from the point size: SFNS gives opsz 17 at 12 pt, 30 at 30 pt and 96 at 96 pt. instance_variable pins every axis except wght to its default (opsz 28 for SFNS).

Measured advance per em of 'Hamburgefonstiv 0123' in CoreText:
- auto: 10.23 at 12 pt, 9.14 at 30 pt, 8.88 at 96 pt;
- kCTFontOpticalSizeAttribute='none': constant 9.15.

So the preview is about 12% wider at 12 pt than the result. Qt on macOS is also size-dependent (13.67 → 12.78 px/pt from 12 to 96 pt).

On a stock Mac all 32 opsz faces are hidden '.'-system faces or 'System Font', so the impact is on user-installed opsz fonts (e.g. Inter 4, Roboto Flex).
- **Evidence:** work/verify-native/opsz.py output:
- CoreText auto: 10.2331 / 9.1401 / 8.8764 at 12 / 30 / 96 pt; opsz 'none': 9.1498 at every size.
- Qt: 13.6680 / 13.3792 / 12.7812.

CTFontCopyVariation: 12 pt → opsz 17.0, 30 pt → 30.0, 96 pt → 96.0.

CTFontDescriptor.h: 'Starting with macOS 10.15 … "none" can be used … to explicitly disable automatic optical sizing'.

The scan found 91 variable faces, 32 with opsz, all '.'-prefixed or System Font.
- **Recommendation:** - Swift preview: build descriptors with kCTFontOpticalSizeAttribute='none', or pass an explicit kCTFontVariationAttribute containing the engine's pinned values for every axis.
- Qt: in make_font, set QFont.setVariableAxis for opsz (and the other non-wght axes) to the fvar defaults the engine uses, then verify Qt honours it over CoreText's automatic value.
- Alternatively, let the engine instantiate opsz at a user-chosen size and show that value in the UI.

### NATIVE-M4: The recommended engine subprocess needs a frozen-app entry point: `python -m fontplayground.engine.cli` does not exist inside a packaged .app

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/__main__.py:5`, `fontplayground/ui/workers.py:39`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:38`
- **macOS behaviour:** NATIVE-7 and NATIVE-11 plan QProcess running `python -m fontplayground.engine.cli`.
- In a PyInstaller or Briefcase bundle, sys.executable is the app launcher, not a python interpreter.
- On macOS, multiprocessing defaults to 'spawn', which re-executes the main program.

Without an argv dispatch or freeze_support() the helper either fails to start or starts a second full app.
- **Evidence:** - `venv/bin/python -c 'import multiprocessing as m; print(m.get_start_method())'` → spawn.
- fontplayground/__main__.py:5 always imports and runs the Qt UI.
- PyInstaller documents that frozen apps using multiprocessing must call multiprocessing.freeze_support() (https://pyinstaller.org/en/stable/common-issues-and-pitfalls.html).
- **Recommendation:** Make __main__ dispatch before any Qt import:

```python
if '--engine-helper' in sys.argv:
    from fontplayground.engine.cli import main
    sys.exit(main())
```

Then launch [sys.executable, '--engine-helper'] with QProcess, which works both from source and frozen. Alternatively, use a multiprocessing spawn context with freeze_support() in main.

The engine-only process never imports Qt/AppKit, so it shows no Dock icon. Put a packaged-build smoke test in CI.

### NATIVE-M5: pyproject pulls the full PySide6 meta-package (885 MB of unused Addons) although only QtCore/QtGui/QtWidgets are used; the Qt 6.11 wheels require macOS 13+

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `pyproject.toml:15`
- **macOS behaviour:** `dependencies = ['PySide6>=6.8']` installs PySide6-Essentials and PySide6-Addons, 1.2 GB on disk.
- A grep of every import shows only PySide6.QtCore/QtGui/QtWidgets.
- Packagers that collect the whole distribution bloat the .app and add hundreds of extra Mach-O files to sign.
- The wheels are tagged macosx_13_0_universal2 (otool minos 13.0), which fixes the app's minimum macOS.
- **Evidence:** - `du -sh site-packages/PySide6` → 1.2G.
- RECORD sums: essentials 343 MB, addons 885 MB.
- WHEEL Tag: cp310-abi3-macosx_13_0_universal2.
- `otool -l QtCore | grep minos` → 13.0.
- The import grep shows no PySide6 module outside QtCore/QtGui/QtWidgets.
- **Recommendation:** - Depend on 'PySide6-Essentials>=6.8' (pytest-qt works with it).
- Set LSMinimumSystemVersion=13.0 in the Info.plist.
- Exclude unused Qt plugins (keep platforms/cocoa, styles/macstyle, imageformats if needed) in the PyInstaller/Briefcase spec.

### NATIVE-M6: iPadOS (and probably sandboxed App Store builds) cannot install a font generated at runtime: CTFontManager installs only fonts shipped in the bundle or on-demand resources

- **Severity:** minor (auditor: minor); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/install.py:1`, `fontplayground/ui/build.py:164`
- **macOS behaviour:** This undercuts the long-term iPad/App Store rationale in NATIVE-5 and NATIVE-11.
- CTFontManagerErrors.h: kCTFontManagerErrorInvalidFilePath (306) means the file must be in the application's bundle or an on-demand resource.
- WWDC19 session 227: font-provider apps (Fonts capability, user consent sheet) may install only fonts in their bundle or asset catalog; fonts cannot be downloaded or produced at runtime.

A forged font is always produced at runtime, so 'Install' on iPad would have to be a configuration profile with a Fonts payload, or export/share only. Whether macOS sandboxed persistent registration enforces the same rule is untested; it was off-limits here.
- **Evidence:** - SDK CTFontManagerErrors.h lines 78-79: 'The file is not in an allowed location. It must be either in the application's bundle or an on-demand resource.'
- https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2019/227/: fonts 'need to be part of your bundle, or they could be part of an asset catalog'.
- https://support.apple.com/guide/deployment/fonts-payload-settings-depeba084b8/web describes the Fonts payload for configuration profiles.
- **Recommendation:** Do not use iPad install as a reason to rewrite the engine.
- If iPad is ever built, scope Install as 'Export .ttf' plus an optional generated .mobileconfig with a Fonts payload.
- For a Mac App Store build, prototype persistent registration of a container-generated font in a throwaway account before committing; the Developer ID build copying into ~/Library/Fonts remains the dependable path.

### NATIVE-10: Cheap speed-up that needs no rewrite: HarfBuzz subsetting inside the Python helper matches the fontTools closure 57x faster

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:70`, `fontplayground/engine/prepare.py:35`, `fontplayground/engine/merge.py:25`
- **macOS behaviour:** A Latin+CJK build takes 23–28 s on this M-series Mac (README says about 15 s on Windows). Most of it is fontTools: subset 6.3 s, cu2qu 7.1 s, scale 2.6 s, save 2.7 s, finish/final_subset about 5 s.
- **Evidence:** private venv uharfbuzz 0.56.2 (HarfBuzz 14.5.0):
- `face 3: hb-subset 28258 cps keep_locl=False: 0.11s ... glyphs=41005 features=26 outline=CFF`
- versus the engine's subset_font on the same face: `subset (fontTools): 6.29s glyphs=41005`
- SFNS.ttf pin wght=600 + Latin subset: `hb-subset: 0.00s pin=1 ... fvar=False outline=glyf`
- **Recommendation:** Optional, after the helper exists. Behind a feature flag, use uharfbuzz for subset_font and instance_variable in prepare(). This also removes the Segoe-UI-Variable GPOS instancing workaround path.
- Keep fontTools for merge, scale_upem, cu2qu and finish.
- Validate with the existing forge tests plus a glyph-closure diff.
- The same step would later be the hb-subset call in a Swift or Rust engine, so it de-risks Option 3/4.
- Remaining cu2qu time could be cut by caching prepared parts per (face, codepoint set, weight, scale).
- **Verifier (confirmed):** The glyph-name sets are identical (41,005, no differences) and hb-subset is about 52x faster (0.105 s vs 5.46 s, subset only). uharfbuzz adds 8.2 MB. 'Removes the Segoe-UI-Variable GPOS workaround' (prepare.py:143-158) is speculative: nobody tested whether hb instancing accepts a broken VarStore, and that font is Windows-only.
- **Verifier's recommendation (follow this where it differs):** As proposed, behind a flag, but keep the fontTools fallback path, including the GPOS-drop retry. Another cheap win: instance_variable currently instances the whole font before subsetting (prepare.py:143). Subsetting first, or doing both in one hb-subset call, cuts time on large variable CJK fonts.

### NATIVE-M7: Native-only opportunity: session-scope registration lets users try the forged font in other Mac apps without installing it

- **Severity:** polish (auditor: polish); effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/build.py:1`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:1`
- **macOS behaviour:** Today the only choices are Save or Install, and Install is Windows-only. CoreText's kCTFontManagerScopeSession (macOS-only) makes a font available to every app until logout and leaves nothing behind. That gives a 'Try in TextEdit/Pages' button that pairs naturally with honest CoreText checking (NATIVE-M1) and avoids ~/Library/Fonts churn while iterating. Not exercised here: registering beyond process scope was kept off-limits.
- **Evidence:** SDK CTFontManager.h lines 129-142: 'kCTFontManagerScopeSession: The font is available to the current user session, and will not be available in subsequent sessions. Session scope is only available in macOS.' (enum value 3, CT_ENUM_UNAVAILABLE on iOS).
- **Recommendation:** In the Mac installer interface, offer try_in_other_apps(path) = CTFontManagerRegisterFontURLs([url], .session). Use pyobjc in Option 1 or Swift in Option 2.
- Unregister on the next build or on quit.
- Keep persistent install as copying into ~/Library/Fonts.
- Test on a real session before shipping.

#### Data points

- Source layers (lines): engine 812, catalog 354, paths 39 => 1,205 Qt-free engine+catalog; Qt-free UI logic (languages 196, smart 193, mix 43, textutil 23, requests 12) 467; Windows install.py 227; Qt-coupled logic (model 868, build 353, fonts 113, workers 77) 1,411; Qt views 3,418 (picker 757, recipe 676, app 415, advanced 333, action_bar 323, preview 322, preview_pane 263, theme 232, widgets 97). Qt-free 1,911 vs Qt-importing 4,829 of 6,740 total.
- Tests: 4,802 lines; 85 pure test functions (954 lines) vs 213 Qt test functions (3,616 lines); baseline 339 passed / 3 skipped / 2 failed.
- Effort (person-weeks): Option 1 Qt-native-ish 3.5-5; seam refactor (Qt-free model + engine CLI) 1-2; Option 2 hybrid Swift shell 10-14 (after seam); Option 3 full Swift rewrite 22-33 (engine 14-22 + app 8-11); Option 4 Rust core + Swift shell 17-25 (engine 8-12 + bindings 1 + shell 7-10).
- Code reuse at runtime: Option 1 ~95% of source (all 6,740 lines minus platform shims), 100% of tests; Option 2 ~18% (1,205 engine/catalog lines + helper) plus 85 pure tests, while ~1,750 lines of model logic are ported using their tests as the spec; Option 3 0% (tests/fixtures only as golden spec); Option 4 0% (the helper protocol is reused).
- Forge timings on this Mac (M-series, macOS 27): Georgia + PingFang HK 23.1 s (33,793 chars, 45,771 glyphs); Georgia + Hiragino Sans GB 27.7 s real, 703.8 MB max RSS (30,104 chars, 30,179 glyphs).
- PingFang SC prepare breakdown (fontTools): subset 6.29 s, cu2qu 7.13 s, scale+strip 2.62 s, save 2.73 s; longest gap between progress events (cancel latency) 17.7 s.
- hb-subset (HarfBuzz 14.5.0) on the same PingFang SC input: 0.11 s with identical 41,005-glyph closure (57x faster); SFNS wght=600 instancing + Latin subset <0.01 s.
- CGPath stroke+union synthetic bold: 0.3 ms/glyph, output 60 cubic / 0 quadratic segments (needs cu2qu for glyf).
- Startup: engine import 0.05-0.06 s warm (no Qt); PySide6.QtWidgets + ui.app import 0.13 s warm; Swift prototype compile 1.8 s.
- Bundle inputs: needed Qt pieces 88 MB universal2 (full PySide6 install 1.2 GB, 243 Mach-O files); python-build-standalone 3.12.14 71 MB on disk (libpython 17 MB, stdlib 27 MB, 10 Mach-O files, ad-hoc signed); fontTools 17 MB (6 .so); skia-pathops 7.1 MB; pyobjc core+CoreText+Foundation ~11.5 MB.
- Estimated app size: Option 1 ~150 MB universal / ~85-95 MB arm64-thin; Option 2 ~60 MB; Option 3 ~10-15 MB.
- Catalog: CoreText 411 unique font files vs repo scanner 379 (32 missed, 29 in AssetsV2 incl. PingFang); CoreText enumeration 0.01 s; scanner with AssetsV2 888 faces, cold 4.9 s, warm 0.44 s, cache 10.1 MB; scanner families 422 vs CoreText 221 (74 '.'-hidden); Apple downloadable catalog 1,170 descriptors / 332 families (query took 126 s).
- AAT: 189 of 921 installed faces have morx without GSUB (74 more have both); forged Georgia+Baghdad loses Arabic joining (CoreText check: contextual forms used False vs True in source); both fontTools subset and hb-subset drop morx.
- Toolchain present: Apple Swift 6.4 / Xcode 27, codesign, notarytool; no rustc/cargo.

## Completeness critic (`CRIT-*`)

The largest gap the six audits missed is the app's look. The Python binary that runs the app, and the PyInstaller bootloader that would run the packaged .app, are both linked against the macOS 15 SDK. macOS 26 and later therefore draws the whole app in its pre-26 compatibility look. I proved this by rendering Qt's native 'macos' style twice, once with an SDK-15 python and once with an SDK-27 copy: the controls and window colours come out different. Every screenshot the UI audit took used that legacy look. The second gap is moving over from Windows. A Windows forge_last.json restored on the Mac silently drops fonts that exist on both systems (Georgia), and it fills the Save panel with a relative file name full of backslashes. The Windows fonts the user's recipes were built on (YaHei, SimSun, DengXian, Meiryo, Malgun, Calibri) exist on this Mac only inside the Office app bundles: 3 identical copies of 280 files, not usable by other apps, under a licence limited to the Microsoft product. The remaining gaps are smaller. The app has no Open With / drop-on-Dock support. The scanner handles macOS disks badly: AppleDouble `._` files are counted as unreadable fonts, fonts in `.Trashes` are listed, and symlinked subfolders are skipped. The catalog cache never deletes old entries, so it grows by 1.9 MB with each PingFang asset update. The tests use only made-up fonts, so none of the real-Mac-font failures can show up. The minimum window width prevents window tiling. There is no handling of fonts disabled in Font Book, and no localisation setup. I tested several things and none of them needs a change: quarantined fonts, Rosetta, App Nap for a windowless process, main-thread responsiveness during a 25 s CJK build, and a rebuilt font's preview winning over an installed copy with the same name.

### CRIT-1: The app draws in AppKit's pre-macOS-26 compatibility look because its main executable (uv CPython and the PyInstaller bootloader) is linked against the macOS 15 SDK

- **Severity:** major; effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405`, `pyproject.toml`
- **macOS behaviour:** AppKit decides whether an app gets the macOS 26+ design from the SDK recorded in the MAIN executable. Qt's 'macos' style draws QPushButton and QComboBox through AppKit cells, so the whole app follows that executable. Run from the venv, or as the PyInstaller .app, the app gets the legacy bezel buttons, the blue chevron box on pop-ups, and the old window grey. It does not match the other apps on this macOS 27 machine. Every UI-audit screenshot was taken in this mode, so its 'looks fairly native' verdict describes the compatibility look.
- **Evidence:** otool -l on the shared venv python (~/.local/share/uv/python/cpython-3.12.14-macos-aarch64-none/bin/python3.12) gives minos 11.0, sdk 15.0. The PySide6 QtGui and libqcocoa give minos 13.0, sdk 15.0. The tooling auditor's PyInstaller bootloader ('Font Playground.app/Contents/MacOS/Font Playground') gives minos 11.0, sdk 15.0. The local Xcode SDK is 27.0.
Experiment 1 (work/critic/h_sdk/render.swift): the same NSButton and NSPopUpButton rendered by a binary linked on SDK 27 and by one recorded as SDK 15 (-platform_version macos 11.0 15.0). The images differ: a flat capsule vs a white bezel with shadow, and a plain chevron vs a blue chevron box. NSButton fitting size is 64x24 (27) vs 74x32 (15).
Experiment 2 (qtrender.py, cocoa QPA, no Dock icon via QT_MAC_DISABLE_FOREGROUND_APPLICATION_TRANSFORM): a copy of the venv python re-stamped with `vtool -set-build-version macos 11.0 27.0` and re-signed ad hoc, compared with the SDK-15 copy. Same Qt widgets, system in Dark mode. SDK 15: window background #323232, button face #656565, combo arrow #1664da (blue box). SDK 27: window #1e1e1e, button #303030, no blue box. Qt sizeHints are identical in both (button 70x32, combo 98x32).
Docs: https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/information-property-list/uidesignrequirescompatibility ; https://github.com/warpdotdev/warp/issues/16149 (an app on an older SDK gets pre-Liquid-Gl …
- **Recommendation:** Treat the executable's SDK as a release requirement. (1) Build the PyInstaller bootloader from source with Xcode 26 or later (PYINSTALLER_COMPILE_BOOTLOADER=1 pip install --no-binary pyinstaller pyinstaller, or `cd bootloader && python ./waf all`), or ship a small Swift/ObjC launcher built with the current Xcode that embeds Python. (2) Add a release/CI check: `otool -l 'Font Playground.app/Contents/MacOS/Font Playground' | grep sdk` must be ≥ 26. (3) For visual QA in development, run from a vtool-restamped, ad-hoc-signed copy of python. Use it for development only; never ship a restamped binary. (4) Once the app has the new look, check again: Qt 6.11 does not change its control metrics (the sizeHints stay the same), and the stylesheet colours tuned against the old #323232/#ececec greys (UI-5, UI-8) need to be retuned. Do not set UIDesignRequiresCompatibility: it is ignored when you build with the 27 SDK.

### CRIT-2: Settings written on Windows do not migrate: the restored recipe silently loses fonts that exist on both systems, the Save panel is pre-filled with a relative backslash path, and extra folders turn into relative junk

- **Severity:** minor; effort M; files: `fontplayground/engine/spec.py:84`, `fontplayground/ui/model.py:686`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:99`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:102`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:299`, `fontplayground/ui/action_bar.py:306`
- **macOS behaviour:** Suppose the user copies %LOCALAPPDATA%\FontPlayground to ~/Library/Application Support/FontPlayground. from_dict looks up materials by the exact (path, index), so 'C:\Windows\Fonts\georgia.ttf' does not match /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Georgia.ttf. Every material is dropped without a message, and the family name falls back to 'Forged'. After a Save on Windows, output_path is marked user-edited. On the Mac it is restored as a single relative file name containing backslashes and pre-fills the Save panel. extra_dirs such as 'D:/Fonts' become relative paths under the current folder and are skipped silently. There is no UI to remove them.
- **Evidence:** work/critic/b_migrate/m.py (offscreen). The catalog holds Georgia, Arial and Songti from this Mac, and the forge_last.json is the Windows-shaped file the app itself writes (materials georgia.ttf + simsun.ttc, names_edited.output=True). Output:
  rows after restore: []
  Georgia on this Mac: [('/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Georgia.ttf', 0)]
  output: 'C:\\Users\\kcice\\Documents\\Georgia SimSun-Regular.ttf' | Path.is_absolute: False | parent: '.'
  family: Forged
  extra_dir D:/Fonts -> is_absolute False is_dir False resolves to <cwd>/D:/Fonts
- **Recommendation:** Tell the user not to copy the Windows config folder: catalog.json and settings.json are worthless on a Mac, and only the last recipe has value. In code: (a) store a portable identity next to the path, {path, index, postscript_name, family, style, glyph_count or cmap hash}. On restore, fall back to that identity when the path is missing, and report 'N fonts could not be found: …' instead of dropping them silently. This also fixes CATALOG-7 and ENGINE-8. (b) Validate on load: reset output_path and drop extra_dirs that are not absolute on this OS or that contain a drive letter or backslashes. (c) Add Export/Import Recipe… with a small JSON document type (e.g. .fontrecipe). It gives the Mac a native document for Open Recent, and the same recipe works on both Windows and macOS.

### CRIT-3: The Windows fonts behind the user's existing recipes (YaHei, SimSun, DengXian, Meiryo, Malgun, Calibri, Consolas…) exist on this Mac only inside Office's app bundles: private, three copies, and licensed only for use with the Microsoft product

- **Severity:** minor; effort S; files: `fontplayground/paths.py:12`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:25`
- **macOS behaviour:** Word.app, Excel.app and PowerPoint.app each carry Contents/Resources/DFonts: 280 identical files, 343 MB per copy. They include msyh.ttc, Simsun.ttc, Deng*.ttf, meiryo.ttc, malgun.ttf, MSJH.ttf, msgothic.ttc, batang.ttc, Calibri and Consola. Segoe UI is not there. Office activates these fonts for its own processes only, so CoreText (and every other app) does not list them. The scanner does not see them either. By default the macOS folder panel treats .app bundles as files, so 'Add folder…' can reach DFonts only through Go to Folder (⇧⌘G). If the user adds it anyway, the scanner reads the fonts as supported and the forge accepts them. An Office-licensed font would then be merged into a font marked freely installable (fsType 0, ENGINE-5). A future scan of /Applications would list every font three times.
- **Evidence:** ls DFonts | wc -l → 280 (same listing in Excel.app; `cmp` shows msyh.ttc is identical in PowerPoint.app). du -sh → 343M.
CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames: Microsoft YaHei/SimSun/DengXian/Calibri/Segoe UI/Meiryo/Malgun Gothic/Consolas → False; Arial/Georgia/Times New Roman/Verdana/Tahoma/YuGothic/PingFang SC → True.
read_faces(DFonts/msyh.ttc): 2 faces 'Microsoft YaHei', supported True, 29,898 cps. OS/2 fsType 8. Name ID 13: 'Microsoft supplied font. You may use this font … as permitted by the license terms, or terms of use, of the Microsoft product'.
- **Recommendation:** (1) Ship a short Windows-to-macOS equivalence table, used both in the README's migration notes and when a restored recipe names a missing font. For example: Segoe UI→SF Pro/Helvetica Neue, Microsoft YaHei→PingFang SC, Microsoft JhengHei→PingFang TC, SimSun→Songti SC, SimHei→Heiti SC/STHeiti, KaiTi→Kaiti SC, Meiryo/Yu Gothic→Hiragino Sans/YuGothic, MS Mincho→Hiragino Mincho ProN, Malgun Gothic→Apple SD Gothic Neo, Consolas→SF Mono/Menlo. (2) The scanner should never descend into .app/.bundle/.framework packages unless the user picked a folder inside one. (3) When a chosen folder is inside an app bundle, or a face's licence/vendor record names Microsoft and Office, show a one-line licence note before building. (4) Dedupe identical files by (size, SHA-1) so the three Office copies appear once. (5) Tell the user: copying C:\Windows\Fonts to the Mac is also outside the Windows font licence.

### CRIT-4: No macOS file-open integration: no QFileOpenEvent handling, no CFBundleDocumentTypes, no way to open a single font file

- **Severity:** minor; effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:339`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405`
- **macOS behaviour:** The only way to use a font that is not installed is 'Add folder…', which then rescans that whole folder on every launch. To try a .ttf from ~/Downloads, the user has to add all of Downloads, which also triggers the Downloads privacy prompt (TOOLING-M3). 'Open With › Font Playground', dropping a font on the Dock icon or on the window, and double-clicking a recipe are not possible. A bundled app gets file opens as Apple Events. Qt turns them into QEvent.FileOpen on the QApplication, and nothing handles that event.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn 'FileOpen|setAcceptDrops|dropEvent' fontplayground → no handlers. The only drop-related code is preview.py:211 (plain-text paste/drop into the sample).
UTIs on this Mac via NSURLTypeIdentifierKey: Georgia.ttf → public.truetype-ttf-font; Songti.ttc → public.truetype-collection-font; LastResort.otf → public.opentype-font; GenevaCY.dfont → com.apple.truetype-datafork-suitcase-font. NSWorkspace default opener for all four: /System/Applications/Font Book.app.
The tooling prototype's Info.plist has no CFBundleDocumentTypes.
Docs: https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/qfileopenevent.html
- **Recommendation:** Add CFBundleDocumentTypes with LSItemContentTypes [public.truetype-ttf-font, public.opentype-font, public.truetype-collection-font] (plus the recipe type from CRIT-2), CFBundleTypeRole Viewer and LSHandlerRank Alternate, so the app appears in Open With without taking over from Font Book. Subclass QApplication, or install an event filter on it, to catch QEvent.FileOpen. Queue the paths until the first scan has finished, then add the file's faces as ad-hoc catalog entries. Remember each file by path (a security-scoped bookmark if the app is ever sandboxed), not by its folder. Add File › Open Font… (⌘O) and accept font drops on the window. Optional: a Share button (NSSharingServicePicker) for the result. PyInstaller: pass info_plist= in BUNDLE().

### CRIT-5: The scanner's folder walk ignores macOS file-system conventions: AppleDouble '._*.ttf' and '__MACOSX' count as unreadable fonts, fonts in '.Trashes' are listed, and symlinked subfolders are skipped

- **Severity:** minor; effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:25`, `fontplayground/paths.py:38`
- **macOS behaviour:** When macOS copies a file that carries extended attributes (quarantine, provenance, WhereFroms) to an exFAT, FAT or SMB volume, it writes a 4 KB '._name.ttf' AppleDouble sidecar. Zips made on a Mac carry '__MACOSX/._name.ttf'. The scanner accepts any path whose suffix is .ttf, so every sidecar shows up in '· N files couldn't be read'. A 100-font USB stick gives 100 errors. Fonts deleted on an external disk (in .Trashes/<uid>) or in ~/.Trash (if the user adds ~) are offered as normal fonts. Python 3.12's rglob does not follow directory symlinks, so a symlinked subfolder is skipped silently. Finder aliases are ignored as well.
- **Evidence:** work/critic/a_appledouble. A 20 MB scratch exFAT image (hdiutil … -nobrowse -mountpoint) with Georgia.ttf (quarantine xattr) and more/Arial.ttf copied in. macOS created ._Georgia.ttf, ._more and more/._Arial.ttf. scan():
  mnt faces: 2 failed: [('/._Georgia.ttf', 'TTLibError: Not a TrueType or OpenType font (bad sfntVersion)'), ('/more/._Arial.ttf', 'TTLibError: …bad sfntVersion')]
  unz (ditto --sequesterRsrc zip, then unzip): failed: [('/__MACOSX/Fonts/._Georgia.ttf', …bad sfntVersion)]
  perm/.Trashes/501/deleted.ttf → listed: ['/.Trashes/501/deleted.ttf', '/Georgia.ttf']
  sym/root with linked -> ../elsewhere (containing sub/Georgia.ttf) plus a symlinked file → found only ['root/filelink.ttf']
  A chmod-000 subfolder is skipped without error (fine).
- **Recommendation:** Replace rglob with os.walk(followlinks=True), pruning in place: names starting with '._'; dirs '__MACOSX', '.Trashes', '.Trash', '.fseventsd', '.Spotlight-V100', '.TemporaryItems', '.DocumentRevisions-V100'; and packages (*.app, *.bundle, *.framework, *.photoslibrary) unless the root itself is one. Keep a visited set of (st_dev, st_ino) to break symlink loops. Optionally resolve Finder aliases with NSURL.URLByResolvingAliasFileAtURL. Do not recurse into ~/Library/Containers or Group Containers: since macOS 14 that triggers the 'access data from other apps' prompt (not tested here). Count AppleDouble files as skipped, not as failed.

### CRIT-6: The catalog cache never prunes: entries for vanished files, including every old AssetsV2 hash path, are kept and reloaded forever; Rescan does not clean them

- **Severity:** minor; effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/cache.py:61`, `fontplayground/catalog/cache.py:82`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:45`, `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:56`
- **macOS behaviour:** scan() only calls put() for files it finds and then saves the whole dict, so old keys stay forever. On macOS, MobileAsset font updates move PingFang and the other downloaded fonts into a new <hash>.asset folder (CATALOG-7). Each update leaves about 1.9 MB of dead PingFang JSON that is parsed at every launch and rewritten after every scan. Windows font paths hardly ever change, so the problem did not show up there.
- **Evidence:** work/critic/g_cache: the real PingFang.ttc was copied to assets/aaaa.asset/…, scanned, then moved to assets/bbbb.asset/… and scanned with use_cache=False (what 'Rescan fonts' does).
  entries after asset update + rescan: ['assets/aaaa.asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc', 'assets/bbbb.asset/AssetData/PingFang.ttc'] | size before 1929148 after 3858270
- **Recommendation:** In scan(), after a scan that finished without interruption, keep only the entries for files it visited (build a fresh dict, or call cache.retain(seen_paths)). While doing this, move the cache to ~/Library/Caches/<bundle id> (CATALOG-M3) and write it atomically (TOOLING-M5). With the CoreText enumerator from CATALOG-1, you can key cache entries by file path + inode + size + mtime.

### CRIT-7: The test suite uses only synthetic fonts, so none of the macOS font-data failures the audits found can show up in it

- **Severity:** minor; effort M; files: `tests/fixtures.py:1`, `tests/conftest.py:7`
- **macOS behaviour:** All 339 tests pass on this Mac. Meanwhile the real Mac fonts break: Helvetica Neue + PingFang fails at verify (ENGINE-1), AAT fonts forge broken (ENGINE-2), Courier/AppleGothic without OS/2 crash (ENGINE-M1), Geneva/Cochin bloc/bdat crash (ENGINE-M2), .LastResort gets preselected (CATALOG-2), Apple Arabic fonts get their names wrong (CATALOG-3), and NISC18030 is unreadable (CATALOG-8). The fixtures only build rectangle glyphs with a Windows-platform cmap and no Apple tables, so none of these paths is ever exercised. The Windows install tests are skipped, and nothing takes their place.
- **Evidence:** tests/fixtures.py:1 "Build tiny fonts for tests. No external font files needed."
`grep -rn 'System/Library|/Library/Fonts|morx|bloc|bdat' tests` → no real-font references (matches are only 'blocker'/'block' words).
The fixture cmap comes from FontBuilder.setupCharacterMap (Windows platform only). Baseline: 339 passed, 3 skipped, 2 failed (test_paths only).
- **Recommendation:** Add an opt-in real-font tier (`@pytest.mark.system_fonts`, skipped when a font is missing). It should find fonts through CoreText by PostScript name instead of hard-coded paths, and assert: forge(Helvetica Neue + PingFang SC) keeps its cmap; forging an AAT-only font raises or warns; Courier/AppleGothic (no OS/2) and Geneva (bloc) are rejected cleanly or fixed; no '.'-prefixed family appears in the catalog; PingFang is found. Run it nightly on a macOS 26 GitHub runner with per-test timeouts, and a matching tier on windows-latest for Segoe UI/YaHei. Keep the synthetic suite for PRs. Add fixture variants for a Unicode-platform (0,3) cmap, a font without OS/2, and a font with a morx table, so the most important Apple cases run in normal CI too.

### CRIT-8: A minimum width of 1000 pt plus a fixed 420-pt left column blocks macOS window tiling and Split View on this 1352-pt display

- **Severity:** polish; effort M; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:35`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:123`, `fontplayground/ui/app.py:413`
- **macOS behaviour:** Tiling to half the screen (Fn-Ctrl-arrows, dragging to an edge) and Split View give each window about half the width. This MacBook Pro's display is 1352×878 pt (visible 1352×761), so half is 676 pt. The window's minimum is 1000×640 and the left column is fixed at 420 pt, so the app cannot sit next to Finder or Font Book. That side-by-side arrangement is exactly what a font tool wants.
- **Evidence:** NSScreen via PyObjC: 'Built-in Retina Display frame 1352.0 x 878.0 visible 1352.0 x 761.0 scale 2.0' (Mac17,9, M5 Pro). app.py:413 window.setMinimumSize(1000, 640); app.py:35 LEFT_WIDTH = 420; app.py:123 setFixedWidth(LEFT_WIDTH). How macOS actually tiles with this minimum was not observed, because that would need a visible window.
- **Recommendation:** Lower the minimum width to about 640 pt. Make the left column a collapsible QSplitter pane (or a sidebar toggled with ⌃⌘S, matching the macOS sidebar convention) with a smaller minimum. Let the action bar wrap or elide below about 800 pt. Save the splitter state together with the window geometry (UI-4).

### CRIT-9: Fonts disabled in Font Book are not recognised: the file scan lists them like any other font, and the proposed CoreText enumerator would hide them without saying so

- **Severity:** polish; effort S; files: `fontplayground/catalog/scanner.py:19`, `fontplayground/ui/fonts.py:87`
- **macOS behaviour:** Disabling a font in Font Book leaves the file where it is. CoreText then refuses to create the font for any app. The file scanner still lists it. system_font() (lookup by name) finds nothing, so the preview loads the file directly, and the user can forge and install a font that the rest of the Mac hides on purpose. The name-clash check does not know about disabled names either. If CATALOG-1's CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontURLs approach is adopted, disabled fonts disappear with no hint instead.
- **Evidence:** CTFontDescriptor.h:278 kCTFontEnabledAttribute. CTFontCollection.h:82-84 kCTFontCollectionIncludeDisabledFontsOption: disabled descriptors cannot be used to 'create a CTFontRef'. On this Mac, CTFontCollectionCreateFromAvailableFonts with/without the option: 655/655 descriptors, 0 disabled. I could not reproduce the case without changing the user's Font Book state, which is not allowed.
- **Recommendation:** When enumerating through CoreText, pass kCTFontCollectionIncludeDisabledFontsOption and read kCTFontEnabledAttribute. Tag those faces 'Disabled in Font Book'. Hide them by default behind a 'Show disabled fonts' toggle, or show them dimmed, and include their names in the Install conflict check.

### CRIT-10: No localisation setup: Qt's own strings and (in a bundle without .lproj) AppKit-provided UI stay in English on a non-English Mac

- **Severity:** polish; effort S; files: `fontplayground/ui/app.py:405`
- **macOS behaviour:** There is no QTranslator and no tr() wrapping. The Qt-drawn parts that other Mac apps show in the user's language stay in English: text-field and sample-editor context menus, QMessageBox Yes/No, and the Qt-created application menu items. The tooling auditor's .app has no .lproj folders and no CFBundleLocalizations or CFBundleAllowMixedLocalizations. By Apple's localisation rules, system panels shown inside the app then use the development language; this part was not tested. This Mac is set to en-JP, so there is no visible impact today.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn 'QTranslator|QLocale|tr(' fontplayground → nothing relevant. `defaults read -g AppleLanguages` → ("en-JP"). PySide6/Qt/translations includes qtbase_ja.qm, qtbase_zh_CN.qm and qtbase_zh_TW.qm. find '<tooling .app>' -name '*.lproj' → none; its Info.plist (PlistBuddy) has only CFBundle* identity keys and NSHighResolutionCapable.
- **Recommendation:** Cheap step now: load qtbase_<QLocale.system().name()>.qm from QLibraryInfo.path(QLibraryInfo.LibraryPath.TranslationsPath) at startup, and set CFBundleDevelopmentRegion=en and CFBundleAllowMixedLocalizations=YES (or add en.lproj/ja.lproj/zh-Hans.lproj). Later: wrap the user-facing constants (action_bar.py, model.py STAGE_TEXT, app.py) so they can be translated. Given the app's CJK focus, zh-Hans and ja are the obvious first languages.


## Findings from spec writing (`N-*`)

### N-1: Merged cmap format 4 subtable overflows 65,535 bytes

- **Severity:** major; effort S; files: `engine/prepare.py`, `engine/merge.py` (reference)
- **Behaviour:** a merged font with large, fragmented BMP coverage needs a format 4 subtable over 65,535 bytes. The forge fails at `finish` with `struct.error: 'H' format requires 0 <= number <= 65535`. Example: Avenir Next + Hiragino Sans W3 (Han, Kana, CJK symbols) + Apple SD Gothic Neo (Hangul).
- **Evidence:** reproduced on the unmodified reference engine with that Mac combination, and on Linux with a synthetic 8,500-code-point fixture. The prototype fix keeps 8,188 code points in format 4, with full coverage in format 12.
- **Recommendation:** in WP-101's cmap normalisation, write a truncated format 4 (the BMP code points that fit) plus a complete (3,10) format 12, and report the issue `cmap_format4_partial`. CoreText, DirectWrite and HarfBuzz read format 12. Closed by WP-101.
